▶▶ 00-STARTHERE — 2026-08-24. READ THIS FIRST. STARTHERE-2026-08-24

Where it stands. The AI route explorer runs from a trailhead the rider SELECTS on the map. The hard-coded Panguitch anchor is gone. Verified end to end on Droid 2 — three routes built and promoted to saved routes. Commits 09c5f5b27 and 70e122d1a on feature/convoy-event-ride.

Flow: AI Design panel → PROCEED TO MAP → guided checklist over the live map → tap a trailhead → return question → prose summary → route build with streamed progress → results → CONTINUE → Work in Progress list.

⭐ THE DESIGN REVERSAL — trailheads are SELECTED, not captured

Patch C intercepted long press to create a trailhead directly and it NEVER FIRED — the effect sat in a block above // -- WebView -- that is not live while the map is in use. The indentation drop from 20 spaces to 8 was the tell, visible before the patch was written.

The fix was not to move the effect. Fred, 08-24: "why not make this two steps, create the trailhead then select it?" Long press had ONE meaning for weeks; C gave it a second one depending on hidden state. Creating and selecting are two acts, and making them two acts in the UI removed the interception entirely.

⛔ WHY THE TAP DID NOT REACH KOTLIN — three wrong theories, recorded

  • "The marker consumes the click." NO. There is no stopPropagation on that path — the tap falls through to map.on('click') and places a route vertex. Deliberate: ROUTEFALLTHROUGH-2026-07-30.
  • "Popups bound after setRouteMode(true) escape suppression." NO. POPUPBIND-2026-08-13H stashes instead of binding and re-checks on every rebuild. Fred said this before the read confirmed it.
  • "The corridor graph grew sevenfold." NO. The nav_test DB copy holds 216,616 trails across several states; Droid 2 holds Utah alone. Same query, different data.

Actual cause: ConvoyRouteToolbar:194, the AI chip calling onAddModeChanged(false) — the ROUTEMODE -> false logged 1.3s after +ROUTE armed it. Its comment was correct when written; what changed is that AI mode now OPENS with a step needing taps to reach Kotlin. Fixed by putting the choice back in the rider's hands — the BUILD row is visible in every mode again.

⛔⛔ THE ORPHAN BUG — 303,585 rows

The trails clear removed 86,969 trails and 303,585 trail_properties rows. Fred called it: "the Utah identifiers are in the trails extension db which we did not clear."

⚠ geom_hash is the IDENTITY; trail_id is the KEY. trail_properties joins on trail_id, a UUID minted at insert. Delete the trails and those rows join to nothing. And the damage was active: TrailImporter:461 inserts with INSERT OR IGNORE on (source_id, source_unique_id), so a stale row KEPT THE SLOT and the re-imported trail got no properties, with nothing reporting an error. Ledger: inserted=30 before, inserted=86923 after.

⚠⚠ METHOD — seven guard failures, one root cause

Every failed patch guard today had the expected NUMBER written from what the edit was MEANT to do rather than derived from what it does. TARGETED RIDE DURATION also in a comment; "trailhead" already in WAYPOINT_TYPES; ROUTEFALLTHROUGH in three handlers; weight(1f)) { three others already present; braces asserted balanced on a file that was 952/951 before the patch.

⭐⭐ STANDING FIX, adopted from patch E onward: DERIVE the expected count. Count the token in the OLD blocks and the NEW blocks, compute before − removed + added. It caught a correct edit in patch I where a comment mentioned .width(248.dp) while replacing it. Never write an expected count by hand.

And the bracket invariant is "UNCHANGED", not "balanced" — asserting a property the file never had cost a cycle.

⭐ MEASURED — rider points, the base cost dominates

3 points → 93.5 mi. 5 → 97.3. 7 → 110.6. 10 → 139.0. Three points already blow an 80-mile ceiling, and seven more cost only 45 miles. Getting into the region costs ~60; the points are nearly free once there. Two points two miles apart cost 32.9 network miles. A rider cannot predict this from a map — hence live feedback while pins are dropped.

Dead-end spurs: 1,388 on the main component, only 38 carry a feature (2.7%). A "short AND featureless" cut at 300 yd removes 606 and loses none worth riding. Length alone is the wrong test.

⭐ OSM ACCESS TAGS DO NOT EXIST IN WHAT WE IMPORT

PRAGMA on both .gpkg extracts: ten columns on the roads layer, no access field of any kind. Geofabrik's GeoPackage is shapefile-derived and flattens OSM's tag model — ohv, motor_vehicle, access, 4wd_only all dropped at export. fclass answers by SHAPE, not by permission.

⭐ THE PBF INSIGHT. Fred: "isn't the route id the same as the protobuf file? Can't we just mine the attributes?" YES — source_unique_id already holds osm_id, so the PBF is read for id and tags only and geometry keeps coming from the GeoPackage. That removes node resolution entirely. Mapping is ONE PREPROCESS at import, carried through C3 in the same insert — no update pass, no drift. See GroupTrack_design_trail_classification_2026-08-24.html.

🔴 TOMORROW

  1. Complete the ten-point rider-assisted routing, including dead-end spur removal (drawn on the map, skipped by the router, filtered in the graph build not the importer) — GroupTrack_design_rider_assisted_routing_2026-08-24.html
  2. Turn the map key legend into a filter, persisted, affecting both maps, current state shown in the AI flow

⚠ CARRIED — the two that block a release

  • The trail-maintenance panel is NOT built. The clear runs silently. Fine on Droid 2, not fine on forty phones. QuickStart v8 covers it in writing; the app does not.
  • The filter change did not take. 87,067 trails before the patch and after. Two commands settle it: ls /sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/config/ and logcat | grep OsmLayers. Matters beyond this filter — an override that silently wins means ANY shipped config change can be ignored.

Design docs added 08-24: GroupTrack_design_rider_assisted_routing_2026-08-24.html (task 1) and GroupTrack_design_trail_classification_2026-08-24.html (the PBF decision). Prototype: nav_pass6b_sweep_2026-08-24.py.

⭐⭐ THE SHAPE THAT MADE IT SMALL (Fred, 08-21)

"The bbox is the decider; where it comes from is the driver." State and area share one pipeline — the state run's bbox is the state's corners, the area run's is the drawn box. Catalog resolution, the manifest builder, the execution loop and the recap counters are all shared and unmodified.

"The existing progress and completion IS the import process. Everything else is serving it. Building the JSON, no work." Selection UI decides a bbox and writes a manifest; it does no work. That is why the multi-state loop is not a new loop — the builder emits one geofabrik_area row per state and the existing source loop simply sees N pending rows.

⭐ Consequence worth keeping: granular restart recovery is now latent. The whole plan — every source, and the bbox that defines the work — is written down before any of it runs. A drawn box is ephemeral; area.bbox survives the app dying. Nothing has to be reconstructed.

✅ DEVICE RUN — Droid 2, four-state box, 41 minutes

Box S=36.180 W=-114.573 N=37.022 E=-113.167 (Lake Mead corner, 58 × 78 mi). Resolved Arizona, California, Nevada, Utah → 6 catalog rows + 4 geofabrik_area rows.

  • ✅ Manifest correct: type: "bbox", four states, California's nested slug california/norcal intact, catalog first / Geofabrik last.
  • ✅ The "area" scope reached the OSM worker — Arizona imported 3,335 against 134,242 whole-state. This was the assumption I was least sure of in the build.
  • ✅ The loop advances — state two (California) downloaded, extracted, imported and cleaned up after state one finished.
  • ✅ Arizona State Parks 10,935 adds · BLM 617 adds — both genuinely new to this device.

🔴🔴 PARKED FINDINGS — start here next session

1. THREE SOURCES FAIL EVERY RECORD, AND THE COUNTERS ARE THE ONLY REASON WE KNOW.

  • usgs_national_trails — 391/391 errors (NH), 718/718 errors (SW). Fails everywhere, unexplained. Nothing on this device for it to collide with.
  • ugrc_utah_trails — 117/117 errors. Fred's read: the box clipped southern Utah and all 117 were already present from the full-state import → likely a collision misclassified as an error. ⚠ Note it was the FIRST source in the run, so nothing in that run had written yet — the DB's prior contents are the only possible collision source.
  • nps_public_trails — 6/6 errors in NH but 2/2 SUCCESS in the SW. Same source, same code, same day. Data-dependent, not a broken source.

⭐ All three previously reported imported: 0 and read as "no coverage for this state." That reading was wrong. Evidence saved: /d/grouptrack_screenshots/trailimporter_2026-08-21.txt. Next step is one command — the per-record error text: grep -vE "Done |^--------" <that file>.

⚠ If collisions land in errors, the recap's most important line cries wolf on every re-import. That is the line built to expose real failures.

2. GEOFABRIK 16-RECORD GAP. NH re-import: 42,316 processed vs 42,300 in the parts (42,295 dupes + 5 adds + 0 errors). Likely geometryChanged — a real bucket that is not in the five. Decide whether it folds into dupes or earns a line.

3. MANIFEST OVERWRITE. Filename is import_[date]_[area].json, so a same-day same-state re-run replaces the earlier record (observed: 08:24 → 08:26). manifest_id distinguishes them inside the file; the filename does not. Matters once history exists.

4. COMPLETION TWISTY DOES NOT RENDER. MANIFESTUI-2026-08-21 compiled (marker count 2) and the counters are in the JSON, but the per-source twisty does not open on device. Presentation only — the values are where the sweep and a support call need them.

⚠ BBOX STATE SELECTION IS OVER-INCLUSIVE — decided, not built

The defect, measured: findByBbox tests the drawn box against each state's RECTANGLE. California's reaches east to -114.13; the box's west edge was -114.573, so California was selected, downloaded (600 MB+), extracted, imported ZERO trails, and swept. A rectangle tested against a rectangle can only ever be a rectangle.

⭐⭐ DECISION (Fred 08-21): the deselect checkbox is the fix, on the bbox confirmation screen that the 08-18 mockup already specifies. Cheap, no network dependency, no tuning parameter, no failure modes — and honest: the rider knows the ground, the app does not.

Panel must EXPLAIN the mechanism, not just offer the control (Fred). Draft wording: "Some states may not have trails in your area. States are matched using a rectangle around each state, and those rectangles overlap. A state can be listed here without reaching into the area you drew. If you know a state isn't part of your ride, uncheck it — you'll skip its download." ⚠ Keep "bounding box" out of rider-facing text; audience is riders 65–75.

  • Default ALL CHECKED. Over-inclusion costs time; under-inclusion costs coverage the rider does not discover until they are out there.
  • Show the download size per state if available — that is what makes the choice real.

💡 Considered and NOT chosen: perimeter geocoding. Sample the box edge every ~10 mi, reverse-geocode each point, deselect any bbox-selected state whose name never appears. Fred's insight: a state cannot be inside the box without crossing its edge, so perimeter sampling is complete for a rectangle and interior points add nothing. Measured on the real box: 28 sample points, 11 of them west of California's bbox edge. Written and parked as resolveStatesByPerimeter_2026-08-21.kt. More correct in principle; costs a network round trip, a tuning parameter and three failure modes to buy what a checkbox does for free. ⚠ Structure if ever revived: bbox proposes, perimeter DESELECTS — never let the geocoder add, and deselect nothing when lookups fail.

  1. Convoy submenu "Work with Tracks" — ConvoyTrackExportSheet + ConvoySubMenu.onExportTracks. Superseded by the 08-20 export fix, device-verified. ⚠ Lives in frozen convoy — check blast radius first.

B aborted again on stale state. references that would not have compiled, and on a blanket state.name replace that would have produced areaLabel = areaLabel inside my own new code. Three would-be build failures caught before a build.

Builds JSON manifest in Documents/GroupTrack/imports/, loops sources (catalog via importByArea(), Geofabrik via download→OsmExtractWorker→OsmImportWorker), updates manifest at each state change, publishes StateFlow<ImportProgress>, download progress via downloadDetailFlow. Bugs Found and Fixed During Build

  1. ImportResult has inserted and skipped, no updated — fixed sumOf.
  2. Geofabrik returns 302 redirect (-latest- → dated file via Squid proxy) — added redirect following with recursive downloadFile(redirect, dest, activeSrc).
  3. OsmImportLedger.setPendingImport() silently returns if no ledger exists — added OsmImportLedger.create() call before extract (matching OsmImportPanel.kt:900 pattern).
  4. loadSourceCatalog was private in ConvoyTrailSourceScreen.kt — changed to internal.
  5. Download progress: downloadDetail as plain var invisible to Compose — changed to MutableStateFlow<String?> (downloadDetailFlow).
  6. collectAsState() inside LazyColumn items block — hoisted to ProgressPanel top level.
  7. Guard if (src.currentStep == "Downloading") never matched — removed guard, dlLive shown whenever non-null.

Architecture Decisions (08-20)

  • Manifest check for fresh install — hasCompletedImport() scans Documents/GroupTrack/imports/ for completed JSON. Does NOT query the DB (may not exist on fresh install).
  • Geocoder for GPS detection — bbox matching fails at state borders. Geocoder returns actual state name. Bbox is fallback.
  • OsmImportLedger.create() MUST be called before setPendingImport() — the ledger silently returns null if no file exists.
  • Geofabrik -latest- URLs return 302 redirects to dated files — HttpURLConnection needs explicit redirect following.
  • Download progress requires MutableStateFlow not plain var for Compose visibility across threads.
  • collectAsState() must be hoisted above LazyColumn items block.
  • Gate uses manifest check (hasCompletedImport) not DB query — DB may not exist on fresh install. ⛔ SUPERSEDED 08-21 — see 00-GATE-FINAL → THE STARTUP JOB. The check is now spatial DB file exists → trail count > 0; the file-existence step is what makes it safe on a fresh install, and it must not OPEN the DB.

SEQUENCE: Complete the import pipeline first (gate + area mode), THEN build the prototype. The suggester needs the trail network and POIs on device before it can score routes.
  • POI proximity analysis validated (08-20): Built an interactive Leaflet map showing a simulated Panguitch overnighter with half-mile POI buffer. Colour-coded markers by type (peaks, springs, cliffs, caves, trailheads, settlements). Per-mile POI density, scoring signals table, and "what this tells the suggester" analysis. File: poi_proximity_panguitch_2026-08-20.html.
  • The data is ALREADY IMPORTED — 11,773 scenic attractors + 5,220 settlements in reference_points, 559 trailheads in waypoints. No new data source needed for v1 scoring.
  • Test plan agreed (Fred 08-20): Fred will export 2 recorded tracks (GPX from Droid 1) + the nearby trails and reference_points tables for the same area. Claude builds an offline prototype that: (1) constructs a trail network graph from real geometry, (2) finds 3-4 alternative routes between the track's start/end points, (3) scores each on scenic proximity, settlement avoidance, loop closure, variety, (4) displays all alternatives on an interactive map ranked by score.
  • Ground truth: Fred's actual rides are the benchmark. If the algorithm ranks a similar route highest, it's working. If it finds something better, that's the product.
  • Missing for v1: Elevation (not in trail DB), surface/difficulty (only 30% of trails), BLM permissions (not imported). Scenic proximity + settlement avoidance work TODAY.

Patch Script Lessons Banked

  • Always use dated filenames for deliverables to avoid download overwrite confusion.
  • Python \u2630 in triple-quoted strings is the glyph, not the escape — double the backslash when anchoring on literal escape text in the file.
  • contentColor on a Surface ≠ the text colour.
  • Verify assets INSIDE the AAB before uploading: unzip -p <aab> base/assets/<file> | grep -c.

⛔⛔ 00-SAVEPATHS (08-17) — DISCARD WAS NEVER DIVERGED. SAVE WAS.

⭐⭐⭐ Fred: "the issue was standardizing the discard function. i have seen no divergence in discard. our divergence is in save correct?" — and the code agrees.

The two genuine discard sites, onExit (~1646) and the in-progress dialog (~1775), both do all five operations, identically. No drift. The entire "six variants of discard" framing in 00-DISCARD-DESIGN was built on a survey table that counted SAVE sites and LIST-MANAGEMENT sites as discard variants.

SiteWhat it actually is
~1646 onExitdiscard ✅ all five
~1775 dialogdiscard ✅ all five
~1834 resolverpresents the discard CHOICE when an in-progress file is found on entry — nothing loaded yet, so its omissions are correct
~1923 pickerthe In-Progress list — a reminder shown when more than one autosave exists. "Not part of the discard process." Pure list management; nothing to tear down
~1539 saveCompletednot a discard
~1717 save-as-WIP"not a discard process, it is what we do as an alternative" — and it RENAMES rather than deletes, so it correctly has no deleteDraft

⭐ And the sixth operation was never a discard concern at all. Fred: "it just does not belong in the discard function." setRouteMode is ambient exit behaviour that got copied into each site because it sat nearby. Ending a Route+ session closes the draw panel and turns draw mode off; re-entering is an explicit act.

⚠ METHOD NOTE, worth more than the fix. Claude built an elaborate six-variant consolidation plan from a grep table, and Fred dismantled it by asking what each site actually IS. The tell was that the "variants" needed increasingly baroque justification to remain variants. The real defect was in the code nobody had looked at, because the 08-13 survey filed it under a different word and moved on.

What the save paths actually had wrong — all three fixed today

1. saveCompleted violated a DOCUMENTED invariant. RouteDraftStore.kt:15 states a route lives as a draft JSON or a spatial-DB row, never both; ConvoyRouteToolbar.kt:176 documents this path as "insertRoute (DB row) + delete draft if any [graduation]". The code had insertRoute and no deleteDraft, no draftListTick++ — so a graduated route stayed in route_drafts/ and in the picker, and reopening that stale draft forked it into two diverging versions. Fixed a17b10fda: deletes both routeName and UNNAMED, because routeName can be blank while the autosave file exists (insertRoute substitutes a generated name for the DB row only), and deleteDraft returns true for an absent file so a miss is free.

2. Save-as-WIP never bumped draftListTick, and the list is built in remember(draftListTick) — so a freshly saved draft did not appear until the user left the map and returned, rebuilding the screen. Found on device by Fred. Fixed f71e2ddc9. ⚠ Correctly NO deleteDraft there — it is a rename.

⚠ 3. There is a SECOND, near-identical saveCompleted at ConvoyScreen.kt:1771, carrying the same "one proven completed-save path" comment. Route+ is PLANNER-ONLY, so it is unreachable dead code — deliberately not touched (convoy frozen). ⭐ The two copies had ALREADY drifted: the planner one sets addPointMode = false, the convoy one does not. A comment asserting singularity stopped being true the moment the second copy was made. Remove the dead copy when the freeze lifts.

⚠⚠ The radio-meter crash — and a guard that was never reached

The vertical interval slider in GroupHud (ConvoyScreen.kt ~2496, "5s" above / "INT" below, rotated -90°) calls setGpsInterval → channelViewModel.setConfig. With no radio the binder throws RemoteException: Not connected to radio on the main thread and the app dies.

⛔ setGpsInterval ALREADY wrapped that call in try/catch, logging "Failed to set GPS interval" — and the app still died. setConfig RETURNS before the binder throws, so the exception fires outside the try block. This is why the fix is to not RENDER the control rather than to test before calling it. Second instance in two days of a guard that reads correct and is never reached (see 00-GATE-FINAL's latch correction). ⭐ When a guard exists and the failure happens anyway, the guard is not being reached — check that before adding another.

Fix: render the slider only when state.nodes.size > 1. state is already a GroupHud parameter — no signature change, no call-site edit, no new observation, one conditional in a frozen file. It also matches the usefulness rule: "you're not adjusting broadcast times in a ride with one radio." ⚠ Node count INFERS radio presence rather than testing it; the direct signal is ConvoyViewModel.myNodeInfo != null (line 62, what ConvoyApplyRadioScreen uses). simulationMode exists, so if convoy state is ever populated without a radio, add a check inside setGpsInterval too.

⚠ R8-MINIFIED STACKS ARE ACTIVELY MISLEADING. That trace attributed frames to coil3.RealImageLoader, okhttp3.Cache, androidx.room.RoomRawQuery and UsbKt — all nonsense. Trust only frames naming real behaviour (MeshService.setConfig, SliderState.dispatchRawDelta); file attribution is a hint, not a fact — it sent Claude to the wrong file twice. ⭐ Find a UI element by its LABEL: greps for "meter", "broadcast" and "Slider" all missed it; grep -n '"INT"' found it in one shot.

🗺 00-TRAILDATA (08-17) — WHAT THE TRAIL DATA ACTUALLY CONTAINS, AND WHAT THE SUGGESTER CAN CLAIM

⭐ Full detail — 11 sections, every number measured off utah.gpkg and the 08-16 backup — is in grouptrack_design_trail_data_route_suggestion_2026-08-17.html and in memory at grouptrack-route-suggestion. Headlines only here.

  • 160,628 trails: osm 112,696 (70%) + ugrc_utah_trails 47,932 (30%). NO BLM data at all. OSM rows carry geometry + name only — nothing to filter or score on across 70% of the network.
  • motorized_allowed is EMPTY, 0 rows. "Multiuse" (38,688) is not permission. Nothing in either dataset affirmatively says OHVs are allowed.
  • The scenic layer is ALREADY IMPORTED: reference_points, 16,992 rows, fclass- and bbox-indexed — peak 6,051 · spring 4,855 · cliff 525 · volcano 131 · cave_entrance 96. No new ingestion needed for v1 scoring.
  • 68% of OSM tracks carry NO grade, and grade5 is the largest graded group — so grade can never be a rejection criterion. ⛔ fclass as an exclusion filter is WITHDRAWN (Fred: "we have no idea what some is") — but it has a better use as the SPEED input, since surface predicts pace far better than distance does.
  • BLM publishes what nothing else does: state-level OHV Designation polygons (Open/Limited/Closed per CFR 8342) and national GTLF route designations. ⭐ "LIMITED" means you must stay on designated routes — the two layers compose: polygons answer can I be here, GTLF resolves the Limited case. ⛔ BLM land only — outside it the layer is silent, and silence must not read as "open."
  • RideCalc calibrated against 88 real tracks: filtering to 3–25 mph, the 45+ mile band gives 15.3 avg, range 8.9–23.6, n=31 → default 12–18 mph. Fred's SWAG of 12–17 was essentially right. ⭐⭐ But the mechanism is stopped-time dilution, not terrain (Fred: "lunch on a short ride is a higher percent of the total time") — so avg_speed_mph is an ELAPSED average, which is exactly what a day-planner wants and removes the need for a separate stops parameter. Do not extrapolate the short bands.
  • v1's claim must SHRINK to what the data supports: scenic + loop + variety + envelope. "Trail quality and difficulty" was named first when the feature was described and is NOT available.
  • The width ruler — two bands at 50" and 72", rotatable, no tick marks, cyan+magenta. Standalone 2D overlay, no MapLibre dependency, works on 100% of trails today.

⚠ Track data quality issues found in passing: max_speed_mph pins at 199–200 constantly (clamp or GPS artifact) · one row shows 99,379 ft elevation gain on 57 miles · one row 0.0 hours on 50 miles · several rows at 47–66 mph avg are almost certainly trailer miles, the recorder left running to and from the trailhead.

⭐⭐⭐ 2.6f IS CLOSE. Branch test/authority-api36, HEAD a9f327780. Target 36 builds clean, the authority gate is device-verified on all three paths, and a data-loss defect that had been destroying the databases on every reinstall is found, fixed and certified.

WHAT IS LEFT BEFORE THE AAB — this is the whole list:

  1. Merge test/authority-api36 → feature/convoy-event-ride.
  2. Version code — git-derived (gitVersionProvider + vcOffset) and it CAN regress. Confirm it is above what is published or the upload bounces.
  3. Build the AAB, upload, get through review. ⚠ Review time is not ours to control, and this is a new target SDK plus a changed all-files flow on a re-review. A prior approval does not guarantee a consistent second one.
  4. File the Nov 1 extension form in Play Console. Still not done. It costs a form and takes the Aug 31 deadline off the critical path — worth it after a day that produced two separate data-loss discoveries on the branch we are about to ship.

✂ DROPPED from the pre-ship gate list, with reasons: asset copy + content-grep verification (no asset change this release — Fred 08-16: internal requirement only) · cache-buster (it exists to stop testers seeing stale bundled HTML after an asset update; there is no asset update, and clear-cache is a working remedy again — see 00-MARKER-LIFETIME) · credential rotation (Fred 08-16: ngrok was never used for anything, MySQL credentials already changed).

⛔⛔ THE DAY'S HEADLINE: a plain uninstall/reinstall was deleting both databases, and had been all along.

Three wipes in about twelve hours — 08-15 23:22, 08-16 07:10, 08-16 08:15 — all one defect. The v3 delete-gate in SpatialDbManager.init() was gated on a marker held in SharedPreferences (app-private), while the databases it deletes live in Documents/GroupTrack/ (public). An uninstall wipes the marker and not the data, so every reinstall read marker was 0, concluded "pre-v3", and deleted grouptrack_spatial.db + grouptrack_data.db. Full detail, the fix and the certification: 00-MARKER-LIFETIME.

⭐ AutoResyncHost's conviction is WITHDRAWN. Killing it on 08-15 was correct — it was a vestigial startup migration opening the DB pre-authority — but it did not cause these wipes, and 08-15's diagnosis credited it with the whole thing.

METHOD, banked today:

  • The reinstall test is the one that found it. A clear-cache test had already passed byte-identical and would have been accepted as proof the install work was done. Fred pushed for the uninstall/reinstall anyway. The cheaper test passing is not evidence the expensive one will.
  • The logcat capture is what made it a five-minute diagnosis instead of another evening — 353,608 lines, grabbed before the buffer rolled, and the answer was three I/ lines in it.
  • A guard and the thing it guards must share a lifetime. App-private state protecting public-storage data is the defect, and it reads as correct in review because both halves are individually sensible. Look for this shape wherever a flag, marker or receipt decides whether to touch data that outlives the flag.
  • Instrumentation earned its keep, twice. The gate emitted nothing, so working out what it had concluded meant inferring from platform quota noise around the canary file. One log line per evaluation turned the next question from a theory into a reading — and immediately disproved the theory we had just built a fix around (see 00-GATE-FINAL).
  • A multi-line anchor built from sed output missed a blank line and a patch aborted cleanly at anchor 6 of 6, no write, tree clean. cat -A showed it in one command. The rule holds: cat -A when an anchor spans lines.

DEVICE STATE AT EOD. Droid 2 (24039703201775, sacrificial) carries the a9f327780 release build with a full rebuilt dataset — 152,200 trails · 88 tracks · 559 waypoints, grouptrack_data.db 28.4 MB, grouptrack_spatial.db 281 MB, marker file .db_schema_version present containing 3. Droid 1 (8624SBCEDF00001789, PROTECTED) was unplugged from the bus early and took no part in any of today's destructive testing.

⚠ Droid 1 was attached at the start of the session while the next queued command was an uninstall. With two devices on the bus a missing -s errors out rather than picking one, so the exposure was a mistyped serial rather than a silent wrong target — but on the device holding 89K trails with no backup that is still a stop-and-unplug moment, not a be-careful moment.

⛔⛔⛔ 00-MARKER-LIFETIME (08-16) — A PLAIN REINSTALL WAS DELETING BOTH DATABASES. FOUND, FIXED, CERTIFIED.

The defect

SpatialDbManager.init() carries a one-time "regenerate-not-migrate" delete gate. Its own comment stated the assumption that broke it: "DBs live in PUBLIC storage and survive uninstall/clear-data, so this in-app delete is the SOLE clearing mechanism." True — but the marker gating it was db_schema_marker in SharedPreferences, which is app-private and does not survive an uninstall. The gate and the thing it gated had different lifetimes. "Fires exactly once per upgrade" was really "fires once per install."

08:15:02.463  SpatialDbMgr: DB v3 migration: delete grouptrack_spatial.db = true
08:15:02.503  SpatialDbMgr: DB v3 migration: delete grouptrack_data.db = true
08:15:02.514  SpatialDbMgr: DB v3 migration: spatial-clean=true extension-clean=true (marker was 0)
08:15:02.515  SpatialDbMgr: DB v3 migration: marker set to 3; DBs will rebuild from v3 assets
08:15:02.978  SpatialDbMgr: Spatial DB: 0 trails, 0 tracks, 0 waypoints, 0 routes

⭐ marker was 0 is the whole story. Not a failed open, not an authority problem — the app deliberately deleted the data because it could not tell "never migrated" from "migrated, then reinstalled." The authority canary ran at 08:15:02.123, 340 ms BEFORE the deletion, and passed. The gate worked. This was never an authority bug.

⚠ This is the inverted form of the guard specced after 08-01: marker absent + external DB present = fresh install over existing data → STOP. The marker was designed as a stop condition and implemented as a delete trigger.

The fix — two commits, two independent halves

233e61e50 — marker moves to a FILE, .db_schema_version, beside the databases so its lifetime matches theirs. Read order is file → legacy prefs → 0; the prefs fallback carries existing installs across the changeover, without which this fix would itself have wiped every current install exactly once. Plus a row-count guard: populated databases mean the install has already been through the migration whatever the marker says, so it stamps and skips. A count that cannot be taken (the SELinux ioctl denial on the FUSE-mounted .db is routine) returns −1 and is treated as populated — refuse to delete on uncertainty.

c503b3247 — stamp-if-missing. Caught on device, not in review: an install whose marker came from the legacy prefs value reached neither of the two branches that write the file, so the file was never laid down at all and the install stayed protected by app-private state — the exact mismatch the whole fix exists to remove. Now: marker resolved and current but file absent → write it.

Certification (Droid 2, 08-16)

  • Install over live data → marker file appears containing 3; sizes unchanged.
  • Uninstall → marker file survives in Documents/; prefs gone with the package.
  • Reinstall → log shows no migration block at all, and Spatial DB: 152200 trails, 88 tracks, 559 waypoints. Sizes byte-identical.

⭐ Note what that proves: not the row guard catching a miss, but the marker being read straight from public storage so the gate never armed. Belt and braces both verified independently.

Consequences to carry

  • THE 08-12 CLEAR-CACHE PROHIBITION IS RETIRED. Clear-cache on live data left both DBs byte-identical and mesh setup values intact; no state restore fired, no install questionnaire. ⚠ Scope of the proof: that run had no UID change and no named-track import in flight. ⭐ Consequence: "clear the WebView cache to fix a stale bundled asset" is an available remedy again.
  • REFUSE-TO-CREATE (00-REFUSE) is now load-bearing, not aspirational. Today's fix closes this trigger. A DB open that refuses to CREATE where a file should already exist is what turns the NEXT unknown trigger into a loud error instead of silent loss. There is no backup and the map DBs are 50–60 GB. On today's evidence it is arguably promotable into 2.6f.
  • Signature worth reading instantly: maps survive + artifacts vanish = a create-if-missing DB overwrite, NOT an authority failure. .mbtiles never go through this path, so their survival proves nothing about correctness — and a UID that could not read the DBs could not read the tiles either. Empty-schema fingerprint: grouptrack_data.db ≈ 208,896 B, grouptrack_spatial.db ≈ 118,784 B.
  • Recovery profile, measured: trails re-import from OSM, tracks resync from the track directory, waypoints likewise — full rebuild ≈ 1 hour. Graduated routes are the only unrecoverable class (see 00-DISCARD-DESIGN). Maps are never lost.

✅ 00-GATE-FINAL (08-16, UPDATED 08-21) — THE AUTHORITY GATE: SETTLED BEHAVIOUR, THE LATCH CORRECTION, AND THE STARTUP JOB (designed 08-21, NOT BUILT)

Behaviour as shipped — all three paths device-verified

SituationWhat happens
Authority already real on the FIRST evaluation of the sessionNo gate at all — straight to Convoy. Nothing was just granted, so there is nothing to settle.
State changed to Granted DURING the session (returned from settings)Continue barrier retained — the tap lets the grant land before the DB opens. This is the flash-past race and it is still real.
Went to settings, came back WITHOUT granting all-filesStorageDeclined. States the app will not open without it, and that exiting is safe. Buttons: Try again / Exit. No proceed door.
Background location not grantedAdvisory. Skip for now proceeds. Deliberately NOT remembered — the prompt recurs every launch.

Why no proceed door on storage (Fred, 08-16): "the app is unusable without that authority, period" and "cancel is the option that should be taken — they can choose not to use the app." Claude had proposed a proceed-at-your-own-risk path and then argued against it: without refuse-to-create, proceeding means silently destroying data, described to the user as a risk they cannot evaluate, because the failure looks like "no trails" rather than "your data was overwritten." Settled: no third door. The screen also states that exiting changes nothing — which is accurate, because the gate halts before any database is opened or created.

Why background location IS skippable and IS repeated (Fred): "background authority is not destructive — it reduces functionality and can be quickly changed." And the repetition is deliberate: "the reminder is fine, it warns of degradation when recording a track with an app that goes to sleep." A rider who skipped three weeks ago will not remember before a ride that their track dies when the screen sleeps.

⚠⚠ THE DIAGNOSIS CORRECTION — record this, not the first story

Symptom: grant all-files, return, and the screen stayed on StorageDeclined; a force-close and relaunch then passed immediately.

Claude's theory: a settle race — the probe taken the instant the user returns reads "not granted" because the grant has not landed yet. A five-attempt / 400 ms retry loop was built on that theory.

The logs disproved it the moment they existed. Every retry read realStorage=false all the way through attempt 4, and the successful grant passed on attempt 0. The retries never once rescued a grant.

The real defect: LifecycleResumeEffect(Unit) evaluated once on resume and latched the result. With settingsVisited set, a negative read became StorageDeclined and nothing re-evaluated — the user who had just granted access was stranded on a screen saying the app would not open. Latched state, not a timing race. The fix that mattered was re-evaluating on each resume; the retry loop is harmless insurance.

⭐ The general lesson: a fix built on an unverified theory can pass its test for a reason unrelated to the theory. The instrumentation that made this visible was added in the same patch — without it this would have gone into the release recorded as a race condition that never existed.

Also fixed: Exit was a dead button

onExit was bound to navController?.popBackStack() in ConvoyNavigation.kt — but the gate is the nav START destination, so there is nothing beneath it, the call returns false and no-ops. A dead button on the one screen that tells the user the app will not open. Now finishes the hosting activity: exiting from the start destination means leaving the app, which is the stated intent.

Gate logging (new): tag ConvoyGate, one line per evaluation — realStorage=, background=, chosen state, attempt index. adb -s <serial> logcat -s ConvoyGate. ⚠ StorageManager / MediaProvider "Failed to update quota type for .authority_probe" is noise — platform bookkeeping reacting to a file created and deleted within milliseconds. It appears on launches that pass cleanly and open 152,200 trails.

⭐⭐ THE STARTUP JOB (settled with Fred 08-21 — DESIGNED, NOT BUILT). GATEJOB-2026-08-21

⛔ THIS SUPERSEDES grouptrack_design_unified_import_2026-08-20.html §8 (gate integration) and the Recovery on App Restart half of §7A. The rest of that document — the upsert rules, source model, two entry points, manifest shape — is unchanged and still governing. The design lives HERE, in the checklist, not in a parallel design doc: design in flight expands in place, and a reader who finds §8 first must not get the superseded reasoning without the correction.

ONE JOB, TWO THINGS, before the rider reaches any map:

  1. Resolve every manifest in imports/ — completed swept to history, incomplete stamped killed and swept to history. Fred: "completed is swept, incomplete is killed on startup. Period."
  2. Decide whether the rider needs trail data — and if so, hand off to the Home State picker.

Afterwards imports/ is empty and the rider is either on the Ride Map or in the picker. There is no third state.

The sweep

ConditionAction
Every source section carries a countLeave process_state as written → move to history
Any source section carries no countStamp process_state = "killed" + killed_at → move to history

⭐ ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: stamp first, then move. Same reason OsmImportLedger.archiveLedger() renames before discardState() sweeps. Stamp fails → nothing moved, retried next launch. Move fails after a good stamp → a marked file sits in imports/, visible and recoverable. The state to avoid is a file in history nobody stamped, because then killed must be inferred rather than read.

⚠ "Has a count" must tolerate pre-08-21 manifests. The 08-20 NH manifest carries imported per source but none of the five recap counters and no manifest_id. Absent fields mean OLD, not broken. If the test requires the new fields, a device holding 43,348 records reads as never-imported and gets sent to the picker.

⚠ The sweep must never touch a live manifest. It would archive the controller's own file out from under a running import. The ordering that prevents it is the slot: inside the authority task, before anything can launch an import. Do not call the sweep from anywhere else.

⭐ The one-time cleanup is the same code — both droids hold several completed 08-20 manifests in imports/; first launch after this ships sweeps them. No migration.

The zero-data check — replaces §8's condition

Two tests, in order, both misses landing on Home State (Fred 08-21):

  1. Spatial DB file exists? No → Home State setup.
  2. Exists and holds zero trails? Yes → Home State setup.
  3. Otherwise → straight through to the Ride Map.

⛔⛔ THE FILE CHECK MUST NOT OPEN THE DATABASE. Refuse-to-create is still unbuilt and the gate halts before any DB opens by design — that ordering is what makes the declined path's "exiting is safe, nothing on your device changes" promise true. A create-if-missing open in front of that screen is the 08-01 mechanism: an empty schema written over real data. The count runs only once the file is known to exist.

Query failure falls through to the map. Fred: "query has to be successful to request home state add." Locked/mid-write/unreadable → proceed; the panel remains available at any time. The consequence of being wrong here is a redundant import, not data loss.

⚠ This supersedes the 08-20 architecture note "Gate uses manifest check (hasCompletedImport) not DB query" and §8's trails.count == 0 AND waypoints.count == 0. Waypoints are not the signal — a rider can hold waypoints with no trails — and the file-existence step is what keeps the check safe when no DB exists at all.

Where it lives

Inside the authority task. Not the nav handoff, not ConvoyScreen. The gate is already a state machine: evaluateState() at ConvoyAuthorityGateScreenV2.kt:143 resolves a state, :353 renders Granted. The zero-data condition is a new state in that same structure, rendered with the picker as its resolution. Slot confirmed by the 08-16 ordering finding: after the authority gate, before Convoy — which the gate already owns as the nav start destination, so no navigation change.

⛔ CONVOY IS FROZEN — ConvoyScreen.kt IS NOT TOUCHED. Fred 08-21: "too many competing threads." Nothing here needs that file.

The new state is additive — a branch beside them, not a modification of them.

⛔ REJECTED — AND WHY

ProposedRejected because
Wire the check into the onProceed handler in ConvoyNavigation.kt Claude's proposal 08-21, made without reading the design set — the work had been specified into the gate's own state machine days earlier. Check AllDocs for an owning design before proposing a mechanism.
Folder location alone carries the state (no killed field) Argued by Claude on the grounds that a stored verdict drifts from disk (the 07-27 status=="processed" bug). Overruled by Fred: the stamp is what gets read on a support call, and "every section has counts" needs explaining to someone reading the file cold. Constraint retained: nothing BRANCHES on killed — sweep and gate both test counts.
A guard for the finished-but-not-moved window Folded into the sweep. One rule for every file removes the edge case rather than guarding it.
Auto-recover an interrupted import silently at startup Silent one-shot work at startup is exactly the class the declared startup phase exists to make observable. Runs before anyone is watching.
Gate scans for in_progress before the zero-data check (§7A) Only meaningful with resume, which is cut. The sweep handles those manifests, so the gate gets one new condition, not two.

🔴 OPEN — not settled

  • Skip on fresh install. Carried unresolved from unified spec §14. If the rider backs out of the picker, does the job re-offer next launch or let them through with zero data? The picker has a skip; the 08-20 manual says setup is offered again next launch. Not settled in code.
  • History folder path. Assumed Documents/GroupTrack/imports/history/ by analogy with osm/history/. Not confirmed against importsDir().
  • Where the picker is invoked from once it is a gate state rather than the Map Features test harness (unified spec §13 keeps "Setup Home State" visible until certified).

Build order

  1. Sweep function — standalone, testable without the gate. Walks imports/, stamps, moves. Loud logging: id and destination per file.
  2. Zero-data check — file existence then count, read-only, failure falls through.
  3. New gate state — added to AuthorityState, evaluated in evaluateState() after the authority checks, rendered as the picker.
  4. Retire the test-harness entry — only after the gate path is device-verified.

1 and 2 are independently useful and carry no risk to the certified gate. 3 is the only edit to ConvoyAuthorityGateScreenV2.kt.

Manifest fields this depends on — LANDED 08-21

HomeStateImportController.kt, marker MANIFESTCOUNTS-2026-08-21, compile-verified (BUILD SUCCESSFUL + marker count 5):

  • manifest_id — <slug>-<iso8601>, e.g. new-hampshire-2026-08-21T08:58:21. A field, NOT a filename — existing manifests are named import_[date]_[area].json and renaming them retroactively would invent history. Slug not name, because California ships as two entries.
  • Per source: processed / selected / dupes / adds / errors, with imported retained so the existing completion screen is unaffected. -1 means NOT REPORTED, which is not zero. Identity: selected = dupes + adds + errors.

⚠ Known dead branch, deliberately not fixed: HomeStateImportController.kt:196 is if (allOk) "completed" else "completed" — both arms identical, so a run with a failed source is written as completed. Safe under this design because the sweep tests counts per section, not process_state, and a failed source carries no counts. Decide the line when the gate work is built, not inside an unrelated patch.

✅ 00-API36 (08-16) — TARGET 36 ANSWERED. THE APRIL BLOCKER IS DEAD.

⭐⭐ The all-files toggle does NOT grey out at target 36 on Android 16. Confirmed on device: "no greyed out authority panels."

This kills the entire worst-case branch of the 08-15 decision tree — no force-off-then-grant, no gated explainer screen bracketing two uncontrolled trips through OS settings, no double-prompt UX to design. That question was the reason test/authority-api36 exists.

Nothing in Android 16's documented behaviour changes touches MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE; the API-36 storage changes are photo-picker and selected-media. The April retreat to 35 was far more likely an install-state artifact — the record-vs-UID divergence — than a platform rule.

Deadline facts, stated correctly: from Aug 31 2026 every update must target API 36 or Play rejects the upload. API 35 only keeps an already-published build discoverable to new users on newer devices; it will not let 2.6f be uploaded. Ladder: 34=A14, 35=A15, 36=A16, 37=A17. ⚠ There is no such thing as "Android 16 API level 15" — Android 16 is API 36. Config lives in config.properties at the repo root (MIN_SDK=26 / TARGET_SDK=36 / COMPILE_SDK), read by build-logic/convention/.../KotlinAndroid.kt.

🗣 00-DISCARD-DESIGN (08-16) — THE FIVE-PLUS-ONE BOUNDARY. DESIGN SETTLED, NOT BUILT. (2.6g)

⛔ THE DIAGNOSIS (Fred): "that tangle is what caused unpredictable results — 6 versions of discard all mixing and matching the 6 functions."

Six sites, six independently-assembled combinations of the same operations. Each looked reasonable alone, so every fix was site-local and the next path exhibited a different variant. The bug was the ABSENCE of the module; site-local fixes kept producing new variants. The tell: each fix works and the next report describes different behaviour. This is worse than literal copy-paste — literal copies at least fail identically.

⛔⛔ And some sites are genuinely WRONG, not merely different (Fred): they did not remove the in-memory data or the JSON, and "those items are all joined." The JSON on disk, the in-memory route and the drawn canvas are three representations of one thing. A site that updates a subset leaves them disagreeing, and the next operation reads whichever it happens to consult — state divergence. That is why the "phantom draft" looked recreated: it never left memory.

⭐ VISIBLE SYMPTOM, now a signature: the route reappears when the next vertex is dropped. Memory was never cleared, so the append redraws it with the new vertex attached. The divergence is created at discard and revealed later — which is why it resisted attribution. It points straight at the site; no bisecting needed.

The boundary, as Fred specified it — FIVE in the module, ONE owned by the caller

MODULE (unconditional, every path):
  1. clearRoute()
  2. deleteDraft(name)
  3. JS clearBuildLine()
  4. addPointMode = false
  5. draftListTick++

CALLER:
  6. setRouteMode(...)

Fred: "route mode is a function of the process discard is in — does it stay in the app, in which case draw mode stays active, or does it end the route draw session." Only the caller knows the destination.

⭐ This makes the module take no flags and no exit-vs-stay parameter, and it makes the audit mechanical: any site doing fewer than all five is a bug, full stop. A missing setRouteMode is not automatically a bug — it depends on where the user is going. Previously those two questions were tangled, which is why the 08-13 survey could only mark the resolver site "missing clearRoute + JS + arm flags" without knowing which absences mattered.

⭐⭐ It is TWO operations, not one: discard and save

Both END a route session; they differ only in what happens to the draft first (delete vs persist). Consequence: the two SAVE sites (~1539 SAVE-as-completed, ~1717 SAVE-as-WIP) were filed in the 08-13 survey as "not discards" and were therefore never audited for teardown completeness. Under this factoring they are the same shape and must be checked with the rest. If any save path leaves arm flags set or the build line drawn, that is the same class of bug — just filed under a different word.

The six sites to classify by destination (ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt, ⚠ re-grep line numbers, they drift): ~1646 onExit (fixed 08-13, the archetype) · ~1775 "Delete in-progress" dialog · ~1834 resolver Discard · ~1923 picker Delete · ~1539 SAVE-completed · ~1717 SAVE-WIP.

⚠ No STUB: markers were ever placed — Phase 1 of the 08-13 stub plan never ran (08-15 went to the authority gate instead). The extraction source is f9652276c.

Route recovery — the only unrecoverable data class

Trails re-import, tracks resync, waypoints likewise. Graduated (permanent) routes have nothing behind them, because graduation is exactly the step that stops maintaining a file on disk. WIP drafts survive everything (Fred: "they have survived since day 1") because they are files outside the DB-open path. That asymmetry is the defect, not the incident.

Fred's fix (08-16): archive the WIP into a completed/ subfolder we can recreate from. ⭐ Cheapest useful version: COPY at save time, DB row stays authoritative — a few lines, no new read path, no format decision, and it closes the class on its own. ⚠ OPEN: copy-and-keep-DB-authoritative, or does the archived file become the recovery format with its own re-import path? The second is real work and overlaps the route-distribution feature already wanted.

🔎 00-STARTUP-SURVEY (08-16) — THE DECLARED STARTUP PHASE, AND THE HUNT FOR OTHER ONE-SHOTS. (2.6g)

⭐⭐⭐ THE ARGUMENT IS OBSERVABILITY, NOT SEQUENCING (Fred): "the issue was the unobservability of a one-shot process that deleted and recreated DBs."

It announced itself as three I/ log lines inside a 353,608-line buffer. No UI, no user-facing consequence, no record that survives the session. We caught it only because the ring buffer had not rolled yet. A one-shot destructive operation should be the loudest thing the app does. Same principle already banked for the queue: the record is the artifact — filtering hides reversibly, deletion destroys evidence.

⚠ ORDERING CORRECTION. "Ahead of the authority grants" does not work mechanically: the 08-16 log shows the canary at 08:15:02.123 and the deletes at .463 — the migration already ran AFTER authority. Running it earlier means operating without a verified grant, and a delete that fails on scoped storage takes the retry branch (marker NOT advanced, will retry next launch) = a loop where each attempt is another chance to half-delete. ⭐ Correct slot: AFTER the authority gate, BEFORE Convoy — a slot the gate already owns as the nav start destination, so it needs no edit to ConvoyScreen at all.

Fred on why this outranks the individual fix: "a big ball of yarn twisted into a circle — it rolls, but you are still afraid to untangle the strings." Least-invasive is how a tangle keeps rolling. The deliverable is the RULE about where work belongs, not the relocation. A rule tells a future migration where to go without anyone having to remember today.

⚠ Recorded honestly: a pre-convoy job would have run the SAME logic against the SAME marker and produced the SAME wipe, just from a tidier place. The structural work buys observability and ordering; refuse-to-create buys protection. Keep the two credits separate.

The survey — not started, and it is the input the phase needs

Fred: "who knows how many other start-up tasks are embedded in convoy using the hunt-seek-find method of locating it within the startup stack so it runs today and produces the right results but maybe not tomorrow." Two instances already found — the v3 migration gate and AutoResyncHost. No reason to assume they are the last two.

Hunt shape: markers and version gates (getSharedPreferences, getInt(.*marker, version <) · one-shot latches (firstRun, hasMigrated, if (!.*initialized) · destructive calls in startup paths (.delete(), deleteDatabase, mkdirs near an open) · everything hanging off MainActivity.onCreate, Application.onCreate and Convoy's entry composables. Use --include='*.kt' to skip the .bak_* files.

⭐ Start with field_crossref_raw.txt — one-shot state lives in FIELDS (markers, flags, hasRun), so a field index surfaces them directly; then grep the call sites. ⚠ The xrefs are DEFINITION/symbol indexes, not a call graph — grep stays authoritative for call sites. Regenerated in f7be2e056, current as of 08-16.

Cost: about an hour of grep and reading. No builds, no device risk. Fits a window while something else compiles.

What closed today (08-13 session):

  • THE DISCARD BUG — root cause found + fixed + device-verified. The toolbar Discard button (ConvoyRouteToolbar:196) branches if (routeLifecycleState == ROUTE_LS_RESUMED) onDiscardRequested() else onExit(). For a NEW route it called onExit(), which cleared the screen + memory but had no deleteDraft — so Auto Saved In Progress.json survived every discard. It was never a phantom being recreated; it was one real autosave that discard never deleted, so it looked like it "kept coming back" because it never left. Fix: added RouteDraftStore.deleteDraft(routeName) + draftListTick++ to onExit (ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt ~1651). Device-verified on Droid 2: create route, 5 pts, Discard → route_drafts/ empty. Committed f9652276c.
  • Also in that commit: removed the two inline entry-path deleteDraft(UNNAMED) branches we had bolted on during the earlier DISCARDWINS attempts (they fought the flow). Net 7 insertions, 22 deletions.
  • Earlier in the day: the convoy viewport-save fix was re-applied (lost to the 07-12 checkout) and committed 3bbb71484, and an AAB was published. HEAD now carries both fixes.

⚠ LESSON banked: verify a fix by its on-disk result (grep the current file / ls the device), never by "the patch applied." The whole hunt turned on reading the actual onExit branch instead of theorizing — several plausible-but-wrong theories (empty-shell phantom, persist-guard, between-gates create, gut-the-autosave) were disproven by code + device evidence before the real cause surfaced.

▶ TOMORROW — TWO TASKS, IN ORDER. Full detail so a fresh conversation can execute.

TASK 1 — Standardize all discard functions to the same 6-call teardown

Why: the discard/teardown logic is duplicated across sites, each doing a different subset of the steps — classic copy-paste drift, and exactly what let the onExit bug hide. Fred: "standardize all discard functions to the same 6 calls … there is no reason for the differences." Consistency pass FIRST (safe — only adds missing steps), refactor to a shared function SECOND (separate, later step). Rationale: refactoring identical code is safe; consolidating divergent code forces picking a winner per difference and can propagate a bug to every call site at once.

The canonical 6-step teardown (active-session discard):

1. RouteManager.clearRoute()
2. RouteDraftStore.deleteDraft(<name>)
3. webViewRef?.evaluateJavascript("setRouteMode(false); clearBuildLine();", null)
4. addPointMode = false
5. routeMode = false
6. draftListTick++

Sites in app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt (⚠ RE-GREP line numbers first — they drift every edit):

Site (~line)WhatStatus
~1646 onExitNEW-route discard✅ complete (today's fix)
~1775 dialog "Delete in-progress"RESUMED discard✅ complete
~1834 resolver "Discard"UNNAMED remnant discard❌ MISSING clearRoute + JS setRouteMode(false)/clearBuildLine + addPointMode=false + routeMode=false. Has only deleteDraft(UNNAMED)+draftListTick++. → bring to full 6.
~1923 picker "Delete"list-management delete⚠ DESIGN DECISION PENDING
~1539SAVE-as-completed (graduation, insertRoute)NOT a discard — leave alone
~1717SAVE-as-WIP (renameDraft)NOT a discard — leave alone

⚠ DECISION FRED MUST MAKE FIRST: does the picker "Delete" (~1923) get the full 6-step teardown, or stay as list-management (deleteDraft(d) + draftListTick++ only)? It deletes a draft from the list while you may be in a different context — full teardown there would disarm a drawing session the user didn't mean to end. Claude's recommendation: standardize the three active-session discards (onExit, dialog, resolver) to the identical 6; leave the picker as list-management. But Fred said "ALL discard functions" — so confirm scope before patching.

Method (Fred's patch convention): dated Python script → /c/Users/kixaz/Downloads/, hardcode REPO_ROOT, dry-run default, anchor-guarded, idempotent marker guard, back up target. Re-grep + paste current text of the ~1834 (and ~1923 if in scope) blocks to anchor exactly. Apply → ./gradlew compileGoogleReleaseKotlin (~3 min gate) → build APK → install Droid 2 → retest each discard path leaves route_drafts/ clean. Then commit manually, file-scoped.

Convoy has its own copy at ConvoyScreen.kt:1893 — out of scope unless Fred extends it.

AFTER consistency is proven on device: the REFACTOR — extract teardownRouteSession() (the 5 non-delete steps) + discardDraft(name) (= deleteDraft + teardown), have every site call one of them. Pure de-duplication, no behavior change. This is a SEPARATE step, not part of Task 1. ⭐ THIS IS THE ARCHETYPE of the standing shared-module direction (see §00-2.6e-OPEN): one common discard process replacing the six independent mechanisms. Consistency-first, then consolidate — never extract from divergent copies (that forces picking a winner per difference and can propagate a bug to all six). VERIFY via the stub-first checklist: mark every discard site, wire each to the common process, then grep to confirm ZERO sites still carry their own inline teardown. This is the exact discipline that would have prevented the original discard bug (the missing deleteDraft at onExit would have shown as an unwired site).

TASK 2 — Run recommit_docs_v16.sh and verify no issues

What v16 fixes: the recommit bug where a bare git commit committed the ENTIRE index — so anything already staged before a run got folded into the "docs:" commit (this is how the 08-11 bisect revert and asset edits got swept into docs history, shifting the ground under later edits). v16's fix: the commit is scoped to docs only via git commit -m "docs: …" -- "$DOCS" (pathspec), so nothing outside docs/ is ever swept, regardless of index state. v16 is the uploaded/placed file; it is currently untracked in git (was at docs/recommit_docs_v16.sh).

Pre-run guards (Fred's standing rules — DO NOT skip):

  1. git status FIRST. If anything unexpected is staged, unstage it. (v16 is safe regardless, but the glance is free.)
  2. ONE tree-touching job at a time. No Gradle build running; not inside any patch's generate→certify window. Recommit runs AFTER an AAB publish, never during a build.

Steps:

  1. git status — confirm clean/expected.
  2. Commit the v16 script itself deliberately BY NAME first (don't let a recommit be what commits it): git add docs/recommit_docs_v16.sh && git commit -m "docs: recommit v16 (scope commit to docs pathspec)".
  3. Run bash docs/recommit_docs_v16.sh.
  4. Verify no sweep: git show --stat HEAD on the resulting docs commit — confirm it touched only docs/, no .kt/.html assets folded in.
  5. Confirm the xref / AllDocs regenerated (not stale). Freshness assertions / completion marker should report DONE.

⚠ If it swept anything outside docs/ → the pathspec fix didn't take; inspect the commit line in the script (must be git commit -m "…" -- "$DOCS", not a bare git commit).

Open threads carried forward (not blocking the two tasks)

  • WIP-retrieval / recovery prompt: the recovery prompt is gated on the STALE pmSeed.routeState?.open flag (violates STATUS-IS-DERIVED), not on draftExists() — worth switching to draft existence. listDrafts() itself reads ALL .json and has NO UNNAMED filter, so the "autosave not in the picker" was likely today's fix working (no orphan to list), not a separate exclusion bug. Re-check after Task 1.
  • 07-22 picker bug (populated drafts not listing) — if still live, look in the picker's emulatedDrafts build (MVS ~219-220), not listDrafts.
  • Route-mode second disarm path (item 2 of the 08-13 list below) — still open, still release-affecting.

Map-manifest / DR / device-seeding / coverage-sharing⭐ The strongest use for the backend (from the 08-13 session): a kilobyte-sized recipe describing GBs of tiles → (1) disaster recovery (reinstall→pull→resubmit, unattended vs a day of reconstruction), (2) device seeding, (3) coverage sharing (route-import generalized to whole stores). ⚠ tiles are rebuildable; artifacts (tracks/trails) are NOT — those need real backup, small enough the backend could hold outright. ⚠ Manifest must live OFF-device (survive the thing it protects against). Conversation: "Grouptrack 2.6e playstore release" (08-13).

🗣 00-2.7-DISCUSSION — planning substrate for 2.7 (accumulated design findings, 08-14). NOT a spec — a roadmap to plan from.

⭐⭐ FEATURE VISION (Fred 08-15) — THE "MOST INTERESTING ROUTE" PLANNER. Differentiated; future (2.7+ / own tier). NOT 2.6f.

The feature: user drops vertices/waypoints all over a map → app auto-designs 3-4 DISTINCT rideable routes, each 45-60 miles / 5-9 hours of riding (at OHV speeds), that collectively connect all the dropped points. Multi-day trip planning over the trail network.

⭐ THE DIFFERENTIATOR: existing routing (Google/onX/standard nav) optimizes SHORTEST/FASTEST. OURS optimizes the MOST INTERESTING route — the best RIDE, not the quickest path. For OHV riders "shortest" is the WRONG answer; nobody off-roads to get somewhere fast. We BORROW the routing machinery (graph traversal / pathfinding — solved, take from existing algos) but CHANGE THE OBJECTIVE FUNCTION from minimize-distance to MAXIMIZE-INTERESTINGNESS. Plumbing is standard; the optimization TARGET is ours = the innovation.

The real work isn't the algorithm (borrow it) — it's DEFINING "interesting" (a product problem = where we win). Scoring inputs: trail quality/difficulty (technical sections, the good stuff — Thunderforest Outdoors trail data + trail classifications), scenery (overlooks, vistas, terrain variety), loop-ness (good rides loop, not out-and-back), variety (mix trail types, avoid 40mi of the same fire road), hitting the user's dropped waypoints, all within the 45-60mi/5-9hr envelope as the FRAME. Leans on the spatial DB (89K trails, routing graph) + RideCalc (10-15 mph → the constraint that makes it multi-DAY trip planning, not point-to-point). Big nav apps CAN'T easily do this — built around shortest-path, no OHV-trail-quality data, no rider's-day framing. ⚠ constrained-optimization / vehicle-routing-family problem (distance + time + coverage constraints) — real algorithm needed (nearest-neighbor+constraints or a proper solver). Fits the 2.7 MapLibre/spatial-routing direction. NOT 2.6f (compliance only), likely 2.7+ or its own premium-tier feature.

Raw findings recovered from the design docs + past conversations on 08-14, parked here so 2.7 planning starts from what's already been analyzed rather than re-recovering it. Each item tagged DECIDED / OPEN. Sources: SCOPING_v2.7_vector_rendering_2026-07-26.html, memory grouptrack-vector-rendering / grouptrack-map-sources, and conversations "Track map downloading in 2.6c corridor" (07-26), "Release 2.6e route and convoy handling improvements" (08-02).

Rendering engine — the core decision

DECIDED 08-14 MapLibre, rendering satellite imagery, to leverage the spatial DB + improve functionality + tighter integration with existing JS functions. INTEGRATE not rip-and-replace: JS functions kept and integrated more tightly; MapLibre upgrades the rendering surface beneath them; spatial DB drives sat-image rendering.

DECIDED 08-02 ⭐⭐ THE (A)/(B) SPLIT — carry this into planning, every prior version bundled them:
(A) MapLibre as RENDERER, raster/satellite sources UNCHANGED — gets rotation, 3D tilt, Terrain-RGB slope shading, data-driven artifact styling, and the iPad/PC cross-platform multiplier, while satellite stays exactly as it is. (B) VECTOR BASEMAPS — the storage-savings argument. ⭐ (A) does NOT require (B). The 08-14 direction (sat imagery in MapLibre) is essentially (A).

DECIDED Fred 08-02: "If I could rotate sats we would not be having this discussion." → He CAN — MapLibre rotates RASTER tiles, the camera is source-agnostic. Rotation/tilt/pitch all work on plain satellite with no vector tiles. Leaflet cannot rotate at all (a Leaflet limitation, not a raster one).

What (A) buys — judge each on load-bearing vs. pleasant (Fred's framing)

  • WEIGH Rotation (heading-up) — arguably NAVIGATION, not decoration: a rider following a trail reads a rotated map faster than north-up (every handheld GPS offers it). Judge SEPARATELY from 3D — it was not previously on the list.
  • WEIGH Terrain-RGB slope shading — for OHV arguably more useful than the imagery itself.
  • WEIGH Cross-platform reuse — MapLibre GL is both native iOS/macOS SDK and JS lib; map layer is already HTML/JS in a WebView → one renderer all three surfaces (Android/iPad/PC) inherit. Ties to 2.8.
  • WEIGH 3D tilt / styling polish — closer to genuinely nice-to-have.

The real cost of (A) — known and large

  • Renderer swap on FROZEN code — the dominant cost is operating on the frozen convoy tangle. ⭐ SEQUENCING ARGUMENT: a convoy rewrite opens that tangle anyway → rewrite the layer ONCE with MapLibre as target, or TWICE. (This is why 2.7 = convoy rewrite is the right home.)
  • spatial_artifacts.js rewritten from L.polyline to GeoJSON sources + layer specs (MapLibre gives no DOM per feature — queryRenderedFeatures instead).
  • MapStateStore + self-saving setView extended for BEARING and PITCH.
  • Tile interception moves from shouldInterceptRequest to MapLibre's addProtocol. ⭐ ties to the 2.6e shared-tile-module work — build that module with this migration in mind.
  • ⚠ Snap is UNAFFECTED — it's Kotlin, renderer-independent, resolves by DB ID.

⚠⚠ 08-02 — "THE WHOLE APP CHANGES, MAP SOURCES ETC HAVE TO BE RE-THOUGHT." A MapLibre move re-opens the whole source model: the catalog is RASTER-SHAPED (url_template {z}/{x}/{y}, cache_dir, max_zoom per source); vector needs a style spec, glyph/sprite URLs, source-layers. And the SLOT concept assumes 4 mutually-exclusive raster basemaps while MapLibre composites raster+vector+terrain simultaneously — slots may not survive as a concept.

If (B) vector is ever pursued — the storage arithmetic + gotchas (parked, not decided)

  • ≈80% tile reduction finding: two of SAT's three stores are LABELS not photography (place + transportation labels delivered as photographs of words). Bar 10: ~107,000 raster → ~21,700 with vector labels. Vector is small because it STOPS at ~z14 and renders deeper by scaling geometry — z15-18 is ~98% of downloads; vector removes those levels rather than shrinking them.
  • ⛔ GLYPH BUNDLING for offline labels — the requirement most likely to be missed if (B) is done.
  • ⚠ The storage argument is the part that FAILS in the woods (tagged-data coverage question) — which is why (A) may be all that's wanted.
  • Tooling if ever built: Protomaps/PMTiles (single-file, pmtiles extract --bbox), Planetiler / Tilemaker, OpenMapTiles schema. Local artifacts exist: vector_label_prototype.html.

🔴 GPX IMPORT — THE BYPASS, THE FIX, AND THE PATH THAT STILL FAILS. GPXIMPORT-EOD-2026-08-22

Testers could not import routes. Cause found by reading the code, not guessing: ConvoyTrackOps.kt carried a TEMP BYPASS from 2026-06-02 —

val waypoints = emptyList<GpxWaypoint>()  // was: parseGpxWaypoints(text)
val routes    = emptyList<GpxRoute>()     // was: parseGpxRoutes(text)

with the comment "GPX import of waypoints/routes is a separate untested task... re-enable when import logic is built + tested." ⭐ Everything downstream was already complete — the insert loops, the WKT build, the bbox, the type mapping. Only the two parser calls were stubbed. A deliberate hold that outlived its reason by nearly three months.

AllDocs had it recorded: "import currently processes tracks only — waypoint/route GPX import is a TEMP BYPASS (2026-06-02, untested)."

✅ FIXED AND DEVICE-VERIFIED. Broken Ridge 2 imported to Droid 1 with 31,845 chars of geometry and a correct bbox (38.12–38.26 N, -113.64 to -113.41 W). Marker GPXIMPORT-2026-08-22N.

Also fixed: the <rte> regex matched only a BARE tag, so a GPX writing <rte xmlns=...> imported silently as zero routes — indistinguishable from a file with no routes in it.

Coordinate order verified before enabling, not assumed: parser stores Pair(lon, lat), the data class documents it, the consumer destructures to match, the WKT emits lon lat. No transposition.

⛔ THE AUTO-IMPORT PATH STILL FAILS — and it is the one testers use

Two entry points, only one works:

  • Manual, from Downloads → Map Features: works. Runs importGpxAllArtifacts.
  • Tapping a GPX attachment in email: fails silently for routes. The intent filter routes it to the OLD track-only function.

Why it is not a quick fix: the filters live on com.geeksville.mesh.MainActivity — Meshtastic's own activity, not a convoy class — and every one declares <data android:scheme="content" />. So the intent hands over a content:// URI while importGpxAllArtifacts takes a File and calls readText and delete on it. The stream must be copied to a temp file first. Same problem the OSM import already solved ("SQLite needs a real path").

⭐⭐ THIS BELONGS TO 2.7. GroupTrack behaviour living inside Meshtastic's activity is precisely the entanglement the mesh separation exists to remove. Patching it into 2.6g fixes the symptom in the wrong file.

WORKAROUND for testers, works today: save the attachment to Downloads, then import from Map Features → IMPORT TRACKS, WAYPOINTS & ROUTES. One extra step.

⚠ Two consequences of enabling import, and one open bug

  • The source file is now DELETED once anything imports (totalImported > 0). Before this a waypoints-only GPX imported nothing and survived.
  • ⛔ insertRoute writes a PERMANENT spatial-DB row, bypassing draft-then-graduate. An imported route is permanent immediately — and permanent routes are the only unrecoverable class on this project.
  • ⚠ GPX EXPORT writes a hardcoded label instead of the artifact name (Fred, 08-22, not yet diagnosed). Lives in ConvoyArtifactOps.buildGpx(), the same file as the 08-20 track fix. Untested and open.

⚠⚠ METHOD — three compile failures on a two-line change, all the same mistake

The bypass comment said parseGpxWaypoints(text). I restored it verbatim → Unresolved reference 'text'. Then substituted fullText from a grep → Unresolved reference 'fullText'. It was declared inside a run { } block that closes before the call site.

⭐⭐⭐ BOTH FAILURES WERE THE SAME ERROR: reaching for a variable NAME without checking its SCOPE. And a three-month-old comment is a record, not a specification — it named a variable in a function that has since been split.

⭐ THE XREFS WOULD HAVE ANSWERED IT IN ONE LOOK. where_used_raw.txt showed fullText used ONLY at lines 531–533 — all inside the block. They were open the whole time and I reached for them only after the second failure. Fred, 08-22: "we need to use the xrefs more to research."

⭐ And the obvious fix was wrong. Calling sourceFile.readText() again would have compiled — but the comment at :520 records that the whole-file approach OOM'd on large onX exports, which is why the streaming rewrite happened. Two reads doubles that on exactly the files that already crashed. Correct fix: hoist the single read above the block. ⚠ The OOM risk is unchanged, not removed — it remains open against this function.

🆕🆕🆕 AUTO-EXPLORE ROUTES BY AREA — PROVEN IN RESEARCH 2026-08-22. AUTOEXPLORE-2026-08-22

Fred, 08-22: "so with this function I start with any trailhead waypoint and explore an area for recommendations." That is the feature. Drop a pin or tap an existing waypoint, give it a mileage ceiling, and get four rides back — each with a mile-by-mile narrative of what is on it.

Built and run against the LIVE spatial DB (216,616 trails) in three corridors. Nothing was tuned between them. Scripts: nav_pass1..7, D:\nav_test\.

⭐⭐ THE THREE CORRIDORS — evidence the model generalises

Toroweap (fixed dest)Bar 10 (free)PanguitchBroken Ridge
CharacterArizona Strip — BLM, 77% unnamed track Dixie NF — richly namedModena — sparse rail corridor
Network edges27,20527,2462,084
POIs reachable395122
Best route78 mi / 6 feats74 mi / 10 79 mi / 1379 mi / 9
Retrace50%36%25%16%
Unconfirmed junctions176531041
Vertices4,2032,3683,8691,286

⭐ Panguitch produced the best rides because its ground is NAMED — Indian Hollow Trail, Trail Creek, Upper Bear Valley Road, Showalter Mountain, Buckskin Road, Sandy Caddy Creek OHV Loop. The Strip's routes ran on "unnamed track" for most of their distance and could not be described. Naming is what makes a suggestion communicable, which is the argument for the rider-naming channel in 3.0.

⭐ Broken Ridge produced ONE unconfirmed junction across 79 miles, against 176 on the Strip — same tolerance, same code. The caution count is a property of the DATA, not the setting: sparse well-separated roads have no ambiguous junctions, dense parallel BLM tracks have thousands. That kills the idea that the threshold needed tuning.

⛔⛔ FOUR WRONG TURNS, recorded so they are not taken again

1. DISTANCE MUST NOT BE IN THE SCORE. The first search scored poi_value − miles×0.06, which is a CHEAPEST-PATH search wearing a POI bonus. It returned 5 features on 69 miles and no springs at all though springs were 22 of the 39. Fred: "point value should be the winner and if we are not getting all features & POIs the scoring is incorrect?" ⭐ Miles are a BUDGET, never a cost. Score the features; spend miles until the budget runs out.

2. PENALISE, DO NOT FILTER. Generating 4,000 candidates, ranking them, then filtering for distinctness returns one route — on a corridor with a fixed spine every candidate shares it. Navigation apps do the opposite: find the best, penalise its edges (×2.2), re-solve. The second route is then the best route that AVOIDS the first. That is the difference between "one answer and 3,999 rejects" and four alternatives.

3. RANK, DO NOT PRE-CONSTRAIN. Fred: "if we used typical navigation on its head and ranked the explored tracks by total distance and highest score we could then set the distance/time cutoff to that list." Searching inside a 55–70 band strangled it before it found anything. Explore widely, score, rank — the rider's time budget becomes a cutoff on a ranked list.

4. THE GREEDY RUNS TO THE CEILING. With one target distance every restart produced the same length and the ranked table read "none" at every cutoff below the max. ⭐ Fix: each restart draws its OWN target from the range, so the pool spans it.

⭐ AND A POSITIVE-SCORE FLOOR IS REQUIRED. The iterative penalty eventually makes every scoring corridor too expensive and the search returns the best REMAINING route, which visits nothing — Toroweap 3 and 4 both came back score 0.0. Two good routes beat four padded ones.

⭐ THE COVERAGE CURVE — what mileage BUYS (measured, Arizona Strip)

budgetmilesfeatures
60 mi57.04
100 mi99.48
150 mi139.912
200 mi188.815
unlimited448.739 (all)

⭐ Roughly 12 miles per feature, near-LINEAR, no knee. There is no cheap cluster to sweep — the features are genuinely spread. So a 60-mile ride reaching 4–5 features is not a scoring failure, it is what the corridor costs. All 39 were reachable; nothing was stranded.

⭐ The ride is a coverage TRADE and the app should say so: "this 62-mile route reaches 5 of 39 features in the area" is honest, and it is what makes a longer option meaningful.

⭐⭐ JUNCTION CONFIDENCE — the tolerance sweep, and why cautions are carried not hidden

Trails are joined into a network by snapping nearby vertices. The tolerance decides what counts as a junction, and it was MEASURED rather than picked:

tolerancejunctionscomponentsdead endsdegree-4sample gaps
0 ft5,54013219%30%exact shared vertices
10 ft7,6598514%48%0.0–6.0 ft — float noise, REAL
25 ft13,864628%70%11.8–14.8 ft — probably real
50 ft21,915494%81%25–45 ft
100 ft26,233373%85%53–92 ft
150 ft25,530383%85%junctions FALL

⛔ Degree-4 climbing to 85% is parallel tracks being WELDED TOGETHER, not crossings — a real trail network is mostly degree 1, 2 and 3. And at 150 ft junctions fall, because the snap grid starts merging genuinely separate junctions. Anything above 25 ft is invention.

⭐⭐ FRED'S CALL (08-22): do not hide it behind a threshold — carry it as a rating. "add that factor to the trail rating. caution 25ft intersection boundaries" and "highlight the 25' intersection on the suggested route map so they can use sat to zoom in." Build at 25 ft; every junction wider than 10 ft is a CAUTION carrying its real gap. The wording a rider can act on: "trails mapped 14 ft apart — junction not confirmed, check the satellite view." They can see the ground; the data cannot.

⚠ Still to decide: cautions are currently emitted for every junction the route PASSES THROUGH. A rider only needs the warning where the route TURNS onto a trail that may not connect. That would cut 176 to a handful and is the honest version of the warning.

⭐ And it feeds the 3.0 channel: a rider who rides an unconfirmed junction knows whether it exists. Same shape as naming an unnamed section — an observation from the ground, dated, uploaded, distributed.

The output shape, as settled

  • WIP DRAFTS, not GPX tracks. Four route_drafts/<Set> N.json at schema 1, so the planner opens them as WIPs over satellite with its own editing tools. Only one gets renamed and saved as a real route. ⭐ The planner is already the inspection surface — no HTML viewer needed, and review happens at the zoom that matters.
  • FULL FIDELITY vertices. Fred: "start out with the 4000 points and see if it is a problem we even need to solve." It is not — 4,203 vertices loaded fine. lineId carries the source trail and snapped: true, because these came from real geometry.
  • A .highlights.json SIDECAR beside each draft: summary, trail-by-trail legs with cumulative mileage, cautions, features — plus a narrative block in plain English. Fred: "nothing human readable here about feature and poi on this route."
  • ⛔ NO POI WAYPOINTS. Fred, 08-22: "don't need waypoints of POI. they are already on the map." reference_points came in with the OSM import and already renders. Creating waypoints would duplicate map data and leave litter when a WIP is discarded.
  • ⚠ GPX waypoint import does not currently work — found 08-22. Affects any rider importing from onX or Gaia, so it earns a fix on its own merits.

🔴 PLANNER PREREQUISITES — found by using it, not polish

  1. A WIP must open in ARTIFACT mode, not draw mode. Opening in draw means the first tap adds a point. Measured: one stray vertex took Toroweap 1 from 78.8 to 109.0 miles with a 21.9-mile straight line out and back.
