⭐⭐⭐ REAL ARCHITECTURAL BUG FOUND (Fred 08-15) — IMPORT-TRACKS JOB RUNS BEFORE THE AUTHORITY GATE AND OPENS THE DBs. Sequence: app launch → import-tracks job executes (opens DBs) → THEN the authority gate → convoy. The DB is opened BEFORE authority is established. This VIOLATES the gate's core principle ("no free-for-all — everything pre-convoy goes through the gate first"). We gated the NAVIGATION entry (gate = nav start destination) but a NON-navigation job (import-tracks) fires at PROCESS startup, upstream of the whole nav graph — the gate guards the UI path but not the background-job path. That's why "no data" on the clean test: the import job hit the DB without authority before the gate could run. FIX DIRECTION: the import-tracks job (and anything that opens a DB at startup) must NOT run until the gate establishes authority — either (a) GATE IT: the job checks the same real-read authority and defers/refuses until authority is real, or (b) SEQUENCE IT: the job doesn't fire at process start; it's triggered AFTER the gate proceeds (moved downstream). This is the "no free-for-all" principle applied to background jobs, not just navigation. NEXT: locate the import-tracks job (grep importTracks/TrackImport + Application onCreate/WorkManager) and see where it fires + what opens the DB.

🎯 CULPRIT FOUND (08-15) — AutoResyncHost() at MainActivity.kt:136. It's called directly in MainActivity → fires at APP STARTUP, before the nav graph + gate render. AutoResyncHost (AutoResyncHost.kt:34) calls SpatialDbManager.syncTracksFromFiles(context) (line 53) which OPENS THE SPATIAL DB. So: MainActivity starts → AutoResyncHost() opens the spatial DB BEFORE authority → nav graph → gate (too late, DB already opened). This is the pre-authority DB open, confirmed. It's an auto-resync-tracks-from-files-into-DB job that runs automatically every launch (Fred: "I don't know why it's there" — may be vestigial). NOT manifest-launched (Fred's guess) — launched directly from MainActivity:136. Other syncTracksFromFiles call sites (all user-triggered, fine): ConvoyMapViewerScreen:2155, ConvoyTrackImportScreen:327. FIX (= the gate principle applied to bg jobs): AutoResyncHost must NOT run until the gate establishes authority — either SEQUENCE it (move the call from MainActivity startup to AFTER the gate proceeds) or GATE it (AutoResyncHost checks real-read authority first, defers until real). NEED TO SEE: MainActivity.kt ~130-140 (how it's invoked) + AutoResyncHost.kt 34-65 (what it does) to pick sequence-vs-gate.

✅ DECISION — KILL AutoResyncHost, it's VESTIGIAL (Fred 08-15). Confirmed dead code: the modern version of this task (track/artifact migration/sync) is performed by the WORK-ARTIFACTS / IMPORT-ARTIFACTS panel. AutoResyncHost is old superseded auto-migration; tonight's testing (fresh installs / clear-data / churn) uncovered conditions where autoResyncNeeded() returns true again and it fires at MainActivity startup, opening the DB pre-authority. Safe to remove — no need to re-home the migration in the gate (the import panel covers it). What it was: a one-time track migration ("migrating tracks") gated behind autoResyncNeeded(), calling SpatialDbManager.init + syncTracksFromFiles at MainActivity:134 before the gate. FIX: patch_kill_autoresynchost_2026-08-15_v1.py removes the single AutoResyncHost() call line from MainActivity (dry-run, anchor-guarded, line-ending-agnostic, .bak). Leaves syncTracksFromFiles + user-triggered call sites (ConvoyMapViewerScreen:2155, ConvoyTrackImportScreen:327) intact. AutoResyncHost.kt file left unused (delete later). After kill: DB no longer opens at startup — only after the gate (via convoy) + explicit user actions. This closes the pre-authority DB-open, the last piece.

⭐ TWO-BUILD SEQUENCE (Fred 08-15). BUILD 1 (in progress): compile ConvoyAuthorityGateScreen STANDALONE — prove the code is clean, nothing removed, nothing wired. BUILD 2 (next): the SWAP — (a) REMOVE the existing authority checks + grants from convoy (ConvoyScreen.kt: all-files 145/148-153/465-480; background-location 142/1195/1276), (b) REPLACE by wiring the pre-convoy authority validator+grantor ahead of convoy (EmailGate nav pattern), (c) BUMP TARGET_SDK 35→36 (config.properties) — belongs WITH the swap because the gate's whole purpose is to make all-files + background-location work correctly on Android 16, so it must be built+tested AT target 36 (testing on 35 wouldn't prove it fixes what broke at 36). All on a THROWAWAY branch so a misbehaving API-36 leaves the working branch safe. Result: convoy stops doing its own juvenile authority handling entirely; the gate validates+grants properly before convoy ever loads. Order is deliberate: prove new code compiles FIRST, then remove-and-replace as its own discrete verified change.

⭐⭐ ROOT-CAUSE HYPOTHESIS (Fred 08-15) — THE GREY IS AN AUTHORITY MISMATCH: "can't grant if you already have." Theory: the toggle greyed because the OS side thought authority was ALREADY present (grant control disabled — granting an already-granted permission is a no-op → greyed), WHILE the app's isExternalStorageManager() check said NOT granted (so it kept prompting). The two disagree = a LINGERING/STALE authority record (AppOps grant survived a prior install, or an inconsistent state) → OS-settings and app-runtime-check out of sync, and NEITHER can resolve it because the toggle is disabled precisely because the OS thinks it's done. This IS the item-7 "lying authority" defect WITH A MECHANISM: not random lying, but a stale/mismatched grant record. ⭐ VALIDATES THE REAL-READ DESIGN: the real read asks the FILESYSTEM "can I actually read?" (ground truth) instead of the OS "do I have authority?" (stale/lying answer) — cutting through the mismatch entirely. ⚠ POSSIBLE ADDED NEED: explicitly handle the mismatch state (OS says granted but real read fails) — may need a clean clear-and-re-request rather than assuming the toggle works. "Fixing a lingering issue" — the stale grant record is the lingering thing.

⭐⭐⭐ OBSERVED / CONFIRMED (Fred 08-15) — THE MISMATCH IS REAL, seen directly. On clear-data: the app's authority was REMOVED (app-side lost real access) BUT the system setting still showed "all files: YES" (OS-side stale record) → so the app NEVER GOT PROMPTED. The app checked isExternalStorageManager() → OS returned the stale "yes" → prompt suppressed → app proceeded AS IF it had authority it didn't actually have → destructive-proceed → orphaned/overwrote the real data. THE ENTIRE BUG IN ONE SENTENCE: clear-data wipes the app's real access but leaves the OS record saying "yes," the app trusts the record (the lie), skips the prompt, and destroys data. ⭐ THIS IS EXACTLY WHY THE REAL-READ GATE IS THE CURE: OS says "yes" (stale) and isExternalStorageManager() returns true (trusts it), but a REAL READ would FAIL (actual access was removed on clear-data). Old code trusts "yes" → no prompt → destroys. New gate ignores "yes," does the real read → read FAILS → mismatch DETECTED → halt + force proper re-grant → real read passes → THEN proceed, data intact. The real read is the ONLY check that reflects actual access vs the stale record — it catches the exact case (stale-yes + no-prompt + gone-access) that the old code is blind to.

⭐ MECHANISM THEORY (Fred 08-15, plausible — exact internals NOT confirmed): the grant RECORD and the actual ACCESS live in DIFFERENT places; clear-data updated one but not the other, because the version that SET the grant predated/was-unaware-of the clear-out behavior. Maps to Android's real model: MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE is tracked in AppOps (system special-op record), separate from app data and separate from filesystem enforcement. Clear-data clears app data but the AppOps grant can persist / go inconsistent — especially across the target-SDK churn (36→34→35), since different targets interpret MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE differently, so a grant RECORDED under one target's rules is ENFORCED differently under another → "record says yes, real access gone." ⚠ EXACT term/location ("manifest attached to the object") NOT verified — could be AppOps, a package-manager flag, or MediaProvider state; the precise internals are version-specific and unconfirmed. ⭐ KEY POINT — THE FIX DOESN'T DEPEND ON KNOWING: the real-read test consults NONE of these records; it asks the filesystem "can I actually read?" (ground truth). So the fix is CORRECT whether or not the exact mechanism theory is right — it bypasses the stale record entirely rather than reverse-engineering where it lives. Diagnosis precision matters less than fix robustness here.

⭐ POSSIBLE ADDED REQUIREMENT (Fred 08-15) — FORCE-OFF-THEN-GRANT for the mismatch state. If the mismatch persists (OS record "yes" + real access gone → toggle greyed = "can't grant what you already have"), then granting properly may require first getting authority into a clean OFF state so it CAN be granted. Reset the stale "yes" before re-requesting. ⚠ ENGINEERING REALITY: an app CANNOT revoke its own MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE in code (no self-revoke API) — so the gate can't force-off itself. Options: (1) guide the USER to settings to toggle OFF then back ON (two-step, clunky, but resets the stale record); (2) the app's all-files settings page may itself clear stale state on off→on; (3) for TESTING only (not users): adb shell appops set com.grouptrack.android MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE default resets the AppOps record — useful to reproduce clean-slate on Droid 2. So mismatch handling = detect (real-read fails + OS says yes) AND drive-the-reset (user-guided force-off-then-grant, since app can't self-revoke). ⚠ SCOPE: this is DESIGN-IF-THE-TEST-SHOWS-IT-NEEDED, not built speculatively. Current gate does real-read detection. IF the Droid 2 test shows "real read fails + OS yes + plain re-grant doesn't fix (toggle greyed)" → THEN add force-off-then-grant. The test tells us whether this branch is required (and whether the mismatch even persists on API 36).

⚠ UX CONSTRAINT on force-off (Fred 08-15): since the app can't self-revoke, force-off-then-grant means prompting the user TWICE (off, then on). CONTROLLED PANELS = manageable — wrap the 2-step reset in OUR OWN gate screens as one guided/explained flow ("Step 1 toggle off, Step 2 toggle on"), double-prompt hidden inside a coherent experience. UNCONTROLLED (raw Android settings twice) = HARD TO EXPLAIN — "revoke then re-grant" reads like a bug, not a fix, especially for backcountry OHV users who aren't power users. So IF the double-prompt is unavoidable, it MUST be behind controlled panels. ⭐ PRIORITY ORDER (avoid the awkward thing): (1) hope the test shows API 36 clear-data leaves a CLEAN state (no stale yes → no mismatch → no reset → no double-prompt); (2) if mismatch exists, hope a PLAIN re-grant clears it (single prompt); (3) ONLY if neither → build the controlled-panel double-prompt flow. Don't build it unless the test proves it's unavoidable.

⭐ WORST-CASE FALLBACK DESIGN (Fred 08-15) — GATED EXPLAINER SCREEN. IF the test fails (mismatch persists) AND we can't control the OS panels → build a gated explainer screen that BRACKETS the two uncontrolled OS trips and makes them comprehensible. Flow: [OUR explainer screen: explains the OS error in plain terms ("Android has your permission in an inconsistent state") + explains the TWO screens coming ("you'll see system settings twice — first turn access OFF, then we bring you back to turn it ON; this clears the stuck state")] → [OS settings: turn OFF — uncontrolled but now EXPECTED] → [OUR bridge screen: "now turn it back on"] → [OS settings: turn ON — uncontrolled but expected] → [real read passes → proceed]. KEY: our screens bracket the raw OS trips so we control the NARRATIVE even without controlling the panels — the user is never dropped into raw settings without our explanation first, so "revoke then re-grant" reads as a guided repair, not a bug. This is the worst-case path only (priority order above still applies: prefer no-mismatch, then plain-regrant, THEN this).

⭐⭐ THE DEFINITIVE TEST (Fred 08-15) — CLEAR-DATA / REINSTALL REPRO. The exact sequence that caused the data loss: clear app data → relaunch → first-run/reinstall authority prompts fire (the juvenile flow) → those prompts are when MBTiles/DB DELETES executed. MECHANISM (Fred): after clear-data the app's storage-authority context is effectively FRESH ("new app id" sense) — juvenile flow re-runs, app CAN'T SEE the existing files (real authority not established), treats them as ABSENT, builds NEW empty files it DOES have authority to → the good data (89K trails, MBTiles) is orphaned/overwritten. = the "create-if-missing over good data" destructive-proceed. THE TEST: (1) on SACRIFICIAL Droid 2 (serial 24039703201775) with real map data — NEVER Droid 1 (8624SBCEDF00001789, protected field device); back up test data first. (2) clear app data. (3) OLD code → juvenile flow → destructive overwrite (reproduces bug). (4) NEW gate → clear data → gate runs → REAL-READ FAILS (no authority yet) → gate HALTS, touches nothing → user grants via correct flow → real read passes → THEN app proceeds and finds existing files INTACT. PASS CRITERION: after clear-data + re-grant through the new gate, the existing MBTiles/spatial DB are STILL THERE — not overwritten, not orphaned. The safe-halt prevented the destructive rebuild. ⚠ this test DESTROYS data if the gate fails → Droid 2 only, backup first, never Droid 1.

🐛 00-BUG-RADIOGPS — surfaced 08-14, INTERMITTENT (currently working 08-15, root cause NOT confirmed). Watch for recurrence.

⚠ STATUS 08-15: started working again on its own during testing — root cause was NOT identified/fixed, so this may be QUIET rather than FIXED. If it recurs, the diagnosis below still applies. The isolation test was solid (same radio, two phones, different behavior) so something real was happening — an intermittent handoff/timing issue is the likely character. On recurrence, capture the exact on-screen state of BOTH devices (has-Android-GPS vs no-Android-GPS) immediately, and note what changed right before it started working (reconnect? app restart? moved outside for sky view? nothing?).

Symptom (as diagnosed 08-14): Expected — radio connects → device name centered on map → GPS sourced FROM the radio. Actual — radio connects, device name shows in My Cart (BLE transport OK), but radio GPS not driving the map on a device that ALSO has internal Android GPS. Isolation: SAME radio on both devices ruled out radio config; the variable was whether the phone had its own internal GPS. Exact on-screen states of the two devices were never fully pinned before it self-resolved — capture them if it recurs.

What the symptom narrows it to: BLE is fine (My Cart shows the node). The break is in the POSITION-packet path: Meshtastic sends POSITION packets → app must receive → decode → feed position to map as active source → switch map source from Android-GPS to radio-GPS. Break is in one of: (1) the node itself has no GPS fix (no GPS hardware, or no sky view indoors — MOST LIKELY given it was tested indoors for the Play demo); (2) position packets arrive but app isn't parsing/subscribing; (3) packets parse but app never switches the map's location source from Android to radio.

⭐ FIRST DIAGNOSTIC (cheapest, do before touching code): confirm the Meshtastic NODE has an actual GPS fix — open the official Meshtastic app / node status, check for satellite lock. If the board has no GPS or no sky view, there's no position to send and this is NOT a code bug. Rule this out first. THEN if the node has a fix but the app still doesn't show it → logcat for POSITION packet receipt + the location-source switch (onRadioPosition / the source-selection logic).

⭐⭐ SHARPER DIAGNOSIS (Fred 08-14): device ID correctly shows the RADIO NAME (not Android name) — so the IDENTITY/device switch DID fire; the app knows a radio is connected. But GPS is still not processed from the radio. So there are TWO switches that should move together on radio-connect: (1) identity/device-name → WORKING, (2) GPS-source → NOT following. They're OUT OF SYNC — identity flipped to radio, but the position pipeline didn't switch from Android-GPS to the radio's position stream. Bug = "GPS source selection didn't follow device identity selection."

Two leads (Fred): (1) ⭐ STRONGEST — recent changes to "recognizing the Android default" GPS may have broken the HANDOFF: when a radio connects, the code that switches AWAY from Android GPS TO radio GPS was likely affected, severing "radio connected → use radio position" while leaving the identity switch intact. Look at what changed in Android-default recognition + whether it touched the source-selection branch. (2) SEED GPS SOURCE AT STARTUP — more robust pattern: DERIVE the GPS source from connection state ("is a radio connected?") rather than relying on an event-driven flag that can fail to fire / drift out of sync with identity. Echoes STATUS-IS-DERIVED: source should be derived from connection state, not a held flag.

⭐⭐⭐ CONFIRMED 08-14 by isolation test: a SECOND Android with NO internal GPS, connected to the radio → RADIO GPS WORKS. So the radio-GPS ingestion path is fully functional; the app CAN receive/process Meshtastic GPS. ⭐ PRECISE BUG (Fred): on connection the app REPLACES THE DEVICE NAME (identity) with the radio but does NOT replace the GPS SOURCE. Two separate actions at connect; only the name one fires. Identity flips to radio, position pipeline stays on Android GPS. Device WITH Android GPS → name shows radio but position stays Android (source never replaced) → looks like "radio GPS not working." Device WITHOUT Android GPS → no Android source to stay on → radio GPS used → works. FIX: at the connection handler, replace the GPS SOURCE at the same point the device NAME is replaced (they must move together) — or better, DERIVE the source from connection state ("radio connected? → radio is the source") so it can't be forgotten (STATUS-IS-DERIVED). Find the connect handler that swaps the name and add/repair the source swap beside it.

⭐ ISOLATION COMPLETE (Fred 08-14): SAME radio used on BOTH devices → rules out ALL radio config/firmware/GPS-settings issues (the node is proven good — Device B receives its position fine). The ONLY variable is the phone, specifically whether it has an internal GPS to compete. Fault is 100% app-side in the source-selection/replacement logic, and only manifests when an Android GPS source EXISTS to not-be-replaced. Radio exonerated, ingestion path exonerated. Bug fully located: connect handler replaces NAME but leaves existing Android GPS source in place instead of swapping to radio.

⚠ Does NOT block the Play video — the all-files-access video only needs offline map tiles panning at z18, no radio. ⛔ ALL-FILES-ACCESS (MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE) — Play review justification. HIGHEST rejection risk.

TRIPWIRE RULES: NEVER write on the form — "performance/faster/slow/speed/mph", "convenience/easier", or "forked Meshtastic/inherited/don't control install" (all read as "you don't really need it" or hit the explicit "performance is not acceptable" rejection). ALWAYS frame as: structural/technical INCOMPATIBILITY of the access model (the alternative CANNOT do it) + user-data protection (maps survive uninstall/data-clear) + "accesses only its own map/trail data."

FIELD 1 (feature, ≤500): "GroupTrack is an offline navigation app for backcountry areas with no cellular service. Users download 50–60 GB of satellite and topographic map tiles into a shared-storage map database for offline use. All files access lets the app read and manage this large user-owned map database in shared storage so it persists across app reinstalls and data-clears, ensuring downloaded maps are not lost when connectivity to re-download them is unavailable."

FIELD 2 (usage checkboxes): Core functionality ONLY.

FIELD 3 (technical reason, ≤500): "The map-rendering engine opens map tiles directly by file path from a shared tile database as the user navigates. MediaStore manages only gallery media (images, audio, video), not a path-addressed tile database, and cannot provide the coordinate-based file access the renderer requires. SAF returns document URIs, not file paths, and cannot provide the direct file-path access the rendering and database layer depends on. The scoped alternatives are structurally incompatible with a path-based tile database."

FIELD 4 (video, https:// unlisted YouTube): airplane mode ON → open map → pan/zoom to z18 showing offline tiles loading from local storage. 20–30s. Caption: "Offline map tiles loading by file path from shared storage — no network."

⭐ THE REAL (internal-only) REASON, NOT for the form: at 30–60 mph the map renders hundreds of thousands of large PNG tiles at z18 by direct file path; MediaStore/SAF cannot serve that access model (this is feasibility, but stated as "performance" it auto-rejects — so the FORM says access-model incompatibility). FORK ORIGIN (also internal-only): the app is a Meshtastic fork; the inherited install/permission process has no provision for shared-storage map data — this is the architectural root of the install-authority defect (item 7). item 7's fix guarantees exactly the "survives reinstall" behavior this Play justification promises Google.

Observability — DECISION 08-14: SURVEY, not telemetry. (telemetry rejected)

The gap: Play Console shows only AGGREGATE install/version data, never per-person ("which of my testers, on what version, active when"). Fred wants that visibility.

⛔ REJECTED — launch telemetry (app phones home {userID, versionCode, timestamp} to RDS). Reason (Fred): it would require TRACKING USERS — dragging in Data-Safety declarations, privacy-policy coverage, deletion-request handling, data security, and Google privacy scrutiny. Too much compliance overhead for a free app just to satisfy curiosity about usage.

✅ CHOSEN — SURVEY the testers directly (a quick form: what version are you on? using it? issues?). Gives the observability with ZERO data-collection burden, zero Google privacy exposure, zero backend work — testers volunteer the info, nobody is silently tracked. Cleaner ethically, legally, operationally. Play's aggregate Statistics (active devices by version) covers the rest once the closed test goes live.

2.7 architecture question (raised 08-14 by the Play review): can we DROP all-files access?

Google steers hard toward scoped storage; all-files is the most-rejected permission and a single point of failure for the whole Play presence. Evaluate in 2.7: can the spatial DB + tile store migrate to a scoped shared directory (SAF tree / app-owned shared location) to eliminate MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE? ⚠ Blocker = the data layer opens tiles/DB by ABSOLUTE FILE PATH (SQLite/MBTiles), and SAF gives document URIs not paths — so this is a data-layer rewrite, not a config change. Middle path: request all-files but FALL BACK to a SAF-scoped folder if denied. Ties to the 2.7 rewrite + the install-authority redesign.

🔧 00-TOOLING — the research indexes, and why they were lying

⛔⛔ where_used_raw.txt generated 24 KB against a 481 KB function universe for days, and reported success every run.

Cause: its step greps a HARDCODED list of about sixty names from the radio and ride era. Nothing from the map work was ever added. Proof: a grep of the generated index found ZERO occurrences of four current map symbols that have plenty of call sites in the source.

⚠ It cost real time. Hunting the route-mode reset, the index that should have listed every writer returned nothing — not because those symbols are unused, but because they were never on the list. An empty answer read as an authoritative one.

⭐ FIXED, and the fixed index is what solved it: the search set is DERIVED from function_universe_raw.txt. 24 KB → 2,099,813 bytes, and one grep then eliminated every Kotlin path and left only the JS.

⚠⚠ THE FIX WAS THEN LOST when the recommit script was restored from a backup — last night’s run reported 254 call sites. Use gen_xrefs.sh for research (all four indexes, no git, nothing that can stall), or re-apply the fix.

⭐ The recommit script itself is fine. An uninterrupted run reached DONE and pushed both commits. The earlier "crashes" were interrupts — the cross-reference runs after the commit and push, so a cancelled pass leaves it stale with no warning. recommit_docs_v15.sh is committed but never run: it moves the cross-reference first, carries the where-used fix, and adds freshness assertions. ⚠ Its git section is untested.

Standing rule, reinforced: an index that returns nothing is not evidence of absence until you have confirmed the index covers the thing you asked about.

⛔ Two defects found by reading the code, not by guessing

THE ZOOMSLOT CAP HAD NEVER FIRED. corridorTiles computed zTop from the slot cap and LOGGED it, then passed zMax to both stampPoint calls. Only stepDeg used the capped value — so topo corridor downloads had been stamping z17/z18 all along while the log claimed z16. ⚠ That made download and delete agree by accident; they now disagree deliberately, which is the safe direction.

THE FUNNEL ASKED FOR TILES THAT CANNOT EXIST. HTTP 400 … x=-1&y=4&z=4. At z4 the world is sixteen tiles wide and the buffer is ninety degrees of longitude, so lon - buffer ran off the west edge. ⭐ That is why four unrelated tracks all reported failed=20 — at those zooms the buffer dominates the track, so every Utah track produced the same impossible URLs. Identical counts across unrelated tracks was the tell. No coverage was ever missing.

▶▶ 00-DISPLAYRULES — MY CART / MY GROUP / PLANNER. Build-ready spec.

The promise the whole design serves (Fred): "while you're riding you never lose your spot on the map. The cart and MY GROUP always snap you back to proper zoom for that view, but your cart never leaves the screen."

Everything follows from that sentence: auto-centering keeps you on the map · auto-zoom keeps the group framed · the taper guarantees there is imagery at whatever zoom that lands on. Three rules, one promise.

The three modes

ModeCentreZoomManual pan
MY CARTyour own GPSSTICKY — rider sets it, it staysdisabled
MY GROUPfit to all cartsDERIVED — the fit sets it, snaps backdisabled
PLANNERfreefreeenabled — different job

Both convoy modes: manual pan DISABLED · auto-centre ALWAYS ON · zoom NEVER cancels auto-centre. ⚠ This reverses the old behaviour, where panning or zooming turned auto-centering off.

⚠ 08-12 item 0 gates the PLANNER behind a connection until its offline tiles are fixed.

⛔ Problem 1 — jitter

Fitting on every GPS tick flickers the zoom continuously as carts drift back and forth across a threshold. It needs asymmetric hysteresis:

  • Zoom OUT immediately when a cart leaves the frame — a safety response, no delay.
  • Zoom IN lazily, only after the group has stayed inside a tighter inner margin for several seconds.

⭐ Deliberately asymmetric: losing sight of someone is worse than a slightly-too-wide view.

⛔ Problem 2 — the stale cart

One rider drives home and the fit drags everyone else's map out to include them. At forty miles apart the auto-zoom lands near z10 and nobody can see the trail.

⭐ The fit must EXCLUDE carts with no position update in N minutes — and SAY SO. "2 carts not reporting" on the panel, rather than silently dropping them from the frame.

⭐ How the taper ties in

Whatever zoom the fit lands on, tiles exist. That is precisely what H=4 across z9-z18 guarantees — and it is why H must cover the worst-case screen rather than a typical one.

The fit chooses the zoom; the taper makes sure the choice is always covered.

⭐ Full design, math and device verification: living master 00-STARTHERE item 3.

3 — TAPER THE CORRIDOR, then refresh all corridors as the test

⭐⭐ THE REQUIREMENT IS GROUP TRACKING, NOT "LOOKING AHEAD." Fred: "when you are tracking the whole group in GroupTrack the first to the last cart may be 3 miles apart. The app auto-zooms to keep the group in frame but the tiles disappear. That is the behaviour we are trying to correct."

Group spreadAuto-zoom lands onView widthScreen white today
1 milez161.3 mi22%
2 milesz152.6 mi61%
3 milesz145.1 mi80%
5 milesz1310.2 mi90%

⭐ That table is why z16 is the last good level. A tight group sits at z16, where the half-mile band still fills the screen. The moment the group strings out, the app’s own auto-fit walks off the edge of the coverage.

⭐⭐ AND THE ANSWER IS THE SAME AT EVERY ZOOM: H ≈ 2.1 TILES. That is not tuning — it falls out of the arithmetic. Filling the screen needs half a screen-width either side, and a screen is always ~4.2 tiles. So the formula is self-calibrating.

buffer(z) = H × tileWidth(z)
tiles(z)  = (trackLength / tileWidth(z) + 1) × (2H + 1)

⭐⭐⭐ AND THE TAPER MUST CONTINUE UPWARD PAST z16 TOO — THIS INVERTS THE COST. Fred: "less time at z17 and z18 because the view is tighter — worth doing, not always a minimum of half a mile without regard to zoom."

Applying the same rule at z17/z18 makes the whole thing CHEAPER than today:

RuleTotalvs today (10,791)
H=2 at every zoom5,560−48%
H=3 at every zoom7,784−28%
H=4 at every zoom10,008−7%
H=3 with a 0.25 mi floor8,096−25%

⭐ The saving is nearly all z18: 7,807 → 3,868 tiles, because 0.23 miles either side is ALREADY three tiles of map at that zoom. Every level below z16 added together costs 546. The z18 saving pays for the entire taper five times over.

⭐ Fred: "drops size of tiles sent AND increases coverage to full panels at each zoom." A filled screen at every level from z9 to z18, and a 28% smaller download.

⚠ USE THE 0.25 mi FLOOR — at z18 H=3 is only 0.23 mi either side, which is tight if a recorded track wanders or GPS was loose. The floor costs almost nothing and keeps it at −25%.

⚠ corridorTiles already loops per zoom with bufferDeg as a parameter, so this changes WHAT THE BUFFER IS, not the structure. ⚠ Existing stores keep their shape — only new downloads taper, which is why refreshing all corridors is the test.

⚠ Size the taper per track from getTrackBbox, not from a constant — the figures above assume a 25×25 mile ride; a 60-mile-wide one is roughly 5× at z14.

4 — Fix offline planner tile access

⚠⚠ FIRST, WIDEN THE OFFTRACE FILTER — DO NOT SKIP THIS. It currently matches only URLs containing "tile" or starting with convoy://. A Google URL — mt0.google.com/vt/lyrs=y&x=… — matches NEITHER.

So "zero planner lines" may mean "requests I did not log", not "no requests". That single gap could be the whole of issue 1, and every conclusion drawn from the zero result is provisional until the filter is widened and the run repeated.

⚠ Then decide the design question: should the planner serve offline tiles at all? Fred has settled that it must — "it does not work offline" is a defect, and the online-only reading explains why nobody CAUGHT it, not that it is acceptable.

⭐ If it should, mirror the working branch at ConvoyScreen:757 rather than writing a second implementation. And note the structural finding: ConvoyMapViewerScreen.shouldInterceptRequest hardcodes two Esri overlay URLs and reads two stores that are 24,576 bytes — empty schemas with no tiles.

Eight hours on blank screens with no cause found until late. Fred: "we blamed and hypothesised for 8 hours."

⛔⛔ SIX MECHANISMS WERE PROPOSED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS INSTRUMENTED — storage · the databases · the zoom cap · device state · the planner’s missing interceptor · my own HTML edit. All six died. The trace took twenty minutes to write and answered more than the whole day of theorising.

⭐⭐ INSTRUMENT ON THE FIRST BLANK SCREEN, NOT THE EIGHTH. This is the 07-30 lesson — connect the channel before theorising about what you cannot see — ignored for a second time, at far greater cost.

✅ STORAGE IS PROVEN FINE — the hard negative result

Tiles at EVERY zoom. ⭐ And the %-full column is a corridor’s own signature — 100% at z10 falling to 20% at z18 as tiles shrink below the band width. So a blank map is RETRIEVAL or DISPLAY. Never storage. This also kills the one-zoom-layer theory for good.

✅ CONVOY OFFLINE WORKS — and the "missing" corners are real corridor shape

REQ convoy://tiles/SAT/13/1513/3197.png
MATCHED convoy://
readTile type=SAT z=13 x=1513 y=3197

The full chain fires on Utah ground. ⭐⭐ The blank corners are NOT a fault. The track runs upper-left to lower-right; the empty corners are upper-right and lower-left — the ground furthest from the diagonal, which a half-mile band never covered. At z16 the screen sits inside the band and fills; zoom out and the view widens past it.

🔴 ISSUE 1 — THE PLANNER MAKES NO TILE REQUESTS AT ALL

Zero OFFTRACE lines from the planner, confirmed twice — including with today’s HTML change reverted, so today’s work is ELIMINATED as the cause.

⭐⭐ FRED’S READING, AND IT IS PROBABLY RIGHT: THE PLANNER MAY NEVER HAVE BEEN BUILT TO DISPLAY LOCAL TILES. "Planner by definition requires an internet connection. Convoy uses device GPS to position for the ride. No other activity." He is only hitting this because he is in New Hampshire, using the planner to navigate quickly to Utah ground via persistence — probably the first offline test of the planner ever.

⚠ The code is consistent with that. ConvoyMapViewerScreen.shouldInterceptRequest (976-1005) matches ONLY two hardcoded Esri overlay URLs — World_Transportation and World_Boundaries_and_Places — then returns super. There is no convoy:// branch. ⚠ And both of those stores are 24,576 bytes: an empty schema with no tiles.

⭐ Fred: "hardcoding means something is fundamentally wrong and the quick repair has exceeded its life expectancy." The offline path should resolve source → cache_dir → store from map_sources.json, which already carries cache_dir per layer. Two hardcoded Esri URLs bypass the entire configuration system that exists to make sources interchangeable.

⚠⚠ BUT DO NOT CONCLUDE FROM THE ZERO LINES YET. The trace filter only matches URLs containing "tile" or starting with convoy://. A Google URL — mt0.google.com/vt/lyrs=y&x=… — matches NEITHER. So "no lines" may mean "requests I did not log". Widen the filter and re-run before deciding anything.

🔴 ISSUE 2 — THE HORIZON. Not a bug; a design gap with a 4% fix.

At z16 the screen fills. At z15 and z14 the corners go white.

⚠ EACH ZOOM IS AN INDEPENDENT TILE SET. Having z18 gives you nothing at z13 — they are separate downloads of the same ground at different resolutions, and the corridor fetches all of them, but only for the band. "Less detail" does not mean "less data": it means MORE GROUND PER TILE, so a wider view, so more of it outside your coverage.

THE FIX FRED ARRIVED AT — a horizon measured in TILES, not miles:

buffer(z) = H × tileWidth(z)
tiles(z)  = (trackLength / tileWidth(z) + 1) × (2H + 1)

H = 2  ->  always two tiles of map beyond the track, at every zoom
ZoomBuffer at H=2Tiles
z978.0 mi10horizon
z1039.0 mi16horizon
z129.8 mi48horizon
z142.4 mi177horizon
z151.2 mi350horizon
z16-18½ mi (unchanged)595 · 2,094 · 7,807detail

⭐⭐ TOTAL 11,214 AGAINST 10,791 TODAY — FOUR PERCENT, and it fills the view all the way down to z9.

⭐ Why it is nearly free: the count is dominated by trackLength / tileWidth, which grows 4× per level; the buffer is only a constant multiplier (2H+1). So widening the horizon multiplies the CHEAP levels and leaves z18 untouched. z9 at seventy-eight miles of buffer costs TEN TILES because one z9 tile is 156 km wide.

⚠ corridorTiles already loops per zoom with bufferDeg as a parameter — the buffer just becomes a function of z. ⚠ Existing stores keep their shape; only new downloads get the horizon, so Bar 10 would need re-downloading to fill in (444 tiles, seconds).

⛔ THE OOM — and the last link in root cause 1 was mine

The first Recreate job killed the app: 512 MB, OutOfMemory.

⛔ I recorded "the worker was never the problem" because it filters to hasTile. It does — but only AFTER calculateTiles materialises the WHOLE RECTANGLE. A Recreate box holds at most 50,000 STORED tiles but can span degrees: at z18 a three-degree box is roughly 4.8 million TileKey objects allocated before one is filtered.

⭐ Fixed e128cfc65 — MBTilesStore.tilesInBounds() asks the store what it holds. Bounded by what is stored rather than by the size of the box, and faster: one indexed range scan per level instead of millions of allocations plus a per-tile existence check. Fred’s original sentence, finally built: stream real tiles in existence by zoom for x lon and lat.

⚠ The old code even LOGGED the ratio — "N in bounds, M exist on disk". The waste was visible all along and only mattered once a box got big enough to exhaust the heap.

🔵 00-RECOVERYFILE — the .gtmaps recipe. QUEUED AFTER THE INSTALL REVISIONS.

⚠ This started as Recreate and sprawled into DR + reseeding a next device + sharing with a friend before Fred pulled it back — his scope-creep rule firing: "we can complicate everything ... gotta decide what we want to be."

⭐ DECISION: DR is the purpose. Seeding and sharing fall out FREE because it is a plain file in Downloads. They are not designed for, and nothing in the build is shaped around them.

⚠ Why it is needed at all: 08-11 established that a fresh install destroys map data in a way that cannot be prevented or recovered from. The recipe does not save the tiles — it makes losing them a download instead of a disaster.

The file

JSON, extension .gtmaps, written to Downloads. All slots in one file — a DR file capturing only SAT would be a bad recipe on the day it is needed.

grouptrack_maps_2026-08-11.gtmaps

{ "version":1, "created":"2026-08-11T19:30Z",
  "slots":[ { "slot":"SAT", "source":"google-hybrid",
              "zMin":4, "zMax":18, "tiles":625386,
              "boxes":[ {"n":37.9,"s":37.6,"e":-112.1,"w":-112.4,"tiles":48213} ] } ] }

A few KB. The boxes are exactly what enqueueRecreateSource already computes — the export is that same quadtree with a different sink.

Cadence — and why history removes a problem rather than adding one

Written on startup, once per day (same date overwrites), on a background thread. Delete anything older than 60 days.

⭐ KEEPING HISTORY IS WHAT KILLS THE PARTIAL-SNAPSHOT PROBLEM. If today’s run happens mid-rebuild and captures half the coverage, yesterday’s file is still there. So there is no queue-state check, no migration check, no "is the store healthy" guard, and no skip logic. Just write it.

⚠ The date in the filename IS the index. No prefs, no manifest-of-manifests, nothing to keep in sync — and prefs are wiped by exactly the events this protects against.

⭐ Cost is nothing: ~40 boxes across three slots is a few KB, so 60 days is under 250 KB. The scan is a few hundred indexed COUNTs with no blobs touched — a second or two.

The Recovery section — downloads panel, map settings

Lists the .gtmaps files by date, with tile counts. Select one → REBUILD or EMAIL.

⭐ The tile count is the only judgement left, and it is a human glance rather than logic: a file reading 180,000 next to yesterday’s 625,386 is visibly a mid-rebuild capture, so you pick the other one.

⚠ NO slot selection at rebuild. Fred: "the selection is just another failure point. treat as update in place." INSERT OR REPLACE means a rebuild over a partly intact store simply fills the gaps — nothing to half-apply, and running it twice costs nothing.

⛔⛔ THE ONE THING THAT MUST BE RIGHT — the replay job type

downloadType = DownloadType.AREA          // so the worker actually fetches
priority     = DownloadPriority.REFRESH   // lowest, yields to everything

These are SEPARATE FIELDS on QueueEntry, and DownloadPriority.forType() is only a default helper — nothing forces them to match.

⛔ Type must be AREA because ConvoyDownloadWorker:97 filters MAP_SOURCE_REFRESH jobs to hasTile. Replay a refresh-typed job onto a rebuilt store and it downloads NOTHING — every job completes at zero tiles and looks successful. That is the silent-failure class, and it would only be discovered during an actual recovery.

⭐ Refresh means replace what I have; replay means fetch what I had. Same boxes, opposite intent.

⚠ Put a comment at that line explaining why they differ, or someone will later "fix" the mismatch and reintroduce it.

Import, email, and one thing to verify

⭐ Import is the PICKER / content-URI path — the same mechanism .convoy ride files already use. The URI grants read access, so it needs no All-Files authority: the one path that has never failed all week.

⚠ A file arriving by email is owned by the mail app, so it must be LAUNCHED, not scanned for. The app can only scan for files it wrote itself.

⚠ Email out is ACTION_SEND with the file attached. May need a FileProvider entry — check whether the existing ride transfer already declares one before adding manifest work.

⚠ VERIFY ONCE, DO NOT ASSUME: does a file the app writes to Downloads survive an uninstall? Shared storage generally does, but MediaStore tracks the owning app — and DR must not rest on an untested assumption. Write one, uninstall, look.

⚠ TILES ONLY. Tracks, trails, routes and waypoints are captured data and need real backup. This recipe cannot reproduce them.

🔴🔴 00-ROOTCAUSE (08-11) — THE THREE ROOT CAUSES. FIX THESE, NOT THE SYMPTOMS.

Fred named these after the 08-11 Droid 1 collapse. Everything else that day was downstream of one of them.

1 — AREA DOWNLOAD REQUESTS GROUND THAT HAS NO TILES

Fred: "download area must stream real tiles in existence by zoom for x lon and lat."

The area path derives a bbox and calculates the whole rectangle. The existence check happens later, per tile, inside the worker — so the QUEUE fills with a continent of work, most of which is then skipped one tile at a time. Droid 1: 3,287 jobs, ETA 194 days.

⚠ Why it went continental: the scope comes from the store’s EXTENT. With Utah coverage and any stray tile near New Hampshire, that extent spans coast to coast and the refresh covers all of it. ⛔ And it is not just a Fred problem — any rider who travels between download regions gets the same result.

⭐ THE FIX: scan what actually EXISTS and refresh only that. Query the store for stored tiles, group them into jobs, download those. A cell with no tiles never enters a job, so disjoint regions stay disjoint and corridors stay corridors — by construction, not by inference.

⚠ z16 is the vetting level (Fred): ~490 m, roughly a third of corridor width, so a skinny track is traced rather than smeared. z12 (~7.7 km) would drag in ground never downloaded.

⚠ PAYLOAD: a job cannot carry hundreds of thousands of tile keys in inputData. It must carry a re-queryable range and let the worker re-derive at run time — the same constraint that shaped corridor jobs.

⭐⭐ THIS RETIRES THE MANDATORY DELETE IN OPTION 2. The delete existed because the area pass inferred scope from a bbox and boxed corridor ground. Tile-for-tile refresh has no area pass — nothing infers shape, so corridor geometry cannot be lost. Coverage never drops either, because INSERT OR REPLACE overwrites in place. Fred: "we do not need a delete ... this function should be refresh a map source, all tiles." ⚠ Option 1 (clear and start over) stays for people who want it.

2 — CANCELLING 3,287 QUEUED JOBS ANR’d THE APP

The cancel walks every entry — removing it from the list, cancelling its WorkManager id, and saving the queue file — on the main thread. Thousands of writes, UI blocked, ANR.

⭐ CANCEL WORKERS may be gentler than CANCEL ALL, since it stops workers rather than walking entries. ⚠ The real fix is moving the walk off the main thread and batching the saves.

⭐ Related, already queued: HIDE completed and cancelled entries on hold-and-cancel — the tester’s panel was too congested to find live activity. Hide, NOT remove: the estimator and the yardstick both compute from completed rows.

3 — INSTALL IS STILL HAMMERING EXISTING FILES

This is 00-REFUSE — refuse-to-create — still unbuilt after TWO data-loss events.

A fresh install runs setup, believes it has All-Files authority when it does not, cannot see Documents/GroupTrack/, reads that as a new device, and writes empty schemas over real data.

⚠ It blocked a safe recovery action THREE TIMES on 08-11 alone. Every time an uninstall and reinstall was the obvious move, this was the reason not to. ⭐ Highest-value unbuilt item in the project.

⚠ AND THE 08-11 DROID 1 SYMPTOM WAS NEVER DIAGNOSED

White basemap at every zoom, every source, ONLINE AND OFFLINE, while tracks and trails drew normally.

TheoryKilled by
Storage full81 GB free, 29% used
Databases corruptspatial 297 MB, data 30 MB — both intact
Today’s codeDroid 2 runs the SAME APK correctly
Device runtime statea reboot changed nothing
My zoom-cap editsetTileUrl diff was clean; both call sites pass the slot key

⚠ Fred also reported general bugginess — "same items different results, simple queries not working" — which is the signature of state, not logic. A code bug fails the same way every time.

⭐ Fred uninstalled, reinstalled and is rebuilding data. Whether the symptom returns on the rebuilt device is itself the next piece of evidence.

🔵 00-DEADCODE (2.7 PREREQUISITE) — DISCOVER AND REMOVE UNREFERENCED CODE

Fred 08-11: this is the FIRST 2.7 task, before the convoy rewrite. The sweep is not housekeeping — you cannot cleanly rewrite what you cannot tell is in use, and every dead path carried into the rewrite is a path that gets ported.

THE METHOD, in Fred’s order:

  1. Remove unused functionality from the PANELS first, and kill their launch points. A function reachable only from a menu row you have just deleted becomes detectable — before that it looks live.
  2. Clean the GroupTrack menu, submenus and the settings panel. That is what surfaces the next layer of orphans.
  3. Then use the xrefs to nominate unreferenced functions and remove them ONE AT A TIME, with a build between each, so the model always compiles and runs.

⭐ Use the cheap gate, not a full build: ./gradlew compileGoogleReleaseKotlin is ~2 minutes against ~12-30 for an assemble. That is what makes one-at-a-time affordable.

⭐ THE SCOPE SOUNDING — 2026-08-11

MeasureCount
Function declarations995
Unique function names894
Files containing functions123
Named in an xref165
Not named in either xref729

⛔⛔ 729 IS NOT A CANDIDATE LIST AND MUST NOT BE TREATED AS ONE. The run was captured while where_used_raw.txt was still generating — 24 KB and 254 lines against a 481 KB function universe, so it was very nearly empty. The comparison therefore reduced to "functions not mentioned in a click trace", which excludes every worker, bridge method, service and internal call by construction.

⚠ The proof is in the top files: SpatialDbManager at 91 and MBTilesStore at 18 are among the busiest code in the app. They appear unreached because navigation xref traces CLICKS, not data layers. Re-run the sounding once the xrefs finish.

THE SPLIT IS THE USEFUL PART:

CategoryCountReading
private file-local230 THE TRACTABLE SLICE. A private function’s callers are all in ONE file, so grep is decisive and the compiler proves the removal.
Top-level Composable-cased37panels and screens — check for orphaned UI
override13⛔ NOT candidates — lifecycle and worker entry points
Other (public surface)449cross-layer API; needs the finished where-used

⭐ 230 private functions is the number to act on first, and it is affordable.

⛔ WHAT WILL HIDE A LIVE CALLER FROM THE SWEEP

Your own environment note is the governing rule: "These are DEFINITION/symbol indexes, NOT a call graph — grep remains source-of-truth for CALL SITES."

⭐⭐ THE STANDING RULE: THE XREF NOMINATES, GREP CONFIRMS. WHEN THEY DISAGREE, GREP WINS AND THE FUNCTION STAYS. It is far cheaper to leave one orphan than to remove something the compiler cannot catch.

  • WorkManager workers — ConvoyCorridorWorker.doWork() is never clicked, and never will appear in a navigation trace.
  • @JavascriptInterface bridge methods — called from HTML, so a Kotlin grep shows ZERO callers on entirely live code.
  • Compose callbacks passed as parameters — onClick = ::doThing reads as a single reference.
  • String-named nav routes and reflection — ConvoyRouteCreateScreen is registered in nav BY NAME.
  • Lifecycle overrides — invoked by the framework.

⭐ ALREADY-CONFIRMED DEAD CODE — found incidentally, all looked live

Every one of these was discovered while doing something else, and every one appeared to be in use. That is the argument for the sweep.

  • Import Ride from Downloads (ConvoySubMenu.kt:131) — the callback onImportFromDownloads has NO caller; it falls back to a default {}, so the row already does nothing.
  • setMaxOfflineZoom / maxOfflineZoom — an INDICATOR, not a ceiling, fed by a scan for z-named DIRECTORIES: a layout MBTiles replaced. Likely reports nothing.
  • The migSteps renderer — inside an else if (false) block, so the auto_unreachable migration branch writes to state nothing displays.
  • var isZ16 = false — declared, never read (removed 08-10).
  • ConvoyRouteCreateScreen.kt — 27-line stub, registered in nav at ConvoyNavigation.kt:87, no navigate() caller.
  • Convoy __routeMode scaffolding · spatial_artifacts.js orphan · unreachable showDraftResolve block · var routeModeResetLogged · smoothFactor on trails (device-disproven).
  • getTrackBbox selects a name column it never reads — not dead, but the same class of residue.

⚠ And two whole paths that are dead in a subtler way: the AREA download onDownloadMaps callback stays on the panel SIGNATURE and both screens still pass it, but nothing renders it — deliberately, and the comment says so. calculateTiles has a sourceName parameter that NOTHING passes, so its zoom cap never fires.

▶ WHY THIS IS A 2.7 PREREQUISITE, NOT A CHORE

Fred 08-11: 2.7 is "the rewrite and taming of the convoy app, the removal of dead code and functions no longer used, combined with the rewrite of the convoy map functionality for group tracking and MY CART recording — done in isolation for the Android release, before injecting Apple Swift issues and requirements."

⭐ ONE CONSTRAINT WORTH CARRYING THROUGH THE REWRITE, AT NO COST: the cross-platform arc already landed on a service layer with logic-free panels. If convoy tracking logic lands in composables it must be redone for iPad; if it lands in a service layer it does not. Not a design for two platforms — just an awareness of which side of the line new code falls on. Free to observe, expensive to retrofit.

🟢 00-TODAY (08-10) — ROUTE CORRIDORS SHIPPED · ZOOM CAP · THE ROTATION BUG

Route corridors work end to end, verified on device. SAVE MAPS on a route’s artifact panel derives and queues a corridor, same pipeline as tracks.

getRoutePoints <hash>: 1 segment(s), 181 points
CORRIDOR queued: slot=TOPO tiles=1848 ... hash=4f771154...
CommitWhat
b9017aacashared WKT parser, getRoutePoints, getCorridorGeometry
4c71fecddgeometry + label resolve tracks then routes; label is now ONE INDEXED ROW instead of loading every track hash on every enqueue
7abeb1bfbcorridor bbox resolves both, SAVE MAPS renders for Routes

⭐⭐⭐ WHY IT COLLAPSED FROM A REFACTOR TO THREE SMALL COMMITS

CREATE TABLE routes (... geom_hash TEXT NOT NULL, UNIQUE(geom_hash))

geom_hash is CONTENT-ADDRESSED and UNIQUE in BOTH tables, and insertRoute already computes it. The same hash therefore means the same geometry wherever it is stored, so a corridor derived from either table is identical.

⭐ That removed all of it: no artifact-type parameter, no new QueueEntry field, no inputData change, no worker change, and no serialisation risk to a queue already persisted on devices.

⚠ The 07-27 note in section 17 had this right all along — "the blocker dissolved on inspection: the routes table already carries geom_hash." The 08-06 note claiming otherwise was WRONG and drove a far larger design. ⭐⭐ CHECK THE SCHEMA BEFORE DESIGNING AROUND A DATA MODEL. One query would have saved the detour.

⚠ A FOURTH track-only lookup surfaced only on inspection: the SAVE MAPS lambda calls getTrackBbox BEFORE the pipeline is reached, so a route would have failed at the very first step and been told it had no geometry. ⚠ Two call sites, and each file ALSO has an onDownloadMaps (AREA) lambda with an identical bbox line that must stay tracks-only — a bare-line anchor would have patched the wrong one.

⛔ insertRoute DEFECT, real now that UNIQUE is confirmed: newId() → INSERT OR IGNORE → return id. On duplicate geometry nothing inserts and a fabricated id is returned. Blocks reliable GPX route import; the panel path does not need it. Fix: read back the surviving route_id by geom_hash.

⭐ ZOOM CAP — SAT z18, RENDERED z16, DOWNLOAD AND DISPLAY

490096e49 (ZOOMSLOT-2026-08-10D, corridor downloads) + 151febe07 (both HTML maps).

⭐ KEYED ON THE SLOT, NOT THE SOURCE. Every download path already carries the slot — enqueueCorridor takes slotName, the worker reads refresh_slot, the area path carries selectedSlots. None carry the source: corridorTiles had no source parameter at all. So the slot needs no new plumbing, and it publishes as one sentence a rider can understand instead of a table of vendor ceilings.

Fred’s economics: each level quadruples the tile count. Satellite is upscaled rather than sharper above z18 in open country; a topo map has said everything it has by z16. "most data in the least space."

⛔⛔ THE CAP IS OPT-IN AND MUST STAY THAT WAY. ConvoyCorridorDelete calls the same corridorTiles. A capped delete computes a SMALLER set than an older build stored, leaving z17-z18 tiles that nothing can ever find or remove on a multi-gigabyte store. Empty slot = no cap; only the two DOWNLOAD paths pass one, and the patch VERIFIES the delete path did not gain one.

⚠ STILL UNCAPPED: the AREA path — calculateTiles has a sourceName parameter that NOTHING passes, so its cap never fires; nine call sites would need the slot. And quickEstimate has no slot either, so the live preview overstates against a capped download.

⭐ Display uses maxNativeZoom, so zoom CONTINUES past the cap and upscales rather than stopping. That is the better behaviour: artifact overlays stay sharp at any zoom, so a rider can still place a waypoint precisely over a soft basemap. ⚠ Cost: pre-cap z17-18 topo tiles stop being drawn. They are not deleted and cost nothing; they age out at the next source change.

⚠ setMaxOfflineZoom/maxOfflineZoom is an INDICATOR, not a ceiling — its body writes a text element and never touches currentMaxNative. It is fed by a scan for z-named DIRECTORIES, a layout MBTiles replaced, so it likely reports nothing now. Separate cleanup.

⛔⛔ THE ROTATION BUG — A TESTER LOST A RUNNING MIGRATION

MainActivity declared NO configChanges, so Android destroyed and recreated it on rotation — taking the composable, its state, and the coroutine scope the corridor delete runs on. A tester rotated their phone mid-delete and the panel vanished with no way to tell whether the work was still running.

FIXED 15e1acebf (ROTATE-2026-08-10F): configChanges="orientation|screenSize|screenLayout|keyboardHidden". Fred: "just leave the screen open unless an action is taken to end it. period."

⭐ Chosen over rememberSaveable deliberately: saving state would rebuild the panel with the right numbers, but the coroutine would still have been cancelled — it would look alive while doing nothing, which is worse than a blank screen. Keeping the activity is what keeps the WORK.

⚠ NOT a full fix — a swipe-away or a kill still ends it. Moving the delete off the UI scope remains the real answer. ⚠ Watch for orientation-qualified resources no longer reloading.

⛔ WHAT THE TESTER'S RUN EXPOSED — TWO DEFECTS BEYOND THE ROTATION

1 — STRANDED: the delete completed, the submit never ran. Corridors removed, queue EMPTY, coverage gone, and no indication anything was wrong. The code already warns about this shape: "killing the process mid-run strands the user with removed coverage and nothing scheduled to rebuild it."

⭐ Recovery is a re-run — already-deleted corridors skip at ~0.4 s each, so it moves through and reaches the submit. ⚠ But the app cannot DETECT this state. A check on launch for "coverage removed, nothing scheduled" would turn a confusing hour into a prompt.

⛔ 2 — A SUCCESSFUL DELETE WAS RECORDED AS FAILED. The first pass logged failures; the second pass popped through in seconds, which is the signature of nothing left to delete. The tiles came off and the record said otherwise. The delete and recordCompletedDelete are not atomic, so a failure in the RECORD is indistinguishable from a failure to DELETE.

⚠ This is what made an hour of diagnosis necessary — and on a destructive operation it is worse than cosmetic: a user either re-runs needlessly, or believes their data survived when it did not.

⭐⭐ THE DELETE IS DEVICE-BOUND, NOT CODE-BOUND — SETTLED FOR GOOD

Samsung tablet: 99 tracks in 15 SECONDS. Droid 2: ~1.8 hours. Same build.

⛔ Every hypothesis chased on 08-09 was looking for a defect in code that runs hundreds of times faster on ordinary hardware. Seven died; the answer was that Droid 2 has slow storage.

⛔ CONSEQUENCE FOR THE DOCS: the panel copy saying "a few hours" is calibrated to the slowest device owned and will alarm everyone else. Say it depends on the device and how much is held, and let the progress meter carry the rest. No duration claim.

▶ NEXT — Fred, 08-10

  • LOGICALLY DELETE (HIDE) completed and cancelled queue entries on hold-and-cancel. The tester’s panel was too congested to find live activity. ⭐ HIDE, not remove — the estimator and the yardstick both compute from completed rows, which is also why CLEAR is destructive today. Same hidden flag already designed for the yardstick, defaulted false on read so existing queue files load unchanged.
  • GroupTrack menu / submenu cleanup — multiple items. ⚠ Import Ride from Downloads has NO caller: its callback falls back to a default {} and the row already does nothing.
  • Route mileage — column on route build, plus a running total during the draw.
  • Per-source zoom override — shipped default, user-editable.
  • insertRoute duplicate fix, then GPX route import (state still unknown), then route-create-on-save.
  • ⚠ The coverage overlay may draw BOXES rather than corridor footprints. If so, any conclusion drawn from LOOKING at the map is suspect — log-derived tile counts are unaffected.

🟢 00-TONIGHT (08-09 EOD) — A-01 PASSED · ETA VERIFIED · THE SINGLE-SOURCE FINDING

HEAD 4552b2934, tree clean. Full detail in grouptrack_handoff_2026-08-09_v2.html.

Verified on deviceEvidence
A-01 — the 08-08 data-loss defect is FIXED tracks=true areas=true, and now visible on screen as "Selected: track maps + areas"
The migration checklist RENDERS MIGRENDER-2026-08-09E — branch order, see below
The ETA fix 1,084,513 tiles ÷ 19.3/sec = 15.6 h → shown as 16h 0m. Estimate and displayed rate agree.

⛔⛔ THE SINGLE-SOURCE FINDING — retires the 08-08 concurrency baseline

19-21 tiles/sec sustained tonight against 62 last night. Same device, same source, same 4 slots.

Tonight all four jobs hit ONE host. Last night's 62 was three jobs across THREE sources (SAT / TOPO / TOPO+) — so most of that figure was parallelism across hosts, not concurrency against one.

⛔ 2 slots 26.2 / 3 slots 30.0 / 4 slots 33.2 WERE NEVER MEASURING WHAT THEY APPEARED TO. The 08-08 note flagged the confound and asked for exactly this test. Per-host throughput is the ceiling, not slot count.

⚠ A single-source migration is inherently slower than a mixed one — ~16 h for Fred's store, not the ~5 the old numbers implied. Nothing is broken. ⛔ The docs must not quote per-slot figures.

⚠ Unchecked: pacing vs rejection. grep -iE "429|403|rate limit|too many"

⭐⭐ THE MIGRATION CHECKLIST WAS UNREACHABLE — branch order, not any of its parts

if (...)                        PHASE 1  "Map Source Changed"
else if (!refreshEnqueued)      PHASE 2  the option panel        (~315)
else if (migRunning)            MigrationSteps checklist         (~783)

refreshEnqueued only flips true at the END of the run, so for the whole migration the option panel won the chain and the checklist could never draw. The user pressed CONTINUE, the screen did not change, and a multi-minute irreversible delete ran with no feedback — which is why CONTINUE got a second tap.

⚠ FOUR WRONG DIAGNOSES WERE ANNOUNCED BEFORE THE CHAIN ORDER WAS READ (IO-thread state write · migSteps/migLog mismatch · missing renderer · the gate itself). Every component was correct in isolation, which is exactly why checking them one at a time found nothing. Read the control flow before the parts.

FIX: } else if (!refreshEnqueued && !migRunning) { — one condition.

⭐⭐ THE ETA WAS TWO CALCULATIONS THAT DISAGREED BY 6x

The panel displayed 37.2 tiles/sec and "3 hours" for 69,000 tiles. 69,000 ÷ 37.2 is 31 minutes; a 3-hour ETA implies ~6.4 tiles/sec — a sixth of the number on the same screen.

estimateRemaining() ignored the displayed rate and computed its own from all tiles ever completed over elapsed-since-the-earliest-job. ⛔ Its firstStart block fell back to createdAt — QUEUE TIME — for completed entries, so hours of waiting before the first tile downloaded counted as elapsed. Its own comment says createdAt is queue time and that this is what made the estimates unusable. The code then used it.

⚠ It was asymmetric too: the denominator reached back to the first job QUEUED while the numerator only counted entries still present — so clearing completed rows shrank one side and not the other.

⭐ FIX (Fred): one rate. liveRate() extracted as the single definition; the display and the estimate divide by the same value. 22 lines deleted. If the panel says 37 tiles/sec and 69,000 remain, the estimate is 31 minutes — the arithmetic the user can do in their head.

⚠ STILL TO BUILD (Fred's spec): first sample at 5 minutes, recalc hourly from the last hour, tiles as the unit — so a rate step is followed rather than averaged away. ⚠ Tolerance: "an estimate of 6 days is not going to be managed to the minute. but it shouldn't go to 12 or 2."

THE CORRIDOR DELETE — SETTLED. NOT A DEFECT.

~95-100 tiles/sec, linear with tile count. 88 of 88 completed.

⛔⛔ SEVEN HYPOTHESES, ALL DEAD: missing transaction (already one, 500 rows/statement) · missing index (already exists) · O(n²) re-derivation (the preview did 88 tracks in 40 s) · concurrent deletes (one SQLite writer per file) · delete the union (re-deleting absent rows costs 0.4 s) · drop/rebuild the index (it is the only way to find rows) · the pragmas (applied and confirmed on device, NO EFFECT).

⭐⭐ THE ANSWER: IT IS NOT SLOW. Deletes run ~100 tiles/sec; downloads run 20-62. The delete is faster per tile than the download. And before MBTiles there was no removal option at all — testers factory-reset their devices. Two hours unattended replaced days of manual work or a wipe.

⚠ THE CONTROLLED COMPARISON, by accident: tracks deleted in the morning were re-visited at night and returned tiles=0 in 0.4 s. Bar 10 overnighter: 189 s with rows, 0.4 s without. Same corridor, same statements. The cost is the physical row removal, nothing else.

⭐⭐ THE REBUILD MANIFEST — the idea worth keeping (V3 / 2.7)

Fred: "save downloads in queue and flag source area or corr ... kind of a rebuild my map script" · "if you store it on Google Drive it can be used to seed another device" · "and a DR tool."

Completed download jobs, with scope and kind retained, ARE a manifest of how the store was built. Not a log — a recipe. Kilobytes, where the tiles are 14 GB.

  • DR — two loss events in nine days, both reconstructed by hand. ⚠ TILES only; tracks and trails are captured data needing real backup.
  • Device seeding — install, pull, rebuild. A seed, not a transfer.
  • Coverage sharing — a ride leader's whole store as a recipe. ⭐ The route-import goal generalised.
  • ⭐ It dissolves the delete ordering constraint — area scope currently must be inferred from surviving tiles, which is WHY corridors must be deleted first.

⚠ Pieces mostly exist (geomHash, bbox, slot, counts on completed entries). Missing: an explicit kind flag; and CLEAR destroys them. ⭐ The hide-not-delete change already designed for the yardstick serves both.

⛔ Not in download_queue.json — it quarantines to .corrupt and has boot-looped. A DR tool cannot share fate with what it protects.

⚠⚠ TILE-BY-TILE REFRESH WAS CONSIDERED AND REJECTED: a single job over 1.28M tiles is one unit with no restart point. Fred: "one two week job with no restart for failsafe. not buying it." ⭐ Corridors and areas are not just scope — they are the FAILURE BOUNDARIES. Chunking by count (~40k/job) would work, but the payload must be a re-queryable range, not a list.

⚠ MBTiles metadata carries name/format/scheme and nothing else — checked day one. No record of how any tile got there, which is the same gap behind coverage confidence. It is our file; rows can be added. ⛔ But a flag ON the tiles table would rewrite every row INCLUDING its ~23 KB blob — use a separate small classification table.

🔴 NEW REGRESSION — queue row spacing

~20 lines of vertical space between queue rows, only when the panel is expanded vertically. Introduced by tonight's ETA-ONERATE edit inside if (expanded). ⚠ "Only when expanded" points at fill/arrangement, not fixed padding. NOT DIAGNOSED. Diff against ConvoyDownloadQueuePanel.kt.bak_20260809_202249.

⚠ METHOD — 08-09

  • ⛔⛔ THE PATCH-GUARD LESSON REPEATED A FOURTH TIME. Patch ...09c.py aborted on its own verify because the safety comment QUOTED the journal modes it warned against. DESCRIBE, NEVER QUOTE — it applies to values a comment forbids, not just to code being replaced.
  • ⛔ execSQL REJECTS ANY STATEMENT THAT RETURNS A ROW. Two of three pragmas used it, the first threw, and the shared try took the other two down with it — so the pragma hypothesis went a whole build cycle untested. ⭐ Separate try per pragma, and log what SQLite actually reports back.
  • ⛔ A multi-line git commit -m chained with && ./gradlew SWALLOWED THE BUILD — Gradle printed its help banner and reported BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 14 s with no APK. Run builds on their own line.
  • ⚠ git add -A swept in two stray empty files (0L, e.createdAt) created by an unquoted > in a grep pattern. git status --short before add -A.
  • ⭐⭐ THE PATTERN OF THE WHOLE DAY: seven mechanisms proposed for the delete, four for the progress meter, six for the All-Files defect — and in every case the answer came from READING, not reasoning. One grep found ConvoyScreen.kt:145. One git show settled the z10 history. One sed of the branch chain found the checklist bug. Read first.
  • ⭐ And the arithmetic that would have ended the delete investigation in one minute: compare the delete rate to the DOWNLOAD rate. It was in the data all along.

🟢 00-TODAY (08-08) — FOURTEEN COMMITS. THE QUEUE TELLS THE TRUTH NOW.

HEAD d92e472fb · fourteen commits, all green. Three areas closed: the estimate defects, the unrecoverable queue, and the migration panel rebuilt after the version shipped this morning failed on device.

CommitWhat
6f48b83f5THROUGHPUT — startedAt + tilesPerSec on queue entries; estimator uses real run time; layer count from the SAT slot
4b126268eMIGOPT2 — option 2 enabled with tracks/areas selection (this is the one that failed on device; superseded by d92e472fb)
e4ffedbb1QEVAL — completed-row detail, concurrency 1-4 with stepper, aggregate throughput on the queue panel
c5fc7985bQTRUTH + ORPHAN — whole-run estimator, aggregate fix, default 3 slots, twisties, row format, corridor names, orphan worker block, held-queue escape

⛔⛔ 00-INCIDENT (08-08) — THE UNRECOVERABLE QUEUE. SPATIAL DB LOST ON DROID 2.

WHAT HAPPENED, IN ORDER

  1. Option 2 was run with corridors selected. The delete never executed — optTracks was false at the confirm handler although the box appeared ticked.
  2. Corridor tiles therefore stayed on disk. The area pass read them as area coverage and queued 172 jobs to re-download that ground as rectangles.
  3. The panel stayed up: refreshEnqueued was only set at the END of the coroutine, so nothing dismissed it.
  4. Queue entries were cleared while WorkManager still held the jobs. Workers take every parameter from inputData and never consult the queue, so they kept starting — running 20 minutes at 0 tiles, with no failure.
  5. The panel returns early when the queue is empty, so RELEASE and CANCEL were not on screen. Force-stop did not help: WorkManager exists to survive process death and rescheduled them.
  6. The only exit was clearing app data. The spatial DB went with it.

⭐ THE COST OF NOT DELETING, MEASURED: 30 tracks swept up as areas cost 2M+ tiles on ONE source. 89 tracks as proper corridors cost 1.5M across THREE. Corridors were designed for a ~90% reduction and that holds. The delete is not space reclamation — it is what stops corridor ground being re-covered as boxes.

WHAT WAS FIXED (all in c5fc7985b and d92e472fb)

  • Orphan block. Both workers look their entry up immediately after init. No entry, no work. ⚠ Returns failure, not success — an orphan reporting success writes a lie into the log, and failure() is terminal where retry() would resurrect it on backoff. ⚠ Deliberately does NOT call markFailed: there is no entry to mark, and writing one would trade a running ghost for a permanent row.
  • Held-queue escape. The panel now computes qoHeld from isQueuePaused() and both early returns respect it. A held queue renders even with zero entries, showing QUEUE HELD — nothing will start with RELEASE, and CANCEL WORKERS whenever held or empty.
  • Always exitable. The migration run sets its terminal state in a finally, so a throw cannot strand the panel.
  • Step 0 records what the handler received — tracks=$optTracks areas=$optAreas to the log AND the first on-screen line. ⚠ THIS IS THE FIRST THING TO READ ON THE NEXT RUN. If it disagrees with what was ticked, the original defect is still live and it is visible in one second rather than twenty minutes.

⭐⭐ 00-THROUGHPUT (08-08) — MEASURED AGAINST GOOGLE HYBRID. NEW BASELINE.

PER SLOT          AGGREGATE
2 slots  13.1        26.2
3 slots  10.0        30.0
4 slots   8.3        33.2      zero failures at every setting

Sustained run: ~140,000 tiles/hour (Fred's figure over a long window)
1.5M tiles across three sources in well under a day

⚠ CONFOUNDED, and the caveat matters: those three jobs hit three DIFFERENT sources (SAT, TOPO, TOPO+), so part of the gain is parallelism across hosts rather than concurrency against one. A clean test is several SAT-only jobs at 2, 3 and 4.

⭐ DEFAULT IS NOW 3 — most of the available gain with one fewer connection per source. Runtime settable 1-4 from the queue panel, persisted. ⚠ Raising takes effect as jobs finish; lowering does not pull running jobs back, it drifts down as they complete.

⚠ THIS SUPERSEDES THE 07-11 "2 BEAT 3" RESULT, which was measured against ESRI at ~11 tiles/sec. Google Hybrid is a different server characteristic entirely and that conclusion does not carry over. Every estimate calibrated before 08-08 was calibrated against the Esri era.

🔴 00-ESTIMATOR (08-08) — WAS 8-10x LONG. REWRITTEN.

MEASURED: the panel said Aug 12 5 AM (~86 hours) for 1.5M remaining tiles. The real rate was ~140,000/hour — about 10 hours.

Two defects compounded:

  • The rate came from ONE entry's elapsed time, which is a per-slot rate, while 3-4 slots run concurrently.
  • Averaging the last two entries does not fix it — if those two ran concurrently their windows overlap, so the average is still a slot rate.
  • ⚠ And createdAt is queue time, not start time. A job that waited behind 87 others carried hours of waiting in its elapsed. That alone was worth an order of magnitude.

⭐ REPLACED WITH FRED'S FORMULATION (08-08): total tiles completed since the run began, divided by elapsed wall-clock since the earliest job started. That IS the aggregate rate by construction. Concurrency cannot fool it, it absorbs stalls, holds and slot changes, and it needs no MAX_CONCURRENT multiplier — every failure mode above disappears rather than being corrected for. It is also the same arithmetic the yardstick ledger does across sessions, so the two finally agree.

Supporting work: startedAt stamped at dispatch, tilesPerSec computed at completion and stored on the entry (Fred: "the job knows duration and tiles — that is where the calc belongs"), both persisted and backward-compatible on read.

⚠ LAYER COUNT — ONE ENQUEUE PATH WAS WRONG

enqueueArea's UNSEGMENTED path summed layers across every download source (its own log line said layerCountAllSlots, slot=ALL). Under Esri that summed to 3 and happened to match what one Esri download wrote, so it looked correct. Under Google Hybrid one layer is written and the estimate stayed at 3x.

MEASURED 08-08: a job estimated 19,000 and finished at 6,463 — 2.94x.

⭐ Every other enqueue path (:257, :279, :612) already filtered by slot. Only the unsegmented one was missed. ⚠ Fixed by filtering to the SAT slot, NOT by removing the multiplier — a slot back on Esri must still count its 3 layers.

🟢 00-QUEUE-PANEL (08-08) — WHAT IT SHOWS NOW

  • Aggregate tiles/sec across running jobs, live. ⚠ It previously summed the filtered list and skipped jobs with no startedAt, so it reported roughly one job's rate.
  • Concurrency stepper 1-4 beside it, persisted.
  • Completed rows tap to expand — started, took, tiles/sec, and downloaded vs submitted side by side. ⭐ That last pair makes the layer-count class of defect visible at a glance: matching numbers mean the estimate was right, a ~3x gap means an enqueue path is still inflating.
  • Twisties on QUEUED and COMPLETED with counts. COMPLETED starts closed — after a big run it is the longest section by far.
  • Row format #7 CORR 2 SAT <track name>. The type token is the same scopeLabel() the filter chips use, so the row reads as the thing you filtered by.
  • Corridor jobs carry the track name. enqueueCorridor labelled every job with type-plus-slot — 272 identical rows. ⚠ Applies at enqueue, so jobs queued before this build keep their old labels.
