⚠ 00-METHOD (08-08) — WHAT COST TIME TODAY

  • ⛔⛔ A PATCH GUARD THAT SEARCHES FOR THE OLD CODE WILL FIND IT IN YOUR OWN COMMENT. Three times today a replacement block quoted the expression it was replacing, in a comment explaining the change, and tripped the guard checking that expression was gone. ⭐ FIXED STRUCTURALLY: every patch script now runs a SELF-TEST — each guard string is checked against every replacement block before the script ships. Describe the old code, never quote it.
  • ⛔ MULTI-LINE ANCHORS BUILT FROM sed OUTPUT ARE UNRELIABLE. Blank lines between statements are invisible there. Two anchors failed on exactly this. ⭐ Use cat -A when the anchor must span lines, or anchor on a single unique line.
  • ⚠ A GUARD SCOPED TO THE WHOLE FILE FLAGS CORRECT CODE. Checking that active.size had vanished flagged a legitimate QueueSummaryBar call. Guard the specific expression, not the token.
  • ⚠ A WINDOW ANCHORED ON THE FIRST OCCURRENCE LANDS IN THE WRONG PLACE. The finally check searched from the first migChoice == "auto", which is the option ROW hundreds of lines above the branch.
  • ⚠ A PATCH THAT INTRODUCES A COMPOSE SYMBOL MUST ADD ITS IMPORT IN THE SAME EDIT. Twice today a patch predicted the imports by name in its own output and shipped without them — two avoidable compile rounds.
  • ⭐⭐ THE REASSEMBLY WAS THE HARD PART, NOT THE PIECES. Every component of the migration was already built and proven on device. Calling them in order from a different screen introduced two defects and cost a full device rebuild. Fred: "this was a fairly simple change with all the bits running already, just assembled differently."
  • ⚠ ASSERTIONS FROM MEMORY WERE WRONG REPEATEDLY. The area-refresh-covers-corridors conclusion was reached, retired, and then re-established by measurement. Read the code; measure the device.

🟢 00-CORRIDOR-DELETE (08-07 built, 08-09 A-01 CERTIFIED + PERFORMANCE DIAGNOSED) — CORRIDOR DELETE

⭐⭐ 08-09 — A-01 PASSED. THE 08-08 DATA-LOSS DEFECT IS FIXED.

Build d92e472fb installed on Droid 2, option 2 with BOTH boxes ticked. Step 0 logged:

MIGSURFACE-2026-08-08Q start: tracks=true areas=true slot=SAT

The checkbox state reached the handler correctly. On 08-08 optTracks was false here although the box appeared ticked — the delete never ran, corridor tiles stayed on disk, the area pass read them as area coverage and queued 172 jobs. That is the failure that ended in the lost spatial DB, and it is closed.

The delete then ran for real: preview done: 88 tracks, 633050 distinct tiles, 472129 shared, ON DISK=633050 across 1 store(s) {SAT=633050} → delete start: 88 tracks.

⚠ 08-09 — TWO UI DEFECTS FOUND BY RUNNING IT

  • NO PROGRESS METER ON SCREEN. The design specified "Removing corridor map N of M" with a moving bar. onProgress fires per track and the log records each one, so the data exists — it is not reaching the UI. On a multi-minute irreversible operation that is not cosmetic: it is why Fred tapped CONTINUE twice, believing the first tap had not registered.
  • CONTINUE IS NOT GUARDED AGAINST A SECOND TAP. Two taps six seconds apart launched two preview threads (16315 and 16202). Harmless this time — one preview was discarded and only one reached delete start — but two DELETES racing would not be harmless. ⭐ Fix: disable CONTINUE on first tap, or render the step list immediately so the tap is visibly acknowledged.

⛔⛔ 08-09 — THE DELETE IS ~30x TOO SLOW. FIVE CAUSES RULED OUT BY READING THE CODE.

MEASURED, per-track, from logcat:

TrackTilesSecondsRate
Bar 10 overnighter21,852189116/s
BARRACKS LOOP9,42711681/s
Broken Ridge Jamboree17,73519591/s
Track 02/26/255915118/s

⭐ Cost is PER-TILE and LINEAR — ~98 tiles/sec. The 591-tile track took 5 seconds, exactly proportional, so there is no fixed per-track setup cost. Full run ≈ 633,050 ÷ 98 ≈ 1.8 hours.

⛔⛔ FIVE HYPOTHESES WERE PROPOSED AND THE CODE CONTRADICTED EVERY ONE. Recorded so none of them is re-proposed:

ProposedWhy it is wrong
Per-tile transaction / missing transaction wrapper deleteTiles ALREADY wraps one beginTransaction() and batches 500 rows per statement, grouped by (z,x). MBTilesStore:317-340
Missing index on the tiles table CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS tile_index ALREADY exists and runs on every open. MBTilesStore:94
O(n²) shared-tile re-derivation across 88 tracks The PREVIEW did all 88 tracks in 40 seconds (10:51:34 → 10:52:14) doing the same getTrackPoints + corridorTiles per track. Derivation is not the cost.
Run the deletes concurrently One SQLite file, one writer at a time — parallel threads queue at the DB level. And with all 88 corridors going, there is no shared-tile hazard to guard: every tile goes regardless.
Batch the deletes / fewer larger transactions Already batched. See row 1.

⭐⭐ WHAT THE CODE ACTUALLY SHOWED: NO PRAGMAS ARE SET ANYWHERE IN MBTilesStore. Only two read-only reporting queries at :359-360 (freelist_count, page_size). Every store runs on Android defaults — and Android ships secure_delete ON, which overwrites freed content with zeros rather than marking pages free.

THE ARITHMETIC FITS: 14.4 GB across ~633,000 tiles ≈ 23 KB per tile. Deleting 21,852 tiles frees ~500 MB of page data; 500 MB in 189 s ≈ 2.6 MB/s. That is disk write throughput, not query cost.

⭐ THE FIX — TWO PER-CONNECTION PRAGMAS (MBPRAGMA-2026-08-09C, written, not built)

d.execSQL("PRAGMA secure_delete=OFF")      // stop zeroing freed tile blobs
d.execSQL("PRAGMA journal_mode=TRUNCATE")  // truncate the journal, don't recreate it

Anchored in ensureSchema(), which runs on EVERY open (MBTilesStore.db() :64) — not only on creation.

⭐⭐ WHY THERE IS NO DISTRIBUTION PROBLEM — the question Fred raised. Both are PER-CONNECTION: they are not stored in the database file, they apply fresh at each open, and they touch nothing already written. No migration, no upgrade path, no user action, no setup value. Ship the build and every existing store gets the behaviour on its next open.

⚠ Why ensureSchema and not inside the delete function. The delete WOULD work — the handle is cached (handles[type]) so one pragma would hold for all 88 tracks. But (a) tile WRITES pay the same cost — INSERT OR REPLACE over an existing tile frees the old blob and secure_delete zeroes it, which is every Replace-mode refresh across the whole store; and (b) a pragma inside one function is a fact about that function, where in ensureSchema it is a fact about the store and the next code path inherits it.

⛔⛔ DO NOT "IMPROVE" THIS TO journal_mode=MEMORY, OFF, OR WAL. MEMORY and OFF leave a CORRUPT DATABASE if a write is interrupted. WAL is persistent, changes the file format and adds -wal/-shm companions — that one WOULD need a migration, which is the real answer to "why not do it in the refresh process." ⚠ These stores are ~14 GB with NO BACKUP, and a database the framework considers corrupt can be DELETED by the default error handler (see 00-REFUSE). Crash-safety is not tradeable here for delete speed.

⚠ MEASURING IT: force-stop first so the cached handles close and stores reopen with the new settings. Baseline to beat: 21,852 tiles in 189 s · 9,427 in 116 s · 591 in 5 s ≈ 98 tiles/sec. The patch logs SQLite's actual answer, so logcat shows whether it took.

⚠ SEPARATE, STILL AVAILABLE: 472,129 of the 633,050 tiles are SHARED between corridors, and the loop deletes PER TRACK — so a tile in five corridors gets five DELETE statements, four of them no-ops. The preview already computes the distinct union. One pass over it would remove that redundancy. Cost: the per-track "N of M" progress reporting would need chunking instead.

⚠ 08-09 METHOD — THE PATTERN THAT COST THE MOST TIME TODAY

⛔⛔ SIX SUCCESSIVE MECHANISMS WERE PROPOSED FOR THIS ONE PERFORMANCE PROBLEM, AND FIVE WERE CONTRADICTED BY CODE THAT COULD HAVE BEEN READ FIRST. The same pattern ran earlier in the day on the All-Files defect (six mechanisms, each corrected by Fred against observed device behaviour, ended by ONE grep finding ConvoyScreen.kt:145) and again on the z10 ANR history (two wrong readings, ended by git show on the reverting commit).

⭐⭐ THE RULE: READ THE FUNCTION BEFORE PROPOSING A MECHANISM FOR IT. Every one of these was settled by a single grep or sed -n, and in each case the reading took under a minute while the speculation took much longer.

⚠ ALSO 08-09: a multi-line git commit -m "..." chained with && ./gradlew assembleGoogleRelease swallowed the build command — Gradle printed its help banner and reported BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 14 s with no APK produced. Same class as the patch-guard lesson: the command reported success while doing something else entirely. Run builds on their own line.

Eight commits, all green. The automated refresh ran end to end on Droid 2. And running it produced a design conclusion that supersedes 00-MIGRATION below.

CommitWhat

MEASURED ON DROID 2 (real store, 5.3 GB SAT):

89 tracks total, 88 with geometry
633,050 corridor tiles by geometry
619,005 ON DISK   (97.8% -- corridors sit almost entirely inside downloaded coverage)
472,129 SHARED    (43% of the raw work is ground another track already covers)
      1 store     (SAT only -- label stores already gone on this device)
 33-61s scan      (warm cache roughly halves it)
617,255 deleted   (1,750 fewer than predicted -- Fred ran a delete-by-area between
                   scan and delete; the gap is ACCOUNTED FOR, which is the
                   verification working)

⭐ THE PREDICTION IS A SNAPSHOT, NOT A CONTRACT. Anything touching the store between scan and delete moves the number legitimately. The test is "does the gap have an explanation", not "do the numbers match".

⭐⭐⭐ 00-MIGRATION-V2 (08-07) — THE REDESIGN. SUPERSEDES 00-MIGRATION BELOW.

⭐⭐⭐ THE FRAMING THAT CHANGES EVERYTHING (Fred 08-07)

"This is a trail riding decision. Google maps is available ONLINE in either map as soon as the change in map sources is made."

⛔ The migration is about OFFLINE STORED TILES ONLY. Online rendering switches the instant the slot is reassigned — nothing to migrate, nothing to wait for. Everything below concerns only what is already on disk.

⛔ AND THE ARITHMETIC THAT KILLS THE ORIGINAL DESIGN

Fred: "Refreshing 50-60 GB of data is just not an option."

617,005 tiles is one device's SAT store. A full store refresh is DAYS. There is no automated flow that makes "re-download everything you own" reasonable. The original 00-MIGRATION option 2 — mandatory delete then full rebuild — cannot be offered at real scale.

⚠ AND THE DELETE MAY BUY LESS THAN WE THOUGHT (Fred 08-07, unresolved)

The delete existed to stop corridor tiles being swept into the area refresh as rectangles. But Fred's observation stands and is not yet disproven: the area refresh box already covers overlapping corridor tiles, and where a corridor runs outside any area, the corridor requeue puts those tiles back. Between the two, everything gets refreshed anyway — the delete may only make tiles come back twice.

⭐ THE ONE THING IT DOES BUY: the store's derived extent shrinks when corridors go, so the area refresh covers less blank ground between corridors. Real for a predominantly-corridor map, but smaller than the original framing claimed.

⚠ OPEN — SETTLES THIS IN FIVE MINUTES: read tileBoundsLatLon(slotName) in ConvoyTileDownloader. Single min/max box over every tile → corridors at the edges stretch it, delete helps. Segmented/per-zoom following actual coverage → the blank space was never covered and the delete buys much less.

⭐ THE OPTIONS AS FRED SETTLED THEM 08-07

#OptionForWhat runs
1Clear and start overlight coverage, few tracks; or anyone who does not want to think about it or fears getting it wrongclear the column, rebuild what you need. Cannot leave you half-converted.
2Refresh — corridors and areas, each optional no tracks → areas only · heavy in both → tick both, "click a button and come back in two days"Replace ON, delete OPTIONAL (default off)
3Leave my maps in place, replace at my leisure — with the option of track processing immediatelyheavy trackscorridors refreshed IN PLACE, no delete, areas skipped entirely

⭐⭐ AREAS STAY ON ORIGINAL MAP DATA UNTIL REFRESHED. Nothing goes blank, nothing is deleted. The only degradation is that Esri's label overlays are gone once the column moves off Esri — the base imagery stays. That is what makes option 3 safe rather than merely deferred.

⭐ WHY THE DELETE BECAME OPTIONAL: Replace overwrites in place, so the delete is not needed for correctness — only for reclaiming space. Default off. With it off the whole operation is ADDITIVE: no irreversible step, no point of no return, no permanent gate. The gate appears only if the user opts in.

⭐ USERS ARE ONE OR THE OTHER (Fred, from experience): a corridor-based map has few areas; an area-based map has few tracks. Two independent checkboxes let the user say which rather than the panel guessing.

⭐ THE SCAN IS WHAT LETS SOMEONE CHOOSE. "This many track tiles, this many area cells" turns an abstract decision into an arithmetic one. Someone with 50-60 GB reads the number and takes option 3. That is the scan's real job — not verifying the delete.

⚠ WITH 5 TRACKS THE DISTINCTION STOPS MATTERING — box them, accept a few more tiles. The failure mode is benign, so the design only has to be careful for the heavily-invested case.

WHAT TODAY’S CODE BECOMES UNDER THE REDESIGN

  • "Remove Tiles by Track" — stands alone as a downloads-menu feature, the by-track counterpart to Remove Tiles by Area. ⚠ Its gate text must NOT promise a rebuild: delete-only means coverage is gone until re-downloaded by track.
  • Corridor refresh (Replace on, no delete) — one call, enqueueCorridorBatch(hashes, listOf("SAT"), replaceExisting = true), production code already. ⭐ Needs to be reachable OUTSIDE the migration flow: option 3 + immediate corridor refresh is the path most heavy-track users will take.
  • Preview / on-disk counting / roll-up / progress meter — all reusable, all serve the optional-delete path.
  • ⚠ VERIFY BEFORE RELYING ON IT: enqueueCorridor passes refreshMode = replaceExisting, and that flag is OVERLOADED (isRefreshCell vs replaceTiles — the markComplete bug came from exactly that conflation). Corridor + Replace may never have been exercised together.

⚠ PERFORMANCE — INVESTIGATED, THEN DELIBERATELY DROPPED

Fred: "I could spend a month on this and it will be run once." Correct call — recorded so it is not re-opened.

~25 min for 88 tracks. Five theories were floated (recompute · SQL · transaction-per-track · index traversal · cross-track duplicates) and NONE were measured. The benchmark is free and still available: adb logcat -d | grep "deleteTiles SAT" — consecutive timestamps give per-track duration, and each line carries its tile count. Flat ~7s/track regardless of size → fsync/transaction overhead. Scales with tile count → statement volume.

Evidence found but not acted on: the default rollback journal does at least two disk writes per transaction plus an fsync — 88 transactions, 88 fsyncs, on FUSE storage already logging SELinux ioctl denials. SQLite itself executes millions of statements/sec, so 1.1M delete attempts should NOT cost 25 minutes on statement volume alone.

⛔ DO NOT "FIX" THIS WITH ONE BIG TRANSACTION. SQLite’s own docs: WAL does not work well for very large transactions; past ~100 MB rollback journal is faster, past a gigabyte WAL may fail with an I/O or disk-full error. 617k deletes in one transaction is the worst available combination. Moderate batches (~25-50k) if this is ever revisited.

⛔ DO NOT TURN JOURNALING OFF. No rollback means a crash leaves an UNDEFINED database — 5.3 GB, no backup, storage already throwing ioctl denials. Same exposure class as the 08-01 loss. PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL is the safe lever if one is ever needed.

Also explored and dropped: origin column on tiles / stamp-at-write / temp-table join / sorted-scan dedup. All sound; none worth the work for a once-per-source-change operation. ⚠ If ever revisited: insertTile uses INSERT OR REPLACE, which would blank an origin mark — a side table keyed on z/x/y avoids that entirely.

SCHEMA FACT WORTH KEEPING

ensureSchema creates stock MBTiles and nothing more:

tiles (zoom_level, tile_column, tile_row, tile_data)
UNIQUE INDEX tile_index ON tiles(zoom_level, tile_column, tile_row)

No timestamp, no source, no origin. Nothing distinguishes a corridor tile from an area tile, and nothing can be inferred. Any origin tracking must be added.

🔴 00-2.6e-REMAINING (08-07) — EVERYTHING STILL OWED BEFORE 2.6e IS FINISHED

MUST-FIX — unverified

  • Record a track offline on DROID 1 and confirm it draws. Open since 08-02, the last unverified must-fix. Needs real GPS. Decides whether the offline degradation work is still owed.
  • Hidden artifact types still produce popups. ⚠⚠ MAY BE STALE — VERIFY FIRST. Planner proximity was REMOVED 08-03; this item and the next both predate that. One grep settles whether either still exists: grep -c "checkLayer" app/src/main/assets/grouptrack_map.html. If it survives: two copies race per tap (~line 18 and ~854) and the function has no visibility parameter.
  • Taps still open artifacts while route mode is on. ⚠ Same staleness caveat.

DATA-LOSS GUARDS — never built, highest value on the page

  • ⭐⭐ REFUSE-TO-CREATE on DB open. A failed open still writes an empty schema over real data. SELinux ioctl denials fire on ORDINARY runs. See 00-REFUSE.
  • Behavioural All-Files check at EVERY startup — the permission API returns true and is wrong.
  • .lastgood copy + row-count sanity.

THE ESTIMATE CLUSTER — three coupled defects on one number

  • ×3 layer count — estimator sums layers across ALL sources; worker uses the LIVE slot. Correct under Esri, 3× wrong under Hybrid. Fix reads the live assignment; do NOT remove the multiplier.
  • Per-slot vs aggregate throughput — completedAt − createdAt on one entry is a SLOT rate; two slots run concurrently. Suspected 2× inflation. ⭐ Measure aggregate directly rather than multiplying by MAX_CONCURRENT, which breaks silently if concurrency changes.
  • Averaging the last two completed entries — if those two ran CONCURRENTLY their windows overlap, so the average is still a slot rate.
  • ⚠ The 08-05 "~690/hour is ~50× wrong" correction may itself have been made against a per-slot baseline.
  • ⭐ DISPLAY LIVE TILES/SEC ON THE QUEUE PANEL (Fred 08-07) — makes the estimate falsifiable instead of inferred hours later.
  • Check predicted vs actual across queue history — createdAt/completedAt survive cancelAll, and the yardstick ledger is the independent record.
  • ⛔ NO SCREEN QUOTES A DURATION until the tile count AND the rate are both trusted. Tile counts and "runs in background, resumes after restart" are honest today.

DISPLAY / ASSETS / RELEASE

🟢 00-MIGRATION (08-06 EOD) — SOURCE MAP MIGRATION. ⭐ START HERE ON PICK-UP.

⭐⭐⭐ THE OPEN TASK IS THE AUTOMATED REFRESH — option 2 on the migration panel.

It is the only option not selectable, and it waits on one thing: deleting the maps created from tracks as corridor downloads. Everything else in this section is finished and committed.

DONE   1a  MBTilesStore.deleteTiles          committed a0675be42
NEXT   1b  ConvoyCorridorDeleteWorker.kt     new file, compiles alone
THEN   1c  queue wiring                      ⛔ ATOMIC — see below

PURPOSE: when a user changes the source on a map slot, decide what happens to the tiles already stored for it.

⭐⭐⭐ THE DESIGN, SETTLED — ONE QUESTION, THREE ANSWERS, each protecting something different:

1  DELETE AND START OVER    protect NOTHING     clear · rebuild around tracks · draw new areas
2  MOVE EVERYTHING          protect the AREA    delete track tiles · refresh area · re-import tracks
   (automatic, background)                      all downloads submitted at once; every image slowly
                                                replaced from the new source
3  MOVE ON MY SCHEDULE      protect EVERYTHING  nothing touched; convert manually, ground by ground,
                                                with Replace tiles ticked

⭐ NO MAP-TYPE QUESTION. "How were these downloaded?" only ever existed to route an automated refresh. Option 3 has none, and option 2 handles both shapes together — which is just as well, because nothing on disk records how a store was built (the mbtiles schema does not carry it, and the queue's downloadType is wiped by clears with pre-08-05 completions deleted by the old markComplete bug).

⭐ Clear is now only ever chosen ON PURPOSE — a clean start, reclaiming space, or converting to corridors. No case is forced into it, which is what removed the earlier "are you riding soon?" question entirely.

🔴 THE REMAINING BUILD — delete tiles by track, in three steps

⭐ This is the ONLY new function the whole design needs. Options 1 and 3 are finished code; option 2 waits on this.

1a  MBTilesStore.deleteTiles(type, tiles)   ✅ DONE — applied, compiles, uncalled

1b  ConvoyCorridorDeleteWorker.kt           NEW FILE. Mirrors ConvoyCorridorWorker's
                                            discovery exactly: geomHash → getTrackPoints
                                            → corridorTiles → loop layers, with
                                            deleteTiles instead of downloadTiles.
                                            sourceName = slotName for layer 0, then
                                            layer.cacheDir. Nothing dispatches to it yet,
                                            so it compiles alone.

1c  QUEUE WIRING — ⛔ MUST BE ATOMIC
      · DownloadType.DELETE_CORRIDOR_TILES
      · DownloadPriority.forType() → DELETE
      · launchWorker dispatch → ConvoyCorridorDeleteWorker
      · enqueueDeleteCorridor + batch, DEFAULT = ALL TRACKS (no picker, no list)

⛔ 1c cannot be split. The when at ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt:797 is exhaustive by design — its own comment says "a 5th DownloadType later becomes a COMPILE ERROR here rather than a silent fallthrough" — so adding the enum value breaks the build until the dispatch branch lands.

⭐ Entry shape, mirroring enqueueDelete: label = "DEL CORR $slotName" · refreshMode = false · refreshSlot = slotName · downloadType = DELETE_CORRIDOR_TILES · priority = DownloadPriority.DELETE · geomHash carried so the worker re-derives (a 60K-tile list would be megabytes per entry in download_queue.json).

⚠ totalTiles uses the GEOMETRY count, as enqueueCorridor does. countTileRange cannot count a corridor — it takes min/max x and y, which is the hull again.

⚠ enqueueDelete's comment about refreshMode is now STALE — it says markComplete() silently removes such entries, but that branch was removed this morning in SHOWALLCOMPLETE-2026-08-05. Still correct to leave it false; the stated reason no longer applies.

⛔ SEQUENCING — the part that passes a design review and fails on device

PRIORITY IS NOT SEQUENCING. A delete sorts to the front, but at MAX_CONCURRENT = 2 another job can run alongside it.

⭐ The order that works for option 2 — derive the area bounds while the queue is HELD and the store is track-free:

holdQueue()
  1  delete track tiles     must COMPLETE — the one read that matters
  2  derive area refresh    bounds captured track-free, FROZEN onto each QueueEntry
  3  queue track re-imports geometry-based, needs no store read
resumeQueue()
  tracks execute first (CORRIDOR outranks REFRESH), area rebuilds underneath

⭐ Track tiles are absent for seconds, not hours — corridor outranks refresh in priority so the re-imports run before the slow area work even starts, and area imagery is never absent because enqueueRefresh replaces in place.

⚠⚠ VERIFY BEFORE RELYING ON ANY OF THAT: that enqueueRefresh genuinely derives once and stores north/south/east/west on each entry, rather than the worker re-deriving from slotName. This is a reading of the code, NOT confirmed in the worker, and the whole "execution order does not matter" property rests on it.

⚠ OPEN

  • ⛔ THE ×3 ESTIMATE. A Google corridor download estimated 1,560 tiles and downloaded 520 — ratio exactly 3 = Esri's layer count. Estimate uses getDownloadSources().filter{...}.sumOf{...} → 3; worker uses getSourceByKey(slot)?.layers → 1. The worker is right. ⭐ Second site: ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt:183 sums layers across EVERY slot with no filter and takes no slotName, so it cannot price a selection — callers ConvoyViewModel:987 and SpatialDbManager:2085 (track import). Fred: search git for the total-tiles calc, it was modified to correct display values. ⛔ Fix before any screen quotes times or sizes.
  • 5-second settle delay before resumeQueue(), after the delete loop. One line.
  • Record hygiene — begin() runs unconditionally but the dialog only shows if (count > 0), leaving records at "phase": "opened" (8 on Droid 2).
  • Bytes accounting — report from_bytes_total across all runs so the store count and the MB figure agree.
  • Is "Replace tiles" plainly reachable on the AREA download path? Option 3's instructions depend on it. Present in the corridor picker; the area confirm is unverified.
  • Does CONTINUE fit without scrolling? The body scrolls — the previous layout buried the actions, and only the device can answer whether the rewrite fixed it.

⭐⭐ THE BIGGER GOAL BEHIND ROUTE CORRIDORS — pre-ride map distribution

Fred 08-06, late: "I do not care about route. I am trying to create a method where a PRE-RIDE ROUTE CAN BE DISTRIBUTED AND IMPORTED WITH MAPS, allowing a rider to download accurate maps with efficient space utilisation BEFORE TRAILS ARE CAPTURED."

The route is the DISTRIBUTION UNIT, not a planning toy. A ride leader plots the route, emails it out, and each rider imports it and downloads exactly the corridor that ride needs. Nobody has to have ridden the ground, and nobody has to box a region.

⭐ It solves the case that has been forcing area downloads all along: ground with no tracks yet. You cannot corridor what nobody has ridden — unless someone plots it first. This is why routes belong in final 2.6e rather than 2.7.

⭐ Why it is accurate: a SNAPPED route's geometry IS trail geometry — snap-2 pulls the trail's own vector points — padded half a mile either side, so the corridor follows the real path rather than a straight line between waypoints. Corridor-quality coverage for ground never ridden.

⭐ Email survives it. The snapping happened at authoring time, so the coordinates already follow the trail. What does not travel is the per-vertex lineId — a local DB reference. So a shared route downloads maps and draws perfectly; re-snapping is re-established locally by adding or removing a point. ⚠ If the recipient's trail set differs, a re-snap could pull a point onto a different nearby trail — irrelevant for downloading, relevant if they extend it.

WHAT IT NEEDS, in order: (1) route geom_hash — computeGeomHash(wkt) exists and insertRoute already receives the WKT, so it is a column, one call at insert, and a backfill; (2) artifact TYPE carried alongside the hash through the corridor path — spatialTableFor(type) already exists; (3) a distribution format and import path, mirroring track import's existing corridor-download option.

⛔ Without the hash, ConvoyCorridorWorker resolves against TRACKS, finds nothing, and FAILS QUIETLY — the batch counts no-geometry as skipped.

⚠ Open: whether an UNSNAPPED route can be corridor-downloaded at all. A drawn line cuts across country between its points — its corridor covers ground the rider will not travel and misses the trail where it bends away.

⭐ TRACK NAME ON CORRIDOR QUEUE JOBS — REINSTATED

Fred 08-06 late: "retrieve the hash to retrieve track name on corridor download queue tasks" — and the reason: "it is important to know what are completed as we move along so I can drill down and test results without waiting for the whole batch to finish."

⚠ This REVERSES the rejection in the table below. It was dropped earlier the same day as scope creep, when the justification was user-facing polish. The new justification is INCREMENTAL VERIFIABILITY — with 262 rows all reading CORR SAT, a test run is unreadable. Treat this entry as authoritative.

⭐ No schema change: geomHash is already on every corridor entry. Resolve the name at display time, so enqueue, batch and worker are untouched and a renamed track shows its new name. ⚠ Batch the lookup — one query for all distinct hashes, not a read per row.

✅ Half of it already works: completed entries now survive in the queue (markComplete() was silently removing them until SHOWALLCOMPLETE-2026-08-05). ⚠ Expect one row per track per slot, so with three sources a single track produces three entries — cap the name (~20 chars) so the SOURCE stays visible.

⭐ FACTS LEARNED 08-06 — keep these

Google Hybrid's cache dir is google_hybrid, not SAT — one layer, its own file. The SAT column changes store FILENAME with its source. ⚠ Anything keyed on the literal "SAT" is reasoning about a store that only exists under Esri.

Under Esri the worker DOES fetch all three layers. They were stale since 08-02 only because everything since ran on Hybrid. So the ×3 is correct under Esri and wrong only under Hybrid — fix the estimate to read the live assignment, do not remove the multiplier.

⛔ enqueueRefresh shares segmentCells with enqueueArea (parity comment dated 07-22). The "second chopper needs retiring" claim in 00-ENQUEUE-PRINCIPLE is WRONG — correct it there.

tileBoundsLatLon(:201) derives its box as min/max over tiles at the densest zoom only — a hull, not a coverage map.

Droid 2 SAT column 08-06: SAT.mbtiles 5,314,371,584 (was 4.24 GB on 08-02) + labels 210,006,016 + 251,092,992 = ≈5,507 MB. ✅ The app runs fine with no SAT.mbtiles present — db() is open-or-create.

What it does

source picker  ->  "Map Source Changed"  ->  "Set up SAT"           ->  "Done"
                    NEXT STEP                 advisory + size            result
                    non-dismissible           CLEAR TILES / KEEP TILES

CLEAR TILES deletes every store the old source owned. KEEP TILES deletes only the orphaned label stores — they are dead the moment the source changes, because the new source declares no overlay layers. Both complete the migration record. The old Refresh Tiles / Refresh Now / Later dialog is gone from the app.

⭐ PANEL COPY (Fred's wording): "Please be advised: no matter which option you select, you will lose Esri place and road label information." Then: the new source does not fetch label overlays, so those stores are removed either way and their space is reclaimed.

⭐ The two imports are GREYED, NOT HIDDEN. They show their real checked value and are not interactive.

⭐ They sit LAST on purpose — positioning for origin-dependent expansion (below).

🔴 OPEN FROM TONIGHT

  • ⭐⭐ TRACK NAME ON CORRIDOR QUEUE JOBS (Fred 08-05, requested). "It would be nice to know what tracks have processed and what is next in the queue — handy on committed jobs." With 262 rows all reading CORR SAT, the queue says nothing about progress. The label is already the right place — enqueueCorridor builds label = "CORR $slotName", so it becomes "CORR $slotName · $trackName" and flows through to the panel and to completed entries. ⭐ Pass the name IN from the picker rather than looking it up: the picker already resolves duplicates to Pine Valley (1)/(2), which is exactly what should appear, and it avoids a second DB read per track. ⚠ Cap the name (~20 chars) — the source must stay visible; SAT vs TOPO matters as much as which track.
  • ⭐⭐ CORRIDOR JOBS ARE NOT SPLIT. Area submissions chop a large bbox into consistently-sized jobs; enqueueCorridor emits ONE entry per track per slot at any size. Deliberate on 07-24 ("decide after real numbers") — and the real numbers now exist: 561, 681 and 18,000 tiles in single jobs today, all under the cutoff, but bar 10 measured ~107,000 corridor tiles across three sources. ⛔ An oversized job holds a concurrency slot for its whole duration, cannot be partially cancelled, and shows one long bar instead of steps. Per-submission balance breaks: the queue is only as balanced as its least-balanced producer. ⚠ Use the SAME constant and chunking shape as the area splitter — a parallel implementation is the defect, not the fix.
  • OSM row is not rendering greyed on device (observed 08-05). enabled = false was set. Candidates: the 0.4 alpha is too subtle against that background, NET MODE is active, or the edit landed on the wrong row. Not a blocker.
  • ORIGIN-DEPENDENT PANEL EXPANSION (Fred 08-05, agreed, not built). The panel opens showing the section relevant to how it was reached — from the BUTTON expand the actionable rows; from a TRAIL or OSM import expand those. ⚠ Requires a launch-origin value alongside showDownloadPanel, which is a plain boolean today: nothing currently records WHY the panel opened. ⛔ Reset it on close or the second opening inherits the first one's expansion.
  • Only the PLANNER is wired. ConvoyScreen and ConvoyTrackImportScreen also call the panel; their new params are defaulted so they compile and behave as before. ⚠ The import path is the one that produced 215 jobs — it deserves its own pass, not a ride-along.

⭐⭐⭐ 00-ENQUEUE-PRINCIPLE (08-05) — ONE SUBMISSION MODEL FOR EVERY TILE REQUEST

⭐⭐⭐ Fred 08-05, the governing statement: "the queuing and sizing method should be consistent to all tile requests."

THE SHAPE:

bbox producers            ONE chopper           queue jobs
  drawn area          →   (consistently    →    balanced,
  area refresh             sized cells,          similar size
  selective source         cut at the
    refresh                threshold)

track producers           ONE deriver           queue jobs
  artifact panel      →   (corridorTiles   →    (NOT SPLIT YET
  corridor download        + the same             — see above)
  corridor refresh         cutoff)

⭐ BALANCING IS PER SUBMISSION (one area, one track), not across the queue. It needs no global view and no reshuffling, and it holds whether you submit one area or twenty tracks. A large bbox becomes 1/2, 2/2; one submission may be three jobs depending on tiles and sources.

⛔ A SECOND CHOPPER IS THE DEFECT. enqueueRefresh has its own 12×12 grid (gridCells) while enqueueArea segments properly — so the same bbox produces different job shapes depending which door it came through. That is how 08-02 produced 568 entries at 60–70K tiles each. It is not a sizing bug; it is a second sizing implementation.

⭐ THEREFORE enqueueRefresh IS RETIRED, NOT MIGRATED. Refresh becomes area-or-corridor enqueue with replace = true and downloadType = MAP_SOURCE_REFRESH — the type carries the KIND, the enqueue carries the SIZING, and there is exactly one place where a bbox becomes queue entries.

⚠ Confirm the threshold against the code before building — Fred 08-05 states 60,000 tiles; earlier notes say a ≤50K ceiling. One is stale. The corridor splitter must use whichever the area path actually uses.

⚠ While in that call site, compare enqueueRefresh's argument order against enqueueArea — swapped bounds is the leading unverified hypothesis for the 07-23 continental incident, and this work touches exactly that line.

🔵 00-REFRESH-DESIGN (08-05) — REFRESH IS TOTAL. TWO METHODS. THE CLEAR DECISION.

STATE: designed 08-05, NOT BUILT. Deliberately separated from the corridor download work so the shared enqueueCorridorBatch is proven by one caller before a second arrives.

⭐ REFRESH IS FULL TILE REPLACEMENT BY DEFINITION — there is NO picker. Per-track selection belongs to the DOWNLOAD panel. Refresh uses one of two methods: corridor replacement of all tiles (all known tracks) or area replacement of all tiles (every stored bbox).

REFRESH IS ONE MAP COLUMN, not all slots — so enqueueCorridorBatch serves it with a single-element slot list and replace = true. Same call, different arguments; that is what makes it reusable rather than two functions wearing one name.

⛔ The SAT column owns THREE stores

Fred 08-05: "there is no sat location and places label database — the SAT column owns SAT label and SAT places. If one is deleted they all are."

SAT column
  SAT.mbtiles              4.24 GB
  SAT_LABELS_PLACES         210 MB
  SAT_LABELS_TRANSPORT      251 MB
  ─────────────────────────────────
  total                    ~4.70 GB     (Droid 2, measured 08-02)

⚠ The panel must show live sizes, not these — a user's store will differ. And the real number is 4.70 GB, not 4.24: the label stores are the part people forget they are carrying.

⛔ TOPO and TOPO+ are their own columns and are NOT touched. Clearing SAT must never take topo — a rider who cleared satellite to rebuild by corridor finding their topo coverage gone is a silent, expensive failure nobody notices until they are off-grid.

⚠ A HYB rebuild will not refill the label stores — Google Hybrid bakes labels into the imagery and fetches no overlays. After clear + HYB refresh those two files are simply gone and the ~461 MB stays freed. That is the intended end state, not a loss — the panel must say so, or "two files didn't come back" reads as a bug.

⭐⭐ THE RULE: CLEAR ⇒ REPLACE = FALSE. Change the displayed value and LOCK it.

On a store that was just cleared, replace = true is pure waste — replace exists to bypass the hasTile pre-fetch filter, and on an empty store every tile is missing anyway.

⭐ AND IT ACTIVELY HURTS ON OVERLAPPING TRACKS. Corridors from tracks sharing a trailhead or an approach road overlap heavily. With replace FALSE the second track's corridor hits hasTile and skips what the first already fetched. With replace TRUE it re-downloads every shared tile. The clear runs as its own job ahead of the batch, so by the time any corridor job executes the store is empty and hasTile deduplicates across the WHOLE batch.

UI: selecting clear forces replace false, renders it false, and locks it — greyed with the reason beside it ("store will be empty — nothing to replace"), restored on deselect, visibly disabled rather than hidden.

⛔ The batch must refuse the impossible pair too — not silently: log it and force false. The UI is the enforcement; the function is the backstop, because refresh will be a second caller and must not be able to reintroduce it.

⚠ The clear is its OWN QUEUED JOB, ordered ahead of the batch — DELETE_AREA_TILES already has priority 0 reserved. ⛔ NOT a flag on the first entry: queue order is not guaranteed under MAX_CONCURRENT = 2, and a per-entry flag means whichever job runs first wipes what the others already wrote.

The transition grid — what the panel must recommend

From → ToClear SAT columnRefresh methodWhy
Area → CorridorYES — recommendedCorridorThe area store is ~90% tiles the corridor model will never use. Refreshing without clearing leaves the bulk as stale area fill with fresh corridors through it.
Corridor → CorridorNoCorridorSame footprint — overwrite in place.
Corridor → AreaNoAreaArea is a superset; existing corridor tiles are valid fill.
Area → Corridor AND Esri → HYBYES — the clean-slate caseCorridor⭐ One store, single-source, corridor-shaped, labels baked in. This is the coherent end state and the panel should name it.

⚠ Label deletion is conditional on the SOURCE change, not the shape — Esri → HYB. The grid carries it as a column because it is part of the same decision, but shape does not drive it.

⭐ OVERWRITE IS ONLY CLEAN WITH ALL TRACKS SELECTED. If the corridors cover everything the store legitimately holds, overwrite-in-place ends single-source without discarding 4.7 GB. Refresh a SUBSET after a source change and everything outside those corridors stays the old source — a permanent patchwork, and nothing in a stored tile records which source produced it.

⚠ ALL means all TRACKS, not all TILES. Area downloads drawn by box, and corridors from tracks since deleted, still sit outside every current corridor. Overwrite-with-ALL is clean only if the store was built from corridors alone.

The panel's exits — three, not two

If you are looking at this panel you have already chosen corridor refresh and the method. The panel explains the consequence and offers:

  • Clear and refresh — clean slate, with live sizes and the no-backup warning
  • Refresh without clearing — proceed, area files retained, ⚠ patchwork accepted knowingly
  • ⭐ ← Back to the refresh selection — you did not intend to give up the area coverage. The selection must survive the trip back, or nobody uses it: they will press forward rather than redo the work. ⚠ Back means back to the SELECTION, not out to the map. ⭐ Render the panel OVER the selection rather than replacing it — the state stays live underneath and there is nothing to rebuild.

⛔ NO BACKUP EXISTS and the store is GB-scale. Any clear names the column and its measured size before it does anything.

🔧 00-Z7 (08-05) — TRAILS AND TRACKS DOWN TO z7, BEFORE THE PLAY BUILD

Fred 08-05: "I want to display trails and tracks down to z7. Make a note to adjust before the Play Store version."

⚠ HISTORY THAT MUST TRAVEL WITH THIS: trails minZoom went 11 → 10 on 08-03 and was REVERTED THE SAME SESSION after ANRs on device — capped, on canvas, with both 07-31 objections addressed, and it still hung. z7 is four levels below the level that already failed.

⭐ MITIGATION ALREADY IN HAND (Fred 08-05): the trail set was cut from ~40,000 to the 10,000 largest. That is a 4× reduction in the population the low-zoom query would return, and it is why this is now plausible where z10 was not.

⭐ TRACKS AND TRAILS ARE DIFFERENT CASES. A user has tens of tracks; trails number in the thousands. Tracks at z7 is cheap; trails at z7 is the risk. Treat them separately rather than as one change.

⚠ Current row cap is limit = if (z < 14) 500 else 2000. Whether that alone is enough at z7, or geometry simplification is also needed, is the thing to measure — and the z10 ANR cause was never established, so do not assume the trail-count cut removed it.

🟢 00-TODAY (08-05) — THE PLANNER DRAW DEFECT. FOUND, FIXED, SHIPPED, PUBLISHED.

⭐⭐⭐ ROOT CAUSE: ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt:717 called evaluateJavascript DIRECTLY on the JavaBridge thread, inside the @JavascriptInterface onViewportChanged declared at :705.

java.lang.RuntimeException: A WebView method was called on thread 'JavaBridge'.
  at android.webkit.WebView.evaluateJavascript(WebView.java:892)
  in ConvoyMapViewerScreenKt$...$2.onViewportChanged

The throw aborted the rest of the method. Everything below :717 never ran: the eight state reads, MapStateStore.lastMapProcessed = "planning", and the Thread { SpatialDisplayManager.processViewport(...) } that performs the actual draw.

⛔ AND IT WAS SELF-PERPETUATING. The call sits INSIDE the lastMapProcessed != "planning" gate. The throw prevented lastMapProcessed ever being set — so the gate stayed open forever and every subsequent viewport event re-entered the same branch and threw again. The planner never completed a single viewport event.

Every symptom follows from that one line

Symptom observedExplanation
Nothing draws on direct planner entryprocessViewport never reached
Works if you visit convoy firstConvoy drew those pixels; the planner inherits them as residue on the shared WebView
Trails toggle does nothingtrailState is assigned two lines AFTER the throw
Continental bbox in the restore loglastViewport* is assigned BEFORE the throw, so it held whatever the last event reported — including convoy's frame
Stale trails from a prior region on zoom-outNever removed and never re-queried; the last successful draw simply persisted
08-03's "FIT bug closed, mechanism not established"Same defect. Removing the proximity block changed timing enough to mask it. That is why it came back.

✅ 00-COMPLETIONS (08-05) — THE QUEUE WAS DELETING ITS OWN SUCCESSES

⭐⭐ markComplete() branched on entry.refreshMode and silently REMOVED the entry — no COMPLETE status, no completedAt stamp, no row. The comment read "Refresh cells: silently remove from queue, no log clutter."

But refreshMode is ALSO the REPLACE-TILES flag. Both of 08-05's Google Hybrid corridor runs logged replace=true, so both took the silent-remove branch:

08:31:29  CorridorWorker: Corridor complete: CORR SAT downloaded=561 failed=0
08:47:04  CorridorWorker: Corridor complete: CORR SAT downloaded=681 failed=0

⛔ Both flawless. Neither visible. markFailed does NOT take that path — so the only download entries that ever survived were the ones that FAILED. That is why 08-02's "CORR SAT failed" was the only artifact left to reason from, and why the whole "Google downloads abort" framing looked worse than reality.

FIXED (Fred's call): the branch is gone. One path for every completion. "We know it's a refresh type and we have a filter — all completions should log."

⭐ THE DURABLE PRINCIPLE: the queue is the RECORD. Deleting rows to reduce clutter destroys evidence permanently; filtering hides it reversibly. Display concerns do not get to mutate state. Same rule as "hide means absent from the map, not absent from the database".

⭐ Side benefit: refresh cells now reach COMPLETE with a stamp, so they finally contribute samples to estimateRemaining() instead of vanishing from the pool.

⚠ Still owed: the flag is OVERLOADED — refreshMode means "refresh cell" in markComplete and "replace tiles" at the call sites. Split into isRefreshCell / replaceTiles. Note enqueueDelete deliberately leaves it false and its comment at :328-331 documents this exact trap.

⭐⭐ 00-HYB-PROVEN (08-05) — GOOGLE HYBRID DOWNLOADS WORK. END TO END.

⭐⭐⭐ THIS RETIRES THE "2–3 DAYS TO SOLVE THE GOOGLE DOWNLOAD ISSUE" FRAMING. Measured on Droid 2, 08-05, against the stable column-file config:

08:30:36  CORRIDOR queued: slot=SAT tiles=561
08:30:36  SLOTTRACE-2026-08-04: slot=SAT -> id=google-hybrid label=HYB
          baseUrl=https://mt{s}.google.com/vt/lyrs=y&x={x}&y={y}&z={z}
08:30:46  DL: https://mt1.google.com/... mt0... mt3...
08:31:29  Corridor complete: CORR SAT downloaded=561 failed=0    (53 seconds)

Second run 681 tiles, failed=0, 56 seconds. A third run of 18,000 tiles completed.

  • Slot resolves correctly to google-hybrid — SLOTTRACE-2026-08-04 doing exactly its job.
  • The {s} substitution works — mt0/mt1/mt3, the modulo distributing as designed.
  • Completion is stamped with counts.
  • RESOLUTION IS ELIMINATED as a cause. Fred placed step 3 after the column file deliberately so this result would be diagnostic either way. The config held still and the download succeeded.

⭐ Throughput: ~11 tiles/second sustained. The ETA's ~690 tiles/hour is off by roughly fiftyfold, matching the 07-25 measurement. The accumulator work stands.

⚠ Still genuinely open: whatever "CORR SAT failed" was on 08-02. A FAILED corridor job is a different animal from a completion that deleted itself, and that one is still unexplained.

⛔ SELinux denial is ROUTINE, not a one-off. Logged mid-run, 08-05:

avc: denied { ioctl } for path=".../maps/tiles/SAT.mbtiles" dev="fuse"
     scontext=untrusted_app tcontext=fuse permissive=0 app=com.grouptrack.android

It did no harm here — 561 tiles wrote successfully straight afterwards, so SQLite fell back fine. But this is the same denial class that preceded the 08-01 data loss, and it fires on ordinary runs. The refuse-to-create guard is protecting against something that happens regularly, and SAT.mbtiles is now 4.2 GB of unbacked work behind that same exposure.

🔴 00-INSTALL (08-04 designed, UPDATED 08-09 — NOT BUILT) — SELF-UNINSTALL FLOW + THE ALL-FILES AUTHORITY DEFECT AND ITS FIX

PURPOSE: remove the new-install data-loss exposure that destroyed ~89,000 trails on 08-01.
STATE: DESIGN AGREED 08-04. No code.
DOCUMENT OF RECORD: grouptrack_install_design_2026-08-04.html — full design, flow, and reasoning.
SUPERSEDES: the manual-only approach in grouptrack_reinstall_procedure_2026-08-03.html, which stays valid as the fallback for anyone not using the in-app path.

The key realisation

The app can uninstall itself. Intent.ACTION_DELETE launches the system uninstall dialog — and at that moment the app is alive and holds authority, so it can do the dangerous bookkeeping while it still can. Ten manual steps become two taps.

THE FLOW: user taps "Prepare for reinstall" → app renames Documents/GroupTrack → .bak and My Tracks → .bak (instant regardless of 50–60 GB) → fires the uninstall intent on itself → user installs the new build → All-Files grant requested FIRST, before anything touches the DB → REAL READ of the .bak dirs → prompt to restore → on confirm, verify the blanks are EMPTY, delete them, rename back.

⭐ WHY RENAMING BEATS DETECTING: every detection approach fails for the same reason — the app creates blanks BEFORE authority arrives, and the permission API reports the wrong answer at exactly that moment. The rename does not detect anything; it removes the data from the path the app will write to until authority is real. Nothing to detect, nothing to destroy.

⭐⭐ The ordering half — grant BEFORE the database

08-01 was an ORDERING failure. The app opened the DB before authority was real, and did not prompt because it TESTED and the test lied — Environment.isExternalStorageManager() returns true in the stale-grant state and is wrong. It reads the stale grant record; the filesystem enforces the truth. No API question returns the right answer — only a real read attempt does.

Fred: "we never got the prompt in the failed install as the app looked like it had authority. probably should not be a test but unconditional grant there." → make the All-Files prompt UNCONDITIONAL on first run and put it FIRST. The app already knows it is a fresh install — it is already asking first-run questions. No new detection needed.

⛔ REJECTED — AND WHY: blocking startup until the app can read would DEADLOCK the device. The app must run before it can be granted authority, so refusing to start until it can read means a reinstalled device can never be recovered. Also rejected: acting at uninstall time — Android has no uninstall hook; ACTION_PACKAGE_REMOVED goes to OTHER apps and arrives too late anyway. The danger is not the uninstall, it is the next install running blind.

The first-run marker — filesDir, NOT shared storage

⛔ THE TRAP if it lived in Documents/: that file sits in the very storage the authority question is about. Without All-Files the app cannot read it, so "file absent" and "cannot see the file" look identical — the exact confusion that caused 08-01, reproduced in a new file.

✅ filesDir needs no authority and is always readable, and is wiped by uninstall — which makes it a better signal, not a worse one:

marker ABSENT + data PRESENT in Documents/ = a reinstall over existing data. That is the detector 08-01 needed, free with the same file.

Contents: {"status":"in_progress"} → {"status":"complete","version_code":N}. Retaining it after completion answers both has setup run? and is this a reinstall?, and gives crash recovery for a first run that dies midway.

🔴 OPEN ITEMS

  • ⚠⚠ FIRST ACTION — VERIFY ON DROID 2 THAT AN UNINSTALL LEAVES Documents/GroupTrack/ INTACT. It should — public shared storage — but Android has changed storage behaviour repeatedly, and the entire design rests on this assumption. Minutes to test. Promoted from "open question" because if it is wrong the whole flow inverts.
  • Cancelled uninstall dialog: directories already renamed, app still installed, finds no data. Needs an undo path — on next launch, if .bak exists and the app is clearly NOT freshly installed, offer to rename back. (Renaming only after removal is confirmed is impossible; the app cannot observe its own uninstall.)
  • Settings/launcher uninstalls bypass the button entirely. The manual procedure stays documented as fallback — and refuse-to-create (below) is the floor under everything.
  • ⚠⚠ THE REVERSE-RISK MOMENT is step 8 — it DELETES directories. Everything before it protected against overwriting real data with blanks; this step can delete the wrong thing if the empty-check is wrong. Refuse if non-empty, log what was found, and ask — a user who recorded a track between install and restore has real data in those "blanks".

⭐⭐ 08-09 UPDATE — THE AUTHORITY DEFECT, LOCATED IN CODE, AND ITS FIX (SUPERSEDES the permission reasoning above)

PURPOSE: the shared root cause of BOTH data-loss events — 08-01 (~89,000 trails) and 08-08 (the whole spatial DB). STATE: design agreed 08-09. No code. DOCUMENT OF RECORD: grouptrack_allfiles_fix_strategy_2026-08-09_v1.html. CORRECTS the permission reasoning in 00-INSTALL above, which assumed a stale grant left over from a previous install.

The defect, located in code

ConvoyScreen.kt:145   mutableStateOf(!Environment.isExternalStorageManager())
ConvoyScreen.kt:151   if (!Environment.isExternalStorageManager()) { ... }
ConvoyScreen.kt:433   ACTION_MANAGE_APP_ALL_FILES_ACCESS_PERMISSION
OsmImportStage.kt:575 Environment.isExternalStorageManager()

ONE FAULT, NOT TWO: THE TEST ASKS THE WRONG SOURCE. isExternalStorageManager() reads the AppOps record. In the failure state that record says granted while the filesystem refuses. So the expression initialises the dialog flag to false and the prompt never fires. Fred: "the app said it had all authority, the checkbox was yes, but the app did not have all authority."

⭐ THE POSITION OF THE CHECK IS CORRECT AND MUST NOT BE MOVED. An earlier draft of this section claimed the check ran too late because it sits inside a Composable. Fred disproved it: it works on every new install. On a new install the value genuinely reads no, the prompt fires, the grant lands, and the DB is built afterwards. Same code, same position, correct outcome — the ONLY difference on the broken path is the API lying. The fix is a substitution, not a restructure.

⭐⭐ WHY IT HAPPENS — THE CHANGE IS ACTED ON, NOT THE VALUE

AOSP: storage access is enforced by a per-process mount view, not by the permission record. A mount namespace is created for each app at Zygote fork time and the appropriate view bind-mounted in. Then — critically — "when permissions are granted, vold jumps into the mount namespace of already-running apps and bind mounts the upgraded view into place", and downgrades always result in the app being killed.

"WHEN GRANTED" — THE ACTION FIRES ON THE TRANSITION. Nothing recomputes the mount view from the current value. That is Fred's question answered, and it explains every observed behaviour:

PathTransitionMount updated?
New installno → yes✅ event fires
The broken stateyes → yes ⛔ no event — nothing happens
Uninstall + reinstallfresh fork✅ view computed at fork
Toggle off then onyes → no → yes✅ two events

⭐ THIS IS WHY OPENING THE SETTINGS SCREEN ON ITS OWN DOES NOTHING — Fred's point, and it is right: entry state and exit state are identical, so there is no transition to act on. The transition has to be manufactured.

⚠ CAVEAT — NOT PROVEN. The AOSP passage says runtime permissions. MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE is an AppOp / special access, not a runtime permission. If the vold path does not cover AppOps at all, then no in-place grant ever updates the mount and only a fresh process fork works — meaning reinstall really is the only cure. ⚠ Several mechanisms proposed during the 08-09 session were wrong and were corrected against observed behaviour. Nothing below depends on this being right.

⭐ WHAT IS ACTUALLY AT RISK — read this before panicking

ThingAt risk?
Data in Documents/GroupTrack/ NO. Public shared storage — survives clear-cache, clear-data, uninstall, all of it.
Lost storage accessRecoverable. Uninstall + reinstall works (observed).
The app writing a blank over the real DB THE ONLY PERMANENT LOSS.

⭐ We do not have to fix the permission to stop losing data. We have to stop the app proceeding when it cannot see its own files.

THE FIX — in build order

STEP 1 — REPLACE THE TEST. Never ask the API. The only truthful question is a real read of a path All-Files actually governs. filesDir/cacheDir always work, so reading those proves nothing.

fun hasRealStorageAccess(): Boolean = try {
    val d = File(Environment.getExternalStorageDirectory(), "Documents/GroupTrack")
    d.exists() && d.canRead() && d.list() != null      // plain File I/O — NO SQLite
} catch (e: Exception) { false }

⛔ "Cannot see it" and "it is not there" get the SAME treatment — treat invisibility as UNKNOWN and refuse. Reading invisibility as emptiness is exactly what built blanks over real data.

⚠ Same substitution at :151 and OsmImportStage.kt:575.

STEP 2 — MANUFACTURE THE TRANSITION. Two panels, two settings trips. Each trip needs its own instruction panel first, because the settings screen tells the user nothing about which way to move the toggle.

1  real read fails, value says YES
2  PANEL A  "Storage access isn't working. The permission shows as ON
             but isn't working. On the next screen turn All-Files
             access OFF. It will look correct already — turn it off
             anyway."                                [ Open settings ]
   ↓ fires ACTION_MANAGE_APP_ALL_FILES_ACCESS_PERMISSION
   ↓ PROCESS LIKELY DIES HERE — the downgrade kills it
3  verify by VALUE that it now reads NO   ← the one place the API is
                                            the correct thing to ask
4  PANEL B  "Now turn it back on."        [ Open settings ]
5  value reads YES — a genuine no→yes transition this time
6  RE-RUN THE REAL READ
       succeeds → carry on
       fails    → uninstall + reinstall message

⚠ PERSIST THE CYCLE STAGE (filesDir or SharedPreferences). The process dying between 2 and 3 is EXPECTED, not a failure — without a marker the user returns to an app that has forgotten it was mid-cycle. ⭐ It degrades safely: a lost marker means step 1 re-runs, finds the value now reads no, and prompts normally.

⚠ PANEL A CARRIES THE HARDEST WORDING IN THE FLOW. The user is being asked to switch off a permission that appears correct. Without the "it will look right — turn it off anyway" line, most people back out.

STEP 2b — LOOP BACK. THE READ IS A GATE, NOT A ONE-SHOT CHECK.

⭐⭐ THE APP NEVER PROCEEDS BECAUSE THE USER CAME BACK FROM SETTINGS. IT PROCEEDS ONLY BECAUSE A READ PASSED. Returning from the settings screen proves nothing — the user may have changed nothing, changed the wrong thing, or changed it correctly and had it not take. The only evidence that counts is the read.

fun gate(): Boolean {                    // ONE function, called at EVERY entry
    if (hasRealStorageAccess()) return true
    runPanelCycle()                      // Panel A → settings → Panel B → settings
    return hasRealStorageAccess()        // ← RE-TEST. Never assume.
}

CALL IT AT EVERY ENTRY POINT, not once at startup:

  • on resume from the settings screen — every return, both trips
  • before any SpatialDbManager open
  • before any MBTilesStore open
  • before any import, download or migration run
  • on app resume from background — authority can be lost while the app lives

⛔ NO CACHED "we checked at startup" FLAG AS THE ONLY SOURCE. A flag set once at launch is the same class of mistake as trusting the API: it answers from a record instead of from reality. Cache the RESULT for the current screen if needed, but re-read before anything that opens or creates.

⚠ THE FAIL PATH NEVER FALLS THROUGH. If the final read fails, the app shows Step 4's message and stops there. There is no "continue anyway", no timeout that proceeds, no dismissible dialog that lets the user past. Backing out of any panel leaves the app in the refused state — which is correct, because the alternative is what destroyed the DB twice.

⭐ WHY THE LOOP MATTERS MORE THAN THE CYCLE: the two-panel cycle is a REMEDY and is unproven. The loop is a GUARANTEE and needs nothing to be true about the permission mechanism. Even if every remedy fails, the loop means the app sits refusing rather than proceeding blind.

STEP 2c — THREE ATTEMPTS, THEN ABORT SETUP. Do not loop forever.

⭐⭐ AFTER THREE FAILED CYCLES THE APP STOPS OFFERING THE REMEDY AND ABORTS SETUP WITH A TERMINAL MESSAGE. The two-panel cycle is unproven. If it has not worked in three attempts it is not going to, and continuing to send the user back to the same settings screen is worse than useless — it teaches them to tap past the warning that is protecting their data.

allfiles_attempts   persisted in filesDir  (NOT shared storage —
                    the app can always read filesDir, no authority needed)

on gate() failure:
    attempts += 1
    if attempts < 3  → run the panel cycle, re-test
    if attempts >= 3 → ABORT SETUP. Terminal screen. No further cycles.

on a PASSING read → attempts = 0        ← reset, so a later unrelated
                                          failure gets its own three

THE TERMINAL SCREEN — abort, do not continue:

"GroupTrack cannot reach its map storage and could not repair it.

 SETUP HAS BEEN STOPPED. Nothing has been changed or deleted.
 Your maps and tracks are safe in Documents/GroupTrack.

 To fix this:  uninstall GroupTrack, then install it again.
 A reinstall does NOT remove your maps or tracks.

 DO NOT move or copy your map files anywhere while this
 message is showing, and do not put them back by hand
 afterwards. The app will find them on its own.

 Do not clear app data or cache — that is what caused this."

⛔⛔ THE MESSAGE MUST TELL THE USER NOT TO RESTORE BY HAND — that is how BOTH stores were actually lost on 08-08. Fred saved the tile stores before clearing cache and lost them moving them back while authority was still broken; the hand-restored spatial DB was zeroed the same way. A helpful user who copies their data back into a blind app is deleting it. The reinstall path needs no manual file handling at all — say so explicitly.

⛔ ABORT MEANS ABORT. No "continue anyway", no dismiss, no proceed-after-timeout. Setup does not run, the DB is not opened, tile stores are not opened. The app sits on this screen until it is uninstalled or storage access genuinely returns. The alternative to sitting there is the thing that destroyed the spatial DB twice.

⭐ THE COUNTER LIVES IN filesDir FOR TWO REASONS: the app can always read it with no authority at all, and an uninstall wipes it — so a genuine reinstall starts fresh at zero automatically, with no reset logic to get wrong. ⚠ Note clear-data also wipes it, which is correct: that is a new episode and deserves its own three attempts.

⚠ THE COUNTER MUST SURVIVE THE PROCESS DEATH between Panel A and Panel B — increment and persist BEFORE firing the settings intent, never after returning from it, or a cycle that kills the app never counts and the loop runs forever.

⚠ KEEP STEP 5's DIAGNOSTICS ON THE TERMINAL SCREEN. This is the moment worth photographing — three failed repairs is the strongest evidence we will ever get about what is actually wrong.

STEP 3 — REFUSE TO CREATE. ⭐⭐ THIS ALREADY HAS ITS OWN SECTION — SEE 00-REFUSE BELOW. DO NOT RE-DESIGN IT HERE. It was lifted out of the install design on purpose on 08-05: the install flow protects users who tap the button, 00-REFUSE protects everyone else. The 08-09 additions (MBTilesStore, the DatabaseErrorHandler question, the hand-restored DB) are recorded THERE, not here.

What the 08-09 work adds to it in one line: the gate function from Step 2b is what 00-REFUSE gates on — a real read, not the permission API.

STEP 4 — SAY ONLY WHAT HAS BEEN OBSERVED. When the read still fails after the cycle: "GroupTrack cannot reach its map storage. Your maps and tracks are safe in Documents/GroupTrack — nothing has been changed or deleted. Uninstall GroupTrack and install it again to fix it."

⚠ DO NOT PROMISE THE TOGGLE CYCLE WORKS. It has never been demonstrated — by the time it was tried on 08-08 the data was already gone, so nothing was proven. Uninstall + reinstall is the ONLY remedy actually observed to work.

STEP 5 — INSTRUMENT THE REFUSAL SCREEN. This has cost data twice and the behaviour is not understood. Put the evidence on screen so the next occurrence is a photograph, not an evening of reconstruction: what the API claims · whether the plain read succeeded · what filesDir contains · what is visible in Documents/GroupTrack/ and file sizes if readable · what triggered this startup path.

🔴 STILL UNKNOWN — do not build past these

  • WHAT LAUNCHES SETUP WITHOUT AN INSTALL? Fred: "it is redefining core app functionality without all object authority, and it stays that way until an uninstall." Clear-cache satisfied whatever condition setup keys on. NOT YET READ. grep -rn "firstRun\|setupComplete\|isFirstLaunch\|hasRunSetup" app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/ ⚠ Setup is Meshtastic's, not ours — which is exactly why Step 3 guards the OPENS rather than trying to gate a flow we do not own.
  • Does the off-then-on cycle actually restore access? Untested. If yes, Step 4 softens to two taps.
  • Does appops set <pkg> MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE allow + force-stop restore it? That isolates record-vs-process.
  • Why did the hand-restored populated DB get zeroed? Error handler is the leading candidate, unverified.

Testing — and why it is awkward

⛔ NOT ON DROID 2. 490k Utah trails and a live corridor rebuild. It stopped being a test device on 08-08.

⭐ REPRODUCE NON-DESTRUCTIVELY: on a spare device put a small DUMMY grouptrack_spatial.db and a marker file in Documents/GroupTrack/, then clear cache / reinstall over the top. Observe: does the permission read as granted, does a plain read succeed, does the dummy survive. Same state, nothing real at risk.

⚠ While you are there, verify 00-INSTALL's founding assumption — that an uninstall leaves Documents/GroupTrack/ intact. It should (public shared storage) but Android has changed storage behaviour repeatedly and the whole rename design rests on it.

What the 08-09 work CORRECTS in the 08-04 design above

  • KEEP: grant before the database · filesDir marker (not shared storage) · refuse-to-create as the floor · the rejection of blocking startup entirely.
  • CORRECT: 00-INSTALL says the API "reads the stale grant record" from a previous install. It is not stale — the grant was live and the enforcement disagreed. No permission API returns the right answer at any time.
  • CORRECT: "make the prompt unconditional and put it first" is necessary but not sufficient — the user was already granted, so an unconditional prompt shows a permission that is already on and changes nothing. The gate must be a real read, and the transition must be manufactured.
  • NOTE: the self-uninstall rename flow only covers the path where the user taps the button. Settings-initiated clear-data and clear-cache bypass it entirely — and that is what bit us both times.

🔴🔴 00-REFUSE (2.6e P0, UPDATED 08-09) — REFUSE-TO-CREATE. THE FLOOR UNDER EVERYTHING.

⭐⭐⭐ LIFTED OUT OF THE INSTALL DESIGN ON PURPOSE (08-05). The install flow protects users who tap the button. This protects everyone else — including every Settings/launcher uninstall, every revoked permission, every FUSE/SELinux hiccup. Different priority, different item.

SpatialDbManager must NEVER create-if-missing on a path it cannot verify. A failed open must ABORT, not write an empty schema. The install design names this as "the single highest-value item on this page" — it alone would have prevented 08-01, and it costs nothing at runtime.

⚠ And it is not hypothetical maintenance: the SELinux ioctl denial on the FUSE-mounted store fires on ordinary download runs (observed 08-05, see 00-HYB-PROVEN). The trigger condition is present regularly; only the create-if-missing turns it into data loss.

Also owed with it: ROW-COUNT SANITY + a .lastgood copy after each successful startup. A startup reporting zero rows where the previous run reported 89,000 is a red flag the app should RAISE, not report as normal.

⭐⭐ 08-09 — WHAT THE GATE MUST BE. The 08-09 investigation located the authority defect in code (ConvoyScreen.kt:145, see 00-INSTALL above) and it changes what 00-REFUSE tests on: NOT the permission API — a REAL READ of Documents/GroupTrack/ using plain File I/O, no SQLite. isExternalStorageManager() returns true in the failure state and the settings toggle shows granted, so any check built on either is the same bug wearing a different hat. ⛔ "Cannot see it" and "it is not there" get the SAME treatment — refuse.

⚠⚠ 08-09 — MBTilesStore IS NOT COVERED AND MUST BE. This section has only ever named SpatialDbManager. ⛔ CORRECTION 08-09: THE MAPS DID NOT "SURVIVE BY LUCK" — THEY WERE LOST TOO. Fred saved them deliberately before clearing cache, and lost them on the way back in: he moved them back after the install while the authority failure was still live, and the app built new empty stores instead of reading them. Same mechanism as the hand-restored spatial DB — one story, not two. Both get the gate.

⛔⛔ 08-09 — THE SIGNATURE, IN FRED'S WORDS: "the app recreated but had no authority to what existed before." This is the failure in one line, and it happened to BOTH stores. A hand-restored file is not safe. Fred restored a real populated spatial DB and the app zeroed it out and reset it; he restored the saved tile stores and the app built empty ones over that ground. The files were physically present and visible to a file manager the entire time. The app could not reach them, so it created — and creation is what destroys.

⚠ MECHANISM STILL UNVERIFIED. Leading candidate for the DB: SpatialDbManager opening without passing a DatabaseErrorHandler, so Android's default handler deletes a database it deems corrupt and recreates it — an unreadable file is indistinguishable from a corrupt one. A custom handler that logs and throws is a one-parameter change and would close that half independently of every permission question. GREP BEFORE BUILDING: grep -rn "openOrCreateDatabase\|DatabaseErrorHandler" SpatialDbManager.kt

⭐⭐ THE OPERATIONAL CONSEQUENCE — THIS IS THE PART THAT CHANGES USER ADVICE: RESTORING FILES BY HAND WHILE AUTHORITY IS BROKEN DESTROYS THEM. Any recovery instruction — manual, quickstart, in-app message — must say restore ONLY after a real read succeeds, never before. Telling a user to copy their data back into a blind app is telling them to delete it.

⭐ Consequence: the gate sits ahead of every OPEN, not merely ahead of CREATION. Refusing to create is not enough if the framework can delete on a failed open before our code decides anything.

✅ Fixed 08-04: SpatialDbManager :166/:168 logged the LITERAL ${spatialFile.absolutePath} because the $ was backslash-escaped — so the DB path never appeared in the log. That was the diagnostic that would have made the 08-01 loss obvious immediately.

📌 00-0804 (08-04) — THE SESSION THAT HAD NO CHECKLIST ENTRY
