On 08-05 the changes appeared unexplained in git status, and the design had to be recovered by re-reading the previous session. This is exactly the failure the embed-the-topic rule exists to prevent — and it only worked because the conversation still existed. A design that lives only in a chat log and an unreferenced file is one cleanup away from gone.

Commit dccf0294e — between dd8370e37 (08-03) and 10b2e7fa3 (08-05).

NOPAD-2026-08-04 — the compounding double-pad

TWO paddings were stacking on every restore. SpatialDisplayManager padded the saved bbox by 15% (added with FIT), on top of fitBounds() which had already been padding 60px since V2.4.

⭐ Neither was FIT-specific, and SpatialDisplayManager is the only Kotlin caller of fitBounds() — so ordinary map re-entry paid for FIT's framing and zoomed out a little EVERY TIME. That is the planner padding drift of §19, found from the other end.

Both removed: the 15% in SpatialDisplayManager.kt, the 60px in BOTH map HTMLs. maxZoom STAYS — it stops the fit zooming past downloaded tile coverage, which ends at z18. ⭐ Same fix applied to both maps in the same session: the parity discipline working.

WEBVIEWDEBUG-2026-08-04 — added 08-04, removed 08-05

WebView.setWebContentsDebuggingEnabled(true) in MeshUtilApplication.onCreate(), to let Chrome DevTools attach and answer whether Leaflet was holding a detached-but-populated layer.

⭐ The hypothesis was wrong and the tool was never needed. The answer came from a StrictMode trace already present in every logcat capture. The instrumentation you do not have to add beats the one you do — same lesson as the 08-02 three-tap test, and the counter-example to a week of instrumentation causing more damage than it found.

✅ Removed in 10b2e7fa3, per its own comment and the 08-03 rule: instrumentation comes out in the commit that closes the task.

🧰 00-TOOLS (08-05) — TROUBLESHOOTING TOOLS AND USES

DOCUMENT OF RECORD: grouptrack_troubleshooting_tools_and_uses_2026-08-05.html — six sections: the three xrefs and their blind spots · layer contracts · divergence · the 2.7 dead-code prune recipe · the draw-failure recipe · pre-build hazards.

⭐⭐ WHY IT EXISTS: four defects this week shared one shape — a method aborted before its real work, silently — and no structural tool could have found any of them. The xrefs answer where is this touched. Every one of these lived on a different axis: on what thread, in what lifecycle phase, against which page. There is no index for that axis.

The layer contracts — the rules with no tooling

BoundaryRuns onRule
@JavascriptInterface methodsJavaBridgeANY WebView or UI call MUST go through Handler(Looper.getMainLooper()).post { }. They throw off-main and the throw aborts the method silently. Four-for-four now — mechanical rule, no case-by-case judgement.
Argument positions—Never ?: return inside an argument. Reads as a null-safe accessor, is an early exit from the whole function.
onPageFinishedmainPage not ready until the postDelayed completes. Earlier Kotlin→JS calls fail with <fn> is not defined, visible only in a console log.
evaluateJavascript(fn)main onlyThe named function must exist in BOTH map HTMLs. SpatialDisplayManager's jsUpdate/jsShow/jsHide contract assumes hand-maintained parity.
WorkManager workersfresh processJobs survive app death. ensureInit() re-reads config from scratch — never trust in-memory state across the process boundary.
shouldInterceptRequestnetworkNo UI, no WebView calls.

⭐⭐ navigation_xref.txt — the one xref that gives CALL SITES

Four sections. A nav-host routes (every registered destination) · C navigation triggers — navigate() calls and onNavigate* params with the line where each is invoked · D screen composables + exit callbacks · E user-visible labels.

⭐ SECTION A vs SECTION C MAKES THE DEAD-DESTINATION CHECK MECHANICAL: a route registered in A with no navigate() in C is unreachable. Proven: ConvoyRouteCreate is registered at ConvoyNavigation.kt:87 (A), its composable declared at ConvoyRouteCreateScreen.kt:18 (D), and it appears nowhere in C — a 27-line stub reachable from nothing. Do this before any manual grepping in the 2.7 prune.

⭐ Section E is the MANUAL-WRITING tool — it is what the user actually taps, which is the vocabulary the manual has to use.

⚠ Check its Generated: timestamp against the last commit. A call-site index that lags the code produces confident wrong answers. (Current copy: generated 2026-08-05 09:14.)

⚠ The xrefs' documented blind spots — each cost real time

  • Lambda parameters — onDownloadMaps appears in no xref; looked dead, was live in two screens.
  • var X by remember { } locals — addPointMode is in none of them.
  • Tags held in constants — 08-05: a grep for Log.x("LITERAL" returned nothing across four well-instrumented files, because the tags are TAG constants. The scan reported a silence that did not exist.
  • Anything inside a non-executing block — 08-03: a def-scan called a range clean; it was inside a <script src> tag whose inline body never executes by spec.

⛔ THE PATTERN BEHIND ALL OF THEM: scanning for one shape of evidence and reporting "clean" when the scan could not have seen what was there. A negative grep result is only as good as its pattern. Before trusting an empty result, prove the pattern matches a known-present case.

⚠ These blind spots apply to field_crossref / where_used / function_universe — declaration indexes. navigation_xref is the exception (above): for screen routing and reachability, read it FIRST rather than grepping.

⭐⭐ CONVOY IS THE REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION — divergence is a defect until proven intentional

Four times this week convoy had a fix the planner did not, and nobody knew until it broke: (1) [Fix2] fresh re-read; (2) seeding lastViewport* before draw; (3) posting evaluateJavascript to main; (4) the reseed gate — this one IS intentional (convoy is persistent and owns one map identity; the planner is entered and left, and the gate prevents cross-map bleed).

⚠ THE LIMIT: the maps are not symmetric by nature and must not be forced to be. The target is SHARED CODE, not MATCHED CODE — two hand-maintained copies drift again however carefully aligned today. SpatialDisplayManager has been the reliable layer all week precisely because there is ONE copy. ⭐ This is the same argument that makes the 2.7 service-layer work worth doing.

⛔ THE TRAP UNIQUE TO THIS CODEBASE — read before any 2.7 dead-code prune

Kotlin calls JavaScript functions BY NAME, from inside a string. ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt:578 calls showProximityPopup that way. No JS tool sees that call. No Kotlin tool sees it either. A JS function with zero references in the HTML can be fully live; a Kotlin function whose only caller is a nav registration can be fully dead. Neither toolchain crosses the boundary — you are the only thing that does.

TOOLING OWED (none of it exists yet): a bridge-thread scanner (WebView/UI calls inside @JavascriptInterface bodies — would have caught :717 the day it was written, and run against both screens it answers the divergence question for free); a cross-boundary name checker (every evaluateJavascript("fn(...)") has a matching function fn in BOTH HTMLs); a screen-parity diff.

🔵 00-CORRIDOR-REFRESH (08-05) — NEXT BUILD, AFTER THE AAB

PURPOSE: refresh existing downloads by CORRIDOR, not by bounding box.
STATE: agreed 08-05, not started. Steps 4–6 of the 08-03 six-step plan.

⭐ WHY IT MATTERS MORE THAN "a nicer option": the 215-job incident was area refresh converting each track BOUNDARY into a BBOX. A switchbacked trail's bbox covers the whole mountain; its corridor covers a line buffer. Corridor refresh is the fix for the thing that produced 215 jobs — the same problem corridor downloads were built for on 07-24.

⚠ Corridor refresh DOES NOT EXIST. Fred 08-03: "the refresh does not currently support corridor imports." Step 5 is BUILD, not MIGRATE.

The shape (Fred 08-05)

Both enqueue functions become callable with parameters, from anywhere: enqueueArea(bbox, …) for bbox items, enqueueCorridor(track, …) for track submissions. The PICKER is then just a caller, not part of either — build it once for corridor download, reuse it for corridor refresh. Corridor stacks per-track submissions.

⭐ The payoff: every download origin already routes through ConvoyDownloadConfirm → submitDownload. If refresh routes through the same picker and the same two enqueue functions, there stops being a separate refresh code path at all — refresh becomes "download, with replace on." That kills the gridCells problem rather than migrating it.

  • ⚠ replace is a PARAMETER, not a separate function. Refresh re-fetches tiles already on disk, bypassing the hasTile pre-fetch filter. ⛔ And that flag is the same refreshMode field that was deleting completions until 08-05 — verify entries survive when the new refresh path passes it.
  • ⚠ The estimate-and-confirm gate belongs in the PICKER, once — both callers go through it. One track was 561 tiles; select-all across a track list times the map slots is how 10 tracks became 215 jobs.
  • ⚠ The AREA path still needs gridCells → segmentCells — corridor re-derives at run time and never builds oversized cells, so keeping area as an option keeps that defect live.
  • ⚠ Compare enqueueRefresh's argument order against enqueueArea while in there — swapped bounds is the leading unverified hypothesis for the 07-23 continental incident, and this work touches that call site.
  • ⛔ Must work in a FRESH PROCESS. WorkManager jobs survive app death; the worker's ensureInit() re-reads config from scratch. The column file is what makes that safe — which is why step 3 was placed before this.
  • OPEN: the picker lists TRACKS or TRAILS? Corridor derivation runs off track geometry (getTrackPoints, corridorTiles); trails would need a different path. Settle before writing.

🌙 00-TONIGHT (08-03 late) — z10 REVERTED · PHASE 1 VERIFIED · FALLBACK REBUILT

⛔⛔ READ BEFORE 00-TODAY BELOW. Two things recorded there were overtaken within hours: trails z10 was REVERTED, and phase 1 was tested and a real bug found in it. 00-TODAY is retained for the trail; where the two disagree, this section wins.

⛔ TRAILS z10 → 11 — REVERTED. ANRs ON DEVICE.

Fred observed ANR messages at z10. Reverted to 11 — TRAILS-Z10-REVERTED-ANR-2026-08-03.
⭐⭐ THIS CLOSES THE z10 QUESTION — it is NOT a repeat of 07-31. That rejection was measured uncapped on L.svg. This attempt was capped at 10,000 rows on L.canvas — both objections addressed — and it still ANRs. So the constraint is neither DOM construction nor an uncapped result set: it is the ROW COUNT ITSELF at a z10 viewport, where the area is ~4× z11. ⛔ Do not attempt z10 again without a fundamentally different mechanism (vector tiles / MapLibre — that is 2.7).
⚠ CORRECTION to a claim made earlier today: the z10 patch comment asserted canvas fails on frame time rather than ANR. Device evidence says canvas ANRs too.
⚠ THE PLAY BUILD HAD z10 IN IT — uploaded 08-03 13:03, replacing 2.6d (29320746). Corrected by tonight's rebuild. ⛔ The v2.6e-fallback tag was MOVED off a385c2221 (the ANR build) onto dd8370e37. A fallback that ANRs is not a fallback.
⛔ DO NOT lower the row cap as an alternative to raising the gate. At z11 trails currently all fit; lowering the cap makes them disappear at z11 too. If z11 ever misbehaves, try 12 before touching the cap — raising the gate costs nothing at zooms that already work.

✅ COLUMN FILE PHASE 1 — VERIFIED ON BOTH DEVICES

Commit dd8370e37, tag v2.6e-fallback, pushed. Checks 1, 3 and 4 all pass. No FALLBACK REQUESTED on either device — the ordering hazard is not firing on this path.
Verified file, Droid 2: version_code 29320781, active "SAT", three columns with real templates and status "active". Cold restart logs column file current (vc=…), no install pass — the correct branch.
⭐ THE CATALOGUE HAS 15 SOURCES, three assigned to columns — so twelve are unassigned and available to the selector. Google is a first-class catalogue entry (google-satellite), not something to hand-add. That matters for step 3.

⛔ A REAL BUG IN PHASE 1 — FOUND BY CHECK 4, FIXED, VERIFIED

SYMPTOM: changing a source logged wrote 3 columns AND Slot SAT updated to source google-satellite — and the file still read esri-imagery-overlays. The mutation happened, the write happened, and the change was still discarded.
CAUSE (Claude's design error): saveExternalJson() passed readColumnFileOrNull() as existing, so writeColumnFile() applied its INSTALL-PASS rule — priorById[slot.legacyKey] ?: slot.sourceId. priorById is built from the file ON DISK, which still held the OLD source. Every save let the stale on-disk value overwrite the fresh selection.
⭐ THE DISTINCTION THAT MATTERS: "prior assignment wins" is CORRECT for the INSTALL PASS — a release may retire a source but must never repoint a column the user chose. It is exactly WRONG for the SAVE PATH, where the user just changed it and defaultSlots IS the truth. Fix (COLUMNFILE-SAVEFIX-2026-08-03): the save path passes null. Install pass untouched.
✅ VERIFIED on Droid 2: slot 1 now writes google-satellite with url_template https://mt{s}.google.com/vt/lyrs=s&x={x}&y={y}&z={z} — carrying the {s} placeholder from the 08-02 fix.
⭐⭐ THE LESSON: checks 1–3 ALL PASSED AND THE FILE LOOKED CORRECT. Only changing a source exposed it. A phase built to be "write-only, safe to commit" would have been committed broken. Check 4 existed precisely to catch this, and it did.
⚠ MINOR, NOT FIXED: three wrote 3 columns lines per interaction — both setActive() and updateSlotSource() call saveExternalJson(), and entering Map Sources appears to trigger setActive on its own. Harmless (atomic, idempotent) but more disk traffic than needed. Tidy when next in there.

⭐⭐⭐ 00-DISPLAY — THE TELESCOPE MODEL (settled 08-03 evening). NOT YET BUILT.

⭐ THIS IS THE DISPLAY PHILOSOPHY THE APP HAS BEEN MISSING. Seven items, one coherent build. Everything below follows from one idea: zoom is the density control, not the artifact panel.

THE WORKFLOW (Fred, verbatim intent): (1) find your area — search by name, or your own tracks; (2) look at tracks around it — where you have actually ridden; (3) drop waypoints — the points of interest; (4) NOW turn trails on — the palette, at the scale you chose. ⭐ Trails come LAST, after you know what you want.
⭐⭐ WHY: "see the ground, see the features, see the trail — not a 100 mile line on the map." At wide zoom 140,000 trails is a blob of colour with no information in it. Drilling into the yarnball, a thick blue line resolves into 30 trails, and there are hundreds of such spots. That intimidates users rather than helping them. The far view must carry enough to RECOGNISE a place by — your tracks, waypoints, town and park names — not enough to plan with.
⭐ THE INSIGHT ABOUT THE PANEL: "you are not using select/omit to turn trails on and off, you are using zoom to narrow what appears — the right tool for the job." The artifact panel is a participation switch, not a density filter. Thinning 3,600 trails by picking items is the wrong instrument. That is why the toggles always felt inadequate — they were being asked to do a job they were never shaped for.
THE SEVEN ITEMS: (1) Trail ceiling 5,000, flat, every zoom that displays — inert at z16/z18 where the viewport is already small; a ceiling only for z11–z12. (2) Trails toggle disabled below z11, label reads Trails (Z11 or greater to display) on the text line when ALL is not selectable. (3) Trails forced OFF on MAP CHANGE — convoy→planner and planner→convoy, in the lastMapProcessed gate. ⚠ Not on every panel entry: only crossing between the two maps clears it. (4) Viewport state still restores normally — pan and zoom come back as they do now. Only artifact visibility is overridden. Where you were is a fact worth restoring; what you had switched on is a choice worth re-making. (5) Search by track / trail / town / park name, returning a ~100 sq mi area around the point — the planning frame, not a pin. (6) No GPS snapback on the planning map after search — if search is the first move in planning, the map must stay where it was sent. (7) Manual: the telescope workflow, and "turn trails on when you have found your area."
⭐ ONE TAP TO GET IT BACK. That is what makes the reset acceptable rather than an imposition. Conservative default, trivial override, no persistence fighting the user's judgement.
⭐ TUNABLES, NAMED AND IN ONE PLACE — TRAILS_MIN_ZOOM and TRAILS_MAX_ROWS. ⚠ At ~39 minutes a build, tuning by rebuild is expensive: a TEMPORARY dev control to move both values live would let a full tuning pass happen in one session on real ground. ⛔ It comes out in the commit that closes the task — that is the instrumentation lesson, not a cleanup-list item.
⚠ ARGUED AND REJECTED — a permanent user-facing "max trails" number. It asks the rider to solve a problem they did not create and cannot reason about (what number? ordered by what? re-spent per viewport?), and it makes the yarnball their fault for setting it wrong. If ever exposed, use words not digits — Fewer / Balanced / More — so the rider expresses a preference they can judge.
⚠ Most planners will settle at z12–z13, so the yarnball case is rarer than it looks — z11 is the boundary where trails are admissible, not where people will live. ⭐ And scale of ride sets the zoom: "nobody wants to travel 100 miles and plan it at z13." Be permissive above z11; give the boundary and get out of the way.

⭐⭐⭐ 00-ENDSTATE — SUGGEST. Fred's stated vision for the product.

THE VISION (Fred 08-03): "one of the unfinished buttons is just that — set your points of interest, your waypoint to embark and return from, the range of distance and time, and let the software recommend routes that touch all your points. That is my vision for endstate." ⭐ That is the Suggest button already sitting in the Route panel marked "soon".
THE PRODUCT, IN ONE LINE (Fred): "the route map is the palette — the routes and trails and the infinite combinations to create a unique experience visiting points of interest." ⭐ A palette only works if you can see the colours. That is what 00-DISPLAY exists to protect.
WHY IT IS CLOSER THAN IT LOOKS: the graph is already in the spatial DB, snapping works, and route vertices already carry lineId. What is missing is traversal — connectivity between segments at their intersections, and a cost model.
⚠ CONNECTIVITY IS THE HARD PART, NOT THE ROUTING. OSM ways are segments; whether two segments actually connect at a shared point — or merely cross without a junction — is the difference between a route and a fantasy. ⚠ TIME IS HARDER THAN DISTANCE: distance falls out of geometry, time needs a speed model per trail type and grade, and riders vary enormously. Start distance-only.
⭐⭐ THIS IS WHY TRACK-TO-TRAIL MATTERS. Tracks are certainty — you rode it, it exists. Trails are a claim — someone mapped it, sometime, with some accuracy. Three operations, different confidence: CONFIRM (track matches a trail — raises confidence, adds nothing) · APPEND (track extends past a trail's endpoint — adds geometry) · FILL (track where no trail exists — creates the trail, or the junction). ⭐ FILL is the direct feed into Suggest: routing fails on gaps, and your tracks close exactly the junctions you physically rode through. ⚠ The hard part is the same tolerance question as proximity snap — GPS wander means a track along a trail is NEAR it, not coincident with it.
⭐ THE HONEST STATEMENT, worth saying plainly to users (Fred): "between trails and tracks you know for certain what areas are known — the rest is up to you." A planner told where knowledge ends makes better decisions than one handed a confident-looking line that turns out to be a fence.

✅ THE PLANNER FIT BUG — CLOSED. Proximity removal fixed it.

SYMPTOM (open at 08-02 close): fit-to-artifact on the PLANNING map rendered nothing, and the fit produced ZERO logcat — no JSONDIAG, no MapJS, from a live PID. Convoy performed the identical fit correctly every time. Exiting to convoy and returning cured it.
WHAT WAS ACTUALLY WRONG: grouptrack_map.html carried a third copy of the proximity block — a bare map.on('click', …) at ~:801, at the script block's TOP LEVEL. map is not created until ~:640, inside a DOMContentLoaded → setTimeout(500) callback. So :801 evaluated at PARSE TIME against an undefined variable and threw Uncaught TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'on') on every single page load. The comment block at :642–655 documents the SAME defect being found and relocated on 07-30 — this copy was left behind.
WHY THE 08-02 THREE-TAP READING WAS BACKWARDS: that test concluded "handler-js-proximity-scan never fired once — the JS scan is not in the live path." True, but not because it was inert by design — its registration threw before it could attach. It was dead because it was broken.
THE PHANTOM POPUPS — a list of trails popping on a blank map — came from the OTHER proximity path: Android.onProximityTap → Kotlin queries the spatial DB → showProximityPopup. The query layer never consults visibility state, so it happily reported artifacts the map was not drawing.

⛔⛔ THE MECHANISM BY WHICH THIS FIXED FIT IS NOT ESTABLISHED. A def-scan of the deleted range found only checkLayer, nested inside the handler — nothing the fit path needs. The removal was justified on its own merits (dead code throwing every load); fit working afterwards is an observed result with no proven cause. This is recorded honestly rather than dressed up. ⚠ Do not re-add a top-level handler to that file without understanding why.

⚠ Fred called the direction on this one — "every fiber of my body blames proximity" — while my elimination of that code range was wrong twice. Recorded because the reasoning pattern matters more than the fix.

⭐⭐⭐ 00-GOOGLE (Fred 08-03) — THE HYB SEQUENCE, RE-SPECIFIED IN SIX STEPS

⛔ THIS SUPERSEDES THE FIX 1 / FIX 2 / FIX 3 FRAMING IN 00-TOMORROW. Two assumptions under the old framing were wrong — see the corrections below.

1 · SLOT/SOURCE SELECTION INTO A USER JSON in the maps directory. On install/init: JSON present → use it; absent → create it from the system defaults as the starting point, then use it. Seed only when absent. Persist only {slot, source_id, legacy_key} + the active key — never the sources array.
2 · ENSURE THE APP REFERENCES THE USER JSON to resolve the column/slot source. Nothing may resolve from default_slots at run time once the user file exists. This is what makes a fresh-process WorkManager job resolve the same slot the job was queued against.
3 · TEST A MAP DOWNLOAD USING GOOGLE. ⭐ Fred put the test HERE — before any download-shape work — so HYB is measured against ground that has stopped moving. Steps 1–2 are the stabiliser.
4 · SECOND CHECKBOX BESIDE "AREA" ON MAP DOWNLOADS. Area → draw box (existing). Corridor → brings up a LIST OF TRACKS: select all, or check as many as needed.
5 · REFRESH BECOMES AREA OR CORRIDOR — AND THE USER CHOOSES.

⛔ CORRECTION (Fred 08-03): the 08-02 entry recording "CORRIDOR IS THE DEFAULT" is WRONG as written. Fred: "during the refresh the user must select if he prefers area or corridor imports." It is a choice presented at refresh time, not a default the app applies. ⚠ Record it this way or a future session builds a silent default and the prompt never appears.

6 · ROUTE THE CHOICE to enqueueArea or the corridor enqueue, submitting download jobs for the changed source.

⭐⭐ 00-GOOGLE-DETAIL — FUNCTIONALITY PER STEP (settled 08-03, build to this)

⭐ STEP 1 IS WRITTEN AND COMPILED — patch_column_file_phase1b_2026-08-03.py, marker COLUMNFILE-2026-08-03, compileGoogleReleaseKotlin BUILD SUCCESSFUL 2m04s. Not committed, not device-tested. Full detail + test plan in grouptrack_handoff_2026-08-03.html.

STEP 1 · COLUMN FILE — CREATE AND MAINTAIN (write-only)

FILE: Documents/GroupTrack/map_slots.json, beside api_keys.json. ⛔ NOT map_sources.json — externalJsonFile() at :66 already claims that name and it is the file 79251ae72 deliberately killed. Different name, different meaning.
CONTENTS: version_code, active, and three column rows — {slot, legacy_key, source_id, short_label, url_template, requires_key, status}. ⚠ Stores the URL TEMPLATE, never a resolved URL. resolveUrl() at :277 injects {key} at use time and keys persist separately. ⛔ Never the sources array — that is what made 79251ae72 necessary.
RULES, in precedence order: (a) fallback ran → NO WRITE AT ALL, log and return — hardcoded data must never reach the user table by any route; (b) unreadable → LOG AND STOP, no seed, no overwrite (unreadable ≠ absent; treating it as absent is the create-if-missing shape that destroyed the spatial DB on 08-01); (c) absent → SEED from defaultSlots + catalogue, stamp the version code; (d) present, same version code → LOAD ONLY, no install pass; (e) present, different version code → INSTALL PASS.
THE INSTALL PASS: refresh url_template per column from the catalogue by source_id; mark status: "terminated" where the source is gone; re-stamp. ⛔ SLOT ASSIGNMENTS ARE NEVER TOUCHED — priorById wins over slot.sourceId. A release may retire a source; it may not repoint a column the user chose. ⭐ The installer expires the source and stops. Notification is somebody else's task (Fred).
PERSISTS: updateSlotSource() already called saveExternalJson() at :221 — an empty body, now filled. setActive() also persists; the active key was in-memory only, defaulting to "SAT" at :53. Atomic write, temp-then-rename.
⚠ DECISION OWED — what "terminated" means. The patch marks a column terminated when its source_id is not found. Rule 3 calls for an explicit tombstone — a status field on the catalogue entry so retired sources stay in the list and the sequence holds. Asset change plus one line here. Absence works today only because nothing has been retired yet.

STEP 2 · THE APP READS THE COLUMN FILE

GOAL: nothing resolves from default_slots at run time once the user file exists. defaultSlots becomes SEED-ONLY.
⭐⭐ THIS IS SMALLER THAN IT LOOKS. All six readers — :153 getSlotSources, :163 getDownloadSources, :172 getSourceByKey, :187 getAllOverlayLayers, :215 updateSlotSource, :273 getSlotAssignments — funnel through one mutable field. You change where that field is loaded from; you do not convert the call sites. And ConvoyConfig.TILE_SOURCES at :17-19 is already a computed property with no cache, so every consumer inherits the fix automatically.
ALSO IN STEP 2 — RECONCILIATION ON INIT: walk the columns, check each source_id against the catalogue, expire any pointing at a retired or missing source. This cannot live in step 1 because nothing reads the file there.
ALSO OWED — the orphan local. ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt:97: var activeSource by remember { mutableStateOf("SAT") } — never synced to the global, and it drives the map-bar highlight through isActive = activeSource == label in ConvoyMapBar.kt:78. ⚠ The UI can show SAT highlighted while the source is something else. Convoy has no equivalent — it passes mapTypeLabel. Line :342 (pmActiveSource) IS seeded from the global; :97 is not.
⭐ FREE MEASUREMENT AFTER STEP 2: retest the refresh "reset" (5%→4%). If it stops, the queue was never the problem — a job re-resolving mid-run and restarting its accounting against a different tile set would look exactly like a percentage going backwards. That retires a P0-shaped item at no cost.

STEP 3 · TEST A GOOGLE DOWNLOAD

GOAL: measure HYB against ground that has stopped moving. ⭐ Fred placed the test here deliberately, before any download-shape work.
⭐⭐ DIAGNOSTIC EITHER WAY. Failure disappears → resolution was the cause. Failure persists → resolution is eliminated for good and you are debugging a real download problem on solid ground. There is no outcome that wastes the step.
INSTRUMENTATION DISCIPLINE: start the capture before queueing; remove TRACK-DBG: TICK first (twice every 5 s, it destroyed the evidence last time); Log.d only — the worker is off the main thread and a Toast there is the 08-01 failure again; nothing in an argument position. ⭐ Log the RESOLVED URL per job — that single line separates slot-resolution from a genuine download failure. ⛔ Remove it in the commit that closes the task.
DURABLE: put the failure cause in the queue entry, not only the log. "CORR SAT failed" with no reason is what turned five minutes into an evening of reconstruction.
CANDIDATE MECHANISM (Fred, hypothesis): "esri would not work when the connection was established with google." If the downloader holds a client for the Google host and the slot re-resolves mid-run to an Esri template on a different host, in-flight requests aim at the wrong endpoint and fail silently. ⚠ Unknown whether the downloader keeps a persistent client per host or builds per tile — per-tile would mean contamination rather than failure. Both disappear once resolution is stable.

STEP 4 · CORRIDOR CHECKBOX ON THE DOWNLOAD PANEL

GOAL: a second checkbox beside Area. Area → draw box (existing). Corridor → a list of tracks: select all, or check as many as needed.
⭐ ONE PLACE TO BUILD IT. Every download origin already routes through ConvoyDownloadConfirm → DownloadQueueManager.submitDownload (V2.6 unification), so area, corridor and refresh all inherit the same behaviour with no per-origin handling. A decision from weeks ago making today's work smaller.
⚠ SELECT-ALL NEEDS AN ESTIMATE-AND-CONFIRM GATE. Selecting every track is a large job even when done correctly — the user must see the count before it queues.
TERMINATED COLUMNS ARE UNCHECKED AND NOT SELECTABLE for new downloads or refresh. The flag lives in the column file so the panel and the selector both read it. ⚠ Show a message, not just a greyed box — otherwise "why did my download come back smaller" becomes a bug report.

STEP 5 · REFRESH BECOMES AREA OR CORRIDOR — THE USER CHOOSES

GOAL: refresh asks which method at refresh time. ⛔ NOT a default the app applies — see the correction below.
⛔⛔ CORRIDOR REFRESH MUST BE BUILT. IT DOES NOT EXIST. Fred: "the refresh does not currently support corridor imports." This is BUILD, not MIGRATE — the 08-02 wording "reuses proven code" understates it badly.
⭐ THE TRACK-LIST PICKER IS SHARED WITH STEP 4. Build it once for download, reuse it when refresh asks.
REPLACE-IN-PLACE IS THE MODEL (Fred). A source change submits a refresh of the column store with replace=yes at low priority, so current maps stay usable offline while they are being swapped. INSERT OR REPLACE reuses pages — which is why the 08-02 signal was mtime advancing with size unchanged. ⚠ Convergence depends on the refresh COMPLETING. A half-swapped store has no record of which tiles are which, so there is no way to resume intelligently. That is what makes "refresh must finish reliably" a correctness item, not tidiness.
⚠ PRECONDITION ON SLOT REASSIGNMENT: queue on hold AND no active job on that column. Hold alone is insufficient — it stops dispatch, not a worker already running. Block the reassignment, not the navigation — opening Map Sources to look must stay safe; committing a change to a column with live work is what is refused, with the reason named.

STEP 6 · ROUTE THE CHOICE TO THE RIGHT ENQUEUE

GOAL: enqueueArea or the corridor enqueue, submitting jobs for the changed source.
⚠ STILL OWED ALONGSIDE — gridCells → segmentCells ON THE AREA PATH. ⭐ The corridor worker re-derives its tiles at run time from track geometry, so it never builds oversized cells — the migration is an AREA-path problem only. Area stays as an option, so it is still owed.
⚠ CHECK enqueueRefresh's ARGUMENT ORDER against enqueueArea — swapped bounds remains the leading unverified hypothesis for the 07-23 continental incident, and the migration touches that call site anyway.

⛔ THE ORDERING HAZARD — ensureInit() / loadFallback() (found 08-03)

WHAT IT IS: ensureInit() at :282-286 calls loadFallback() when not initialized — and loadFallback() sets initialized = true at :147. init() starts with if (initialized) return at :72.
⛔⛔ CONSEQUENCE: any getter firing before init(context) loads hardcoded Esri and sets the flag — then the real init() returns immediately and never reads the asset. The whole process runs on hardcoded Esri, announced by one Log.w nobody is watching for. That looks exactly like the reported symptom: slot 1 back on Esri, no explanation. It is an ORDERING bug, independent of persistence.
⭐ FRED'S CALL — do not remove it blind: "sounds like a trap … i am afraid of why it is there." Phase 1 instead logs the request with a stack trace naming the caller and refuses to write the column file at all when fallback ran. Nothing removed, nothing commented, no risk. If it fires, the caller names itself. ⚠ Watch logcat for COLUMNFILE-2026-08-03: FALLBACK REQUESTED.

⚠ RESOLUTION IS LATE — the job carries a slot name, not a URL (proven 08-03)

ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt:706 writes putString("refresh_slot", …); ConvoyCorridorWorker.kt:52 and ConvoyDownloadWorker.kt:39 read it back. The worker resolves slot → source → URL at EXECUTION time.
⛔ THE DANGEROUS OUTCOME IS NOT FAILURE, IT IS QUIET SUCCESS. A slot that changes between queue and execution silently redirects the download, and stores are keyed by the layer's cache_dir — so the wrong tiles land in the store you believe holds the right ones. No error to notice.
⭐ THIS IS WHY THE COLUMN FILE IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT MERELY CORRECT. With the mapping stable, late resolution becomes correct rather than hazardous — the worker resolves the same column to the same source whenever it runs, regardless of process or navigation.

⚠ THE VERIFICATION LOOP WAS THE CONTAMINATION LOOP (Fred 08-03)

Fred's method for confirming tiles were being replaced was to view offline maps of that column — which required switching sources to flush the cache. Switching sources is what triggers the re-resolve. So checking whether the swap was working is what broke the swap, and repeating the check repeated the damage.
⚠ UNTIL STEP 2 LANDS, VERIFY WITHOUT A SOURCE SWITCH. Store mtime advancing with size steady was the clean signal on 08-02 — no UI interaction, no re-resolve.

⛔ TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE RECORD (Fred 08-03) — read before touching refresh

1 · THE 215 JOBS WERE NOT A SUBMIT-PER-TRACK. Fred: "the import by track does not exist in area refresh. all track boundaries became bboxes and that is how we ended up with 215 jobs." Refresh converted each track BOUNDARY into a BBOX and enqueued area jobs from them. ⭐ Why this matters: a switchbacked trail's bounding box covers the whole mountain; its corridor covers a line buffer. That difference IS the 215 jobs — and it is the same reason corridor downloads were built on 07-24.
2 · CORRIDOR REFRESH DOES NOT EXIST. Fred: "the refresh does not currently support corridor imports." ⛔ So step 5 is BUILD, not MIGRATE. The 08-02 wording — "route refresh through the track selection process, reuses proven code" — understates it. ⭐ Follow-on: the corridor worker re-derives its tiles at run time from track geometry, so it never builds oversized cells. The gridCells → segmentCells migration is an AREA-path problem only, and area stays as an option, so that migration is still owed. ⭐ Step 4 and step 5 share the same track-list picker — build it once for download, reuse it when refresh asks.
STILL WANTED regardless: the estimate-and-confirm gate on select-all (selecting every track is a large job even when done correctly — the user should see the count before it queues), and a check of enqueueRefresh's argument order against enqueueArea — swapped bounds remains the leading unverified hypothesis for the 07-23 continental incident.

⚠ VERSION CODE — EXPOSURE NARROWED (Fred 08-03), not closed

Fred: "our exposure for the release is my sideloads. nobody else is sideloading. playstore publishing protects the regression." Correct — Play rejects a version code at or below what is published, so the regression cannot reach a tester through the store. Sideload exposure is closed operationally by the plain -r rule: without -d, a regressed code makes the install FAIL loudly instead of silently downgrading. ⚠ What remains: the AAB UPLOAD itself would be rejected if the git-derived code has gone backwards relative to the published one. Annoyance, not data risk. One grep settles it: grep -rn "gitVersionProvider\|VERSION_CODE_OFFSET\|vcOffset" --include=*.kts .

2 · THEN THE GOOGLE HYBRID DOWNLOAD WORK — 2–3 days, three fixes, dependency-ordered

FIX 1 · SLOT→SOURCE SELECTION MAINTAINED IN A USER JSON. On init: user slot file present → use it; absent → seed from default_slots in the asset, then use it. ⚠ Seed ONLY when absent — re-seeding on every install recreates the bug. ⚠ Persist ONLY {slot, source_id, legacy_key} + the active key, never the sources array (that is what made 79251ae72 necessary).

⭐⭐ THIS IS THE STABILISER AND IT GOES FIRST. Fred: "We need to stabilize so our testing does not have the ground shifting underneath." If slot 1 silently reverts between runs, every download result is untrustworthy — you cannot tell a real failure from a source that changed underneath you. Route mode cost days for exactly this reason.

FIX 2 · MAP REFRESH MUST USE THE NEW BBOX ENQUEUE SIZE. enqueueRefresh is still on the OLD gridCells (fixed square-mile cells) instead of segmentCells (≤50K tile ceiling) — that is what produced 568 entries at 60–70K tiles each on 08-02. ⚠ While in there, compare enqueueRefresh's argument order against enqueueArea — swapped bounds is the leading unverified hypothesis for the 07-23 continental incident, and the migration touches that call site anyway. Reads: ConvoyMapSourceScreen.kt:255-290 and ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt:218-250.
FIX 3 · REFRESH BY CORRIDOR *OR* BY BBOX AREA, BOTH THROUGH THE NEW ENQUEUE, WORKING IN A FRESH PROCESS. Corridor = submission of maps for ALL KNOWN TRACKS (and it becomes the DEFAULT); area = the bbox path for users who downloaded areas.

⭐⭐ "IN A FRESH PROCESS" IS THE KEY PHRASE. WorkManager jobs survive app death and can start with no app process alive, so the worker's ensureInit() re-reads config from scratch. FIX 1 IS WHAT MAKES FIX 3 CORRECT — without the user slot file, a fresh-process worker resolves slot 1 from default_slots (Esri) regardless of what the user selected when the job was queued.

⚠ A select-all-tracks refresh needs an ESTIMATE-AND-CONFIRM GATE — the import path does one submitDownload per track with no estimate, which is how 10 tracks became 215 jobs. ⛔ CAUSE CORRECTED 08-03 (Fred): that is NOT what happened. Refresh does not do a submit-per-track. It converted every TRACK BOUNDARY into a BBOX and enqueued area jobs from them — a track's bounding box is vastly larger than its corridor, so the boxes gridded out into 215 jobs. The gate is still wanted; the reason recorded here was wrong. ⭐ Suggestion: derive the offered methods from what is actually stored — corridors present → corridor refresh; areas present → offer both. A user is then never given a button that cannot do anything.

⚠ Fred is rightly sceptical the slot revert explains the abort: "I do not know what would cause the JSON to be reread to impact Google Hybrid downloads, so I am leaving 2–3 days." It is a HYPOTHESIS, not a lead. Testable with no build: queue a Google corridor job, force-stop the app, watch whether it continues and what it writes. · ⚠ Also fix the failed-job logging in the same pass — "CORR SAT failed" with no reason recorded is unactionable, and TRACK-DBG rolls the evidence out of the buffer before it can be read.

⛔ THE LESSON OF THE WEEK — three-for-three

Every convoy rendering failure in this arc was instrumentation aborting a method before its real work:

  1. ?: return inside a toast argument killed onPageFinished — no tile setup, no persisted-frame restore, no drawPersistedState
  2. evaluateJavascript/Toast on a @JavascriptInterface background thread killed onViewportChanged — no processViewport
  3. The offline render failure, cleared by removing ten more toasts from the same file

All three landed in or around onPageFinished.

⚠⚠ Instrumentation is not free, and on this codebase it has cost more than the bugs it was added to find. When adding a trace to an override or a bridge method, ask what happens if that line throws or returns.

⭐ The counter-example is today's tap fix: using instrumentation that ALREADY EXISTED found the defect in three taps, with no new code and no build. Fred's call — "Why don't we use the existing instrumentation to see what tap handler is executing?" Read the logs before adding to them. ⚠ Planner console tag is MapJS; convoy's is ConvoyJS.

⚠ Also corrected today: a <script src="…"> tag NEVER executes its inline body. grouptrack_map.html had an entire proximity scan inside one — dead since written, removed (lines 11–58). ⛔ This invalidates the standing "two proximity scans racing per tap" note — one was never executable.

📜 00-PLAN (08-02 EVENING) — THE THREE-ISSUE PLAN (all three now addressed; retained for the trail)

Fred 08-02: "Three issues left before fall back. Then we build fall back — days to solve Google download issue."

1 · ANDROID STARTUP WITH NO INTERNET — a RENDERING issue, not positioning. See §00-OFFLINE.
2 · HIDE OF ARTIFACTS NOT BEING DISPLAYED — a type turned OFF still produces popups on tap. See §00-TAP.

→ THEN BUILD THE FALLBACK AAB. Certify, tag the commit, archive APK+AAB off the build directory so a Gradle clean cannot take it. → THEN the Google download issue, with days rather than hours allotted.

✅ 00-ROUTE+ DONE (08-02) — MERGED PANEL SHIPPED AND TESTED

DONEThree chained dialogs collapsed to one. Fred's original complaint: "We have a panel ahead that says continue, then this panel which says WIPs or new track, then select a track with a delete or cancel. Very confusing."

The panel now:

Start a new route ───────────────────────────────────────────── Test Route Aug 1 · 14 pts [Continue] [Rename] [Delete] Auto Saved… Aug 2 · 8 pts [Continue] [Rename] [Delete] ───────────────────────────────────────────── [ + Plan a New Route ] ← blocked while the autosave exists [ Cancel ]
  • Rename lives ONLY in the panel. Both resolver callers (planner entry and Route+ launch) had their forced-rename prompts removed — they were popping a rename immediately before opening a panel that already has a Rename button.
  • Planner-entry toast is a COUNT, not a list: 2 unfinished routes - 1 needs a name - tap +ROUTE. ⚠ Android 11+ clips text toasts to about two lines, so a multi-line list was being truncated with an ellipsis regardless of length — the log confirmed inventory 2 draft(s) while only one showed. A toast cannot carry a list. The panel is the list.
  • Rename opens BLANK for the autosave rather than pre-filling "Auto Saved In Progress".
  • Cancel AND tap-outside both disarm route mode. routeMode = true fires at +ROUTE before any choice is made, so every exit from the panel must disarm — not just the button.
  • Rename re-prompts on a taken name (isNameTaken checks drafts AND the routes DB) instead of silently failing.

✅ ALL 19 ROUTE-MODE INSTRUMENTATION TOASTS REMOVED from both files — convoy 10, planner 9 — with all eight setRouteMode state writes verified intact. The RESOLVER: logs were deliberately kept: the under-2-point silent delete needs its trace, as the only record a crash occurred.

⚠ Now unreachable and owed a cleanup: the showDraftResolve dialog block and the old recoveryPending "Route recovery" dialog. Nothing sets either. They read as live recovery paths to anyone looking later.

⭐⭐⭐ 00-SOURCE (08-02) — SOURCE CONFIGURATION HAS NO PERSISTENCE LAYER. ROOT CAUSE FOUND.

ROOT CAUSEFred: "Something keeps placing Esri hybrid in slot 1 even after I replaced with Google." ⭐ Nothing is putting it back. The choice was never saved.

XREF EVIDENCE (field_crossref_raw.txt): MapSourceManager has exactly TWO writes — :62 (dir.mkdirs()) and :248 (apiKeysFile().writeText). The API keys file is the only thing it persists. Slot fields are read-only: :113 slot, :114 source_id, :115 legacy_key — all [R], never written back.

DEVICE EVIDENCE: adb shell find /sdcard -name "map_sources.json" returns nothing. The external override the code anticipates at MapSourceManager.kt:66 does not exist.

⭐ ONE MISSING LAYER, THREE SYMPTOMS: (1) slot 1 reverting to Esri; (2) activeSourceKey resetting to SAT on every restart (declared = "SAT", in-memory only, no disk read anywhere); (3) possibly the Google download abort itself — Fred: "May also be why Google download aborts." If the slot flips mid-run the worker's source resolves to something other than what the job was queued against, which is a plausible mechanism for an abort with no exception recorded. TEST THEM TOGETHER — if the revert timestamp lines up with the abort, one fix closes both.

⛔ IT WAS BUILT, THEN DELIBERATELY REMOVED — AND WHY MATTERS

git log -S "externalJsonFile" returns exactly two commits:

  • 49bc8cecb — V2.5 Map Source Selection, "updated MapSourceManager with external JSON" — ADDED it
  • 79251ae72 — 2026-05-21, "V2.5: Fix tile download — read asset JSON only" — REMOVED it

79251ae72 deleted both halves: the read-external-first-with-copy-on-first-run block in init (including extFile.writeText(assetJson)), and the entire body of saveExternalJson() — replaced with // Disabled — asset is single source of truth. ⚠ The empty function still exists and is still called, silently doing nothing.

⭐⭐ WHY IT WAS REMOVED VINDICATES THE FIX. The external file held the ENTIRE CATALOGUE, URLs included — saveExternalJson did root.put("default_slots", …) on a full copy of the catalog JSON. So a stale external copy served stale URLs and broke tile downloads, which is exactly what that commit was fixing. Ripping it out was a correct fix for a real bug.

⭐⭐ THE ARCHITECTURE (Fred 08-02) — TWO STRUCTURES, TWO FUNCTIONS

Fred: "There are two structures — the CATALOG which should be distributed, and the SLOT ASSIGNMENT of catalog to user's map slot. Two separate functions."

CATALOG = map_sources.json in assets. Sources, url_templates, layers, default_slots, catalog_version/schema_version. Distributed with the app, read-only, replaced every release. ⭐ This is what makes a URL correction reach existing users — the {s} fix lives in a template here. Had the catalogue been copied to user space on first run, no existing user would ever receive it.
SLOT ASSIGNMENT = which catalog source_id occupies each user map slot, plus the active slot. User-owned, persisted, survives reinstall. Fred: "The slot assignment is a JSON that should exist in user space and be populated from default asset on install."

Why two structures rather than one copied file: a single copied file needs a merge on every catalog_version bump — take new sources from the asset, preserve the user's slots. More code, more to go wrong, same outcome. Separate structures never overlap, so there is nothing to merge and nothing to go stale.

⛔ Do NOT restore 49bc8cecb as-was — that reintroduces the stale-URL bug. Restore the mechanism, but persist only {slot, source_id, legacy_key} plus the active key, never the sources array. · ⚠ Seed-on-install needs a guard: seed only if the user file is ABSENT. Re-seeding on every install recreates exactly the bug being fixed. · default_slots is named correctly and behaves as named — they ARE defaults; the bug is that no user layer sits above them.

⭐⭐ 00-HYB (08-02) — GOOGLE HYBRID WAS NEVER DEAD. IT WAS A MISSING {s}.

FIXED + VERIFIED ON DEVICEHYB had been written off as a withdrawn service and was sitting in this checklist as "DECIDE: disable behind a marked constant, or ship with a known-issue note." That was wrong. It was two separate bugs with one root cause, and both are now fixed.

⭐ The URLs are NOT in Kotlin. ConvoyConfig.kt:15 — "TILE_SOURCES — reads from MapSourceManager (map_sources.json)". That is why grep lyrs= across app/src returns NOTHING, and it sent two people looking in the wrong place. Asset: app/src/main/assets/map_sources.json.

The two bugs

Template: https://mt{s}.google.com/vt/lyrs=y&x={x}&y={y}&z={z}. Five sources use {s}: google-hybrid, -satellite, -roads, -terrain, cartodb-voyager. Everything else (all Esri, USGS, OpenTopoMap, OSM) uses literal hostnames — which is why only the Google sources looked dead.

BUG 1 — DOWNLOAD PATH. ConvoyTileDownloader.buildTileUrl substituted {z}/{x}/{y} and nothing else. {s} was handled nowhere — grep across all Kotlin returned zero hits. The literal string mt{s}.google.com went to DNS and every tile failed silently. Fix (SUBDOMAIN-2026-08-02): .replace("{s}", (((tile.x + tile.y) % 4 + 4) % 4).toString()) as the first replace. Double-modulo guards negative x at antimeridian wrap; % 4 gives mt0–mt3.
BUG 2 — DISPLAY PATH. Leaflet DOES expand {s}, but its default subdomain set is 'abc' → it requested mta/mtb/mtc.google.com, which do not exist. Neither map set a subdomains option. Fix (SUBDOMAINS-2026-08-02): subdomains: (url.indexOf('google.com') !== -1 ? '0123' : 'abc') on the tile layer in BOTH convoy_map.html (~:47) and grouptrack_map.html (~:111). Derived from the URL, not hardcoded — Google needs 0123, Carto needs abc.

Verified: display logs TILE URL: https://mt{s}.google.com/... LABEL: SAT with no TILE ERROR after it. Download: SAT.mbtiles mtime advanced with size unchanged (INSERT OR REPLACE reuses pages). Offline: ConvoyIntercept: TILE mbtiles hit=true at z17/z18 — Google tiles rendering from the store.

⚠ The subdomains option is still missing on the OVERLAY layers (convoy_map.html ~:71, grouptrack_map.html ~:138). Inert today; a subdomain'd overlay source would fail the same way. · ⭐ METHOD: what settled this was curl-ing the endpoint by hand (200, image/jpeg, 29,722 bytes) — proving the service was alive BEFORE theorising about the app. Ten seconds. Do it first next time.

📊 MEASURED STORE SIZES — the 66% figure was wrong

StoreBytes
SAT.mbtiles4,240,744,448 (4.24 GB)
SAT_LABELS_PLACES.mbtiles210,006,016 (210 MB)
SAT_LABELS_TRANSPORT.mbtiles251,092,992 (251 MB)
TOPO.mbtiles / TOPO+.mbtiles367 MB / 49 MB

⭐⭐ Retiring both label stores saves 461 MB of 4.70 GB — about 9.8%, NOT 66%. The two-thirds figure came from TILE COUNT; label tiles are mostly transparent and sparse, so they are two thirds of the count but a tenth of the BYTES. This materially weakens the case for disabling WebP.

⚠ Google Hybrid tiles are DENSER than bare Esri imagery (roads + labels baked in), so SAT.mbtiles may GROW as it is replaced. Re-measure after a completed run before deciding anything.

The real argument for HYB was never storage: one store instead of three, and street names without MapLibre. SAT_LABELS_TRANSPORT was the piece parked to 2.7 — HYB delivers it now. That is what could move places-from-spatial OUT of 2.6e entirely rather than merely deferring it.

⚠ Google tiles are already JPEG. Re-encoding JPEG → WebP is generation loss on generation loss — an argument for dropping WebP independent of the volume math. Possible future: WebP as a selectable DOWNLOAD option (not per-map — stores are shared across both maps; and it leaves mixed encodings in a store, so confirm the reader handles all formats).

⛔ SOURCE SELECTION DOES NOT PERSIST

OPENMapSourceManager.kt:53 — var activeSourceKey: String = "SAT". In-memory ONLY. Written at :193, read by ConvoyConfig.ACTIVE_TILE_SOURCE (a getter). There is NO read from disk anywhere.

The selection survives exactly as long as the process. Any force-stop, reinstall or background reclaim silently drops the user back to the Esri default. Fred 08-02: "WE HAVE AN ISSUE WITH MAP SOURCE BEING RESET."

⚠⚠ This CONTAMINATES test results: if the source resets mid-download the job keeps writing into the same store with the OTHER source's tiles. Any conclusion drawn from SAT.mbtiles after a reset is suspect.

Fix: persist on write, read in init. Small, but it is a behaviour change on a shared object — its own patch, not bundled. ⚠ Distinguish SLOT ASSIGNMENT (applySource, probably persisted) from ACTIVE KEY.

⚠⚠ ESRI AND GOOGLE SHARE ONE SAT.mbtiles — SLOT-KEYED, NOT SOURCE-KEYED

Google Hybrid occupies the SAT slot (log reads LABEL: SAT while the URL is mt{s}.google.com). Both sources write into the SAME file. Nothing in a stored tile records which source produced it.

Consequences for the swap/prune design: (1) a source swap CONTAMINATES rather than replaces — refresh only rewrites tiles in the refreshed geography at the covered zooms, so the store becomes a permanent Esri/Google patchwork; (2) a clean volume comparison is impossible from a mixed file; (3) the prune is not just "drop the two label stores" — the base store needs clearing before a swap, then refilling. MBTilesStore.deleteSource() already exists and drops the whole file.

Fred's prune design stands otherwise: leave the overlay wiring intact, empty the label stores, and the overlay draws nothing. No conditional logic, Esri stays selectable, stores refill on demand — so it is reversible. Order matters: config first (drop from getDownloadSources()), THEN deleteSource(). Reversed, the next download refills what you just emptied.

🔴 00-REFRESH (08-02) — REFRESH IS STILL ON THE BBOX MODEL AND THE OLD SEGMENTATION

Downloads cut over to corridors on 07-24. Refresh was never migrated — still bbox, and still on the old gridCells instead of segmentCells. Same omission, two symptoms.

INCIDENT 08-02A map-source refresh enqueued 568 entries at 60–70K tiles each (~37 million tiles — would never finish). The ≤50K ceiling was not applied because enqueueRefresh does not use segmentCells. Not the 3,287-entry continental blowup of 07-23, but the same root path.

⛔ BBOX REFRESH JOBS RESET RATHER THAN COMPLETE — observed TWICE: a job at 5% went back to 4%, then a second job reset the same way. Fred: "second job just reset again." This is DISTINCT from the known refresh-never-completes stall, and it means a long run may never finish.

⭐⭐ DESIGN DECISION (Fred 08-02) — REFRESH GETS TWO METHODS

CORRIDOR IS THE DEFAULT. Route refresh through the track selection process — it reuses proven code, kills the oversized-cell problem for free (the corridor worker re-derives tiles at run time), and matches what refresh is actually for. Nobody refreshes a rectangle any more; they refresh the terrain they ride.
AREA REFRESH STAYS AS AN OPTION for users who downloaded areas. Same shape as the download side (downloadType selects the worker, launchWorker dispatches).
AND the area path MUST move from gridCells to segmentCells. Keeping the option means keeping enqueueRefresh, so the 60–70K-cell defect stays live for anyone who chose areas. Corridor-by-default only reduces how often it is hit.

⭐ Suggestion: derive the offered methods from what is actually stored — corridors present → corridor refresh; areas present → offer both. A user is then never given a button that cannot do anything.

⚠ A select-all-tracks refresh needs an estimate-and-confirm gate. The import path already does one submitDownload per track with NO estimate and NO confirm — that is how 10 tracks became 215 jobs. ⛔ CAUSE CORRECTED 08-03 (Fred): the import path is not what did this. Refresh turned each track boundary into a bbox and enqueued area jobs — the bbox of a switchbacked trail covers the whole mountain while its corridor covers a line buffer. That difference IS the 215 jobs. Without a gate, "refresh all tracks" becomes the new way to enqueue thousands of jobs by accident. · ⚠ Fix the completion plumbing in BOTH workers — ConvoyCorridorWorker duplicates ConvoyDownloadWorker's, so the same miss can exist independently. · ⚠ While migrating, compare enqueueRefresh's argument order against enqueueArea — swapped bounds is the leading unverified hypothesis for the 07-23 continental incident, and the migration touches that call site anyway.

⚠ A FAILED QUEUE JOB RECORDS NO REASON

08-02: a corridor job failed and the entry said only "CORR SAT failed" — no exception, no HTTP status, nothing actionable. The cause was unrecoverable because TRACK-DBG: TICK (twice every 5 s) had already rolled it out of the logcat ring buffer.

Fix: put the failure cause in the queue entry AND the log. Small change; the difference between five minutes and another evening of reconstruction.

⚠ TRACK-DBG: TICK floods the buffer and actively destroys evidence. Add to the pre-release removal list alongside WAYPOINT-TRACE.

To catch it next time, start the capture BEFORE queueing: adb logcat -c then adb logcat | grep -viE "TRACK-DBG|DetailGate|NetworkStateTracker|WM-|chromium|ConvoyIntercept" > /d/corr_run.txt and leave it running.

⛔⛔ 00-OFFLINE (08-02) — NO-NETWORK STARTUP IS BROKEN. P0 · RENDERING, NOT POSITIONING.

FRAMING CORRECTEDFred 08-02: "It is not a positioning issue, it is a rendering issue." The device knows where it is. GPS is fine. What fails is RENDERING — the panel does not draw. Do not chase location acquisition, location permissions, or GPS fix time.

⭐⭐ FRED'S HYPOTHESIS — test FIRST: "Problem was solved by connecting to internet. Having no local maps I think caused the panel not to render." With no downloaded tiles covering the device position and no network, there is nothing to draw.

⭐⭐ CONFIRMING EVIDENCE: "My track was also recording, it all popped on map when connection was made." So GPS worked, capture worked, storage worked — ONLY DRAWING FAILED, and NO DATA WAS LOST. The cleanest possible confirmation that the failure is purely in rendering.

CHEAP CONFIRMING TEST — no build needed: download tiles covering the device's current position, go fully offline, cold start. If the panel renders, this is not a code regression — it is the app failing to degrade when the viewport has no local coverage.

IF CONFIRMED, the fix is DEGRADATION: marker/track/panel layers must render on a blank background rather than not at all. A rider legitimately arrives somewhere with no downloaded coverage, and the cart position is exactly what they need most at that moment. Add a user-facing message too — "no offline maps for this area" beats an empty screen.

⚠ Check during the debug session: did connecting trigger a PAGE RELOAD? The connectivity auto-sense lives in onPageFinished. If the map reloaded when the network appeared, that re-ran page setup and re-defined the JS functions — which would make the JS-not-defined errors and the no-local-tiles hypothesis the same failure seen from two angles, not competing explanations. · DEVICE DETAIL: observed on Droid 1; Droid 2 would log the same until the radio synchs.

P0 · REGRESSIONFred 08-02: "Starting the android without a network does not display the MY CART icon, the device name positioned on an empty GPS map. If I record a track I see nothing. This is a NEW behaviour. It works fine connected to a network but since we ride off grid this is an issue. It is ANDROID-ONLY MODE (no radio) and IT USED TO WORK."

SYMPTOMS (all offline, Android-only mode, no radio):

  • MY CART icon does not display
  • Device name shown on an empty GPS map
  • Recording a track shows nothing on the map
  • Works correctly when connected to a network

DEVICE DETAIL (Fred): observed on DROID 1. Droid 2 would log the same message until the radio synchs — "probably the same error." So this is not Droid-1-specific; on a radio-equipped device it may be masked once sync completes.

Evidence on file, recorded as observation only — NOT a diagnosis: convoy startup 08-02 logged three JS ReferenceErrors — drawTrack is not defined, clearMarkers is not defined, setRouteMode is not defined.

⚠ REGRESSION — it used to work. Finding what moved is part of the task.

⭐⭐ 00-ROUTE+ (08-02) — ACTIVE TASK: FINISH THE ROUTE+ ERROR RECOVERY PROCESS

ACTIVEFred 08-02: "The behaviour is a mix of new and old behaviour." The 08-01 resolver shipped, but old paths are still live alongside it and the result is not coherent. This is the task to finish — and it must be finished BEFORE the manual's route section is documented, or the confusion gets baked into the manual.

⭐ TARGET BEHAVIOUR (Fred 08-02)

1 · FORCED RECOVERY ON PLANNER ENTRY. On entry to the planner map, force recovery of Auto Saved In Progress if a crash — or a close of the route while in progress — left one behind.
2 · THE RECOVERY IS A PROMPT TO SAVE THE ROUTE UNDER A NAME, which causes a RENAME. It is FORCED on planner entry — not optional, not dismissible into limbo.
3 · ANY OTHER JSONs in the route_drafts library PROMPT AND DISPLAY A LIST of unfinished routes with their CREATION DATES, in a TOAST message.

DraftInfo already carries createdAt (added 08-01), so the creation-date requirement is already served — sort ascending on it.

⚠ WHAT TO RECONCILE

Four mechanisms from two eras, no single owner: the 08-01 resolver + inventory toast, plus recoveryPending, recoveryLaunched, routeSeedOpen, recoveryDetected. One grep maps them all:

grep -n "recoveryPending\|recoveryLaunched\|recoveryDetected\|routeSeedOpen\|resolverRan\|pendingInventory\|showDraftResolve" ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt

Known symptom of the mix: the in-progress recovery message fires with NO drafts on disk. Suspect routeSeedOpen — it reads an open flag from the planning JSON rather than the draft directory, which is exactly the unreliable-status-flag pattern the STATUS-IS-DERIVED rule forbids. First checks: adb shell ls -la /sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/route_drafts/, and identify WHICH message is showing — the "route was left open" dialog or the inventory toast (an empty inventory legitimately says "No in-progress routes", which reads like a recovery message).

⛔⛔ 00-DATA (08-01) — DATA LOSS EVENT: THE APP DESTROYED ITS OWN DATABASE. P0.

P0 BLOCKERAn adb install -r -d on Droid 2 cost the entire spatial database — ~89,000 trails plus every track, waypoint and route. The app did not report a failure. It logged 0 trails, 0 tracks, 0 waypoints, 0 routes and carried on as though that were a normal state.

This is not an adb problem. It is an application defect, and a Play Store update could reproduce it on a user's device with no warning and no recovery.

⛔ The mechanism, in order

  1. The install registered as a fresh install rather than an update (first-install questions appeared).
  2. The app lost all-files authority; its SELinux category labels are assigned per-install.
  3. SQLite's lock ioctl on the FUSE-mounted .db was denied — device log 11:44:04.805, permissive=0, so enforced:
    avc: denied { ioctl } for path=".../grouptrack_spatial.db" dev="fuse" ioctlcmd=0xf50c scontext=u:r:untrusted_app:s0:c178,c256,c512,c768 tclass=file permissive=0 app=com.grouptrack.android
  4. SpatialDbManager opens with create-if-missing. The failed open wrote empty schemas over the existing files — same path, same names, replaced in place. Nothing called delete.
  5. The app started clean and presented itself as empty.

Fingerprint sizes — memorise these: grouptrack_spatial.db = 118,784 bytes, grouptrack_data.db = 208,896 bytes. Those are empty schemas. A live spatial DB is tens of MB.

🔴 THE FIX — 2.6e, outranks the panel work

  • Never open with create-if-missing when the target file exists. Check existence first; on a failed open with a file present, surface an error and stop. This one change converts silent data loss into a message.
  • Verify access BEHAVIOURALLY, not by permission API. The app believed it had all-files access while the OS denied the ioctl. The permission query lied; a real read attempt would not have.
  • Fresh-install marker in filesDir — row counts, DB sizes, last-good timestamp. Private storage is wiped by a fresh install; external storage is not. Marker absent + external DB present = a fresh install just happened over existing data → stop, report.
  • Row-count sanity. A database that held 89 k trails yesterday reporting 0 today is not a valid state.
  • Belt and braces: copy to grouptrack_spatial.db.lastgood after each successful startup.
  • Fix the log line. SpatialDbMgr prints the LITERAL ${spatialFile.absolutePath} — interpolation is broken, so the path never appears. That is the one diagnostic that would have made this obvious in seconds.

⛔ OPERATIONAL RULES — effective immediately, supersede the standing convention

Install with plain -r. Never -d. Without -d, a regressed version code makes the install fail with an error instead of silently downgrading. The failure is the warning.
First-install questions on an already-installed app → CANCEL. Do not proceed. Fred: "11:41 was the first set of install questions. I should have pulled the plug then."
Ten-second post-install check: adb shell ls -la /sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/*.db — hundreds of KB instead of tens of MB means replaced.

The -r -d rule in the environment file is now superseded. The quickstart still documents the old flag — see §05.

🔴 WHY THE INSTALL REGISTERED AS NEW — unresolved

versionCode is git-derived: gitVersionProvider.get().toInt() + vcOffset (app/build.gradle.kts:65). A derived code that can regress, combined with -d, forces the downgrade path — which resets app identity.

Two earlier installs the same day (08:32, 10:47 builds) were fine. The 11:41 one was not. The dumpsys evidence for that specific install was overwritten by the 12:05 recovery install — firstInstallTime and lastUpdateTime both read 12:05.

Signing key ruled out — same AAB key for three months, two successful installs the same day.

Next step: grep -rn "gitVersionProvider" --include=*.kts . — establish what it derives from and whether it can decrease. Fix by pinning for local builds or bumping VERSION_CODE_OFFSET. Single-build override: -Pandroid.injected.version.code=NNNNNNNN.

✅ RECOVERY — the good outcome, and a number worth keeping

Complete data-model reconstruction in UNDER ONE HOUR — OSM import, Utah state trails, tracks, waypoints. GPX files in my_tracks/, tiles in maps/, OSM extracts in osm/ and the per-map JSONs all survived; only the two .db files were replaced.

This materially lowers the stakes on §06 (delete-by-source) — a full rebuild is cheap, and the pipeline was proven under real conditions rather than in theory.
