✅ 00a-DONE (08-01) — ROUTE MODE: SHIPPED. Section 00a below is the spec that drove it.

Commit af17c85dd plus uncommitted follow-ups. The 07-31 spec held; the fix was smaller than the diagnosis.

  • Planner entry reset — fires on every planner entry, verified by toast on every planner↔convoy switch in both directions. This is what makes the shared window.__routeMode global safe: convoy's stale value cannot survive the trip back.
  • Convoy tap-time reset — window.__routeMode = false; at the top of convoy's map.on('click'). Marker TAPRESET-2026-08-01. Convoy can never legitimately be in route mode (its route-panel anchor is hidden), so this states a fact rather than guessing at lifecycle.
  • Unnamed-draft resolver — planner entry + Route+ launch, file operations only: never arms route mode, never loads geometry, never draws a build line.
    unnamed draft exists? no → inventory · <2 points → deleteDraft + LOG → inventory · ≥2 points → Keep (name it) / Discard; renameDraft can FAIL on isNameTaken → RE-PROMPT → inventory
  • Inventory toast — delay(600), re-read, sort ascending by createdAt, one toast. The delay is deliberate: a just-renamed draft lands on disk under its new name and appears on its own merits while the unnamed one is gone. No filtering needed.
  • Route+ New Route guard — blocks with "A route is already in progress — resolve it first" rather than overwriting the UNNAMED file.
  • RouteDraftStore — DraftInfo gained createdAt; const val UNNAMED replaces the scattered literal; UNNAMED filtered out of the In-Progress picker.

⭐ Rename frees the UNNAMED slot so the next Route+ can use it, and the renamed draft becomes selectable via Route+ → In Progress. Nothing is stranded — which is why rename-or-discard is sufficient and the resolver never needs to resume.

⛔ CORRECTION — :1472 RECOVERY IS NOT A LEAK

The 07-31 "⛔ SUSPECT — THIS IS THE LEAK" label on the planner's recovery arm was wrong. That block sits inside showInProgressPicker's selection handler: the user taps +ROUTE → In Progress → picks a draft by name. A deliberate user action, exactly as the spec requires. It stays.

recoveryPending — the old "a route was left open, go do it yourself" notice — is retired; the resolver acts on the directory instead of instructing the user to.

⭐⭐ CONVOY IS A PERSISTENT COMPOSABLE — IT HAS NO ENTRY EVENT

Fred: "convoy map is a persistent function it underlies all activity. it never goes away."

This single fact invalidated most of a day's work. Every candidate anchor for a "convoy entry reset" assumed an entry that does not exist:

  • onPageFinished — fires once per page load, and the page loads once
  • onViewportChanged — fires per viewport event, not per entry
  • LaunchedEffect(Unit) — never recomposes
  • the [Fix2] block whose comment says "re-entry" — it means re-reads the JSON fresh instead of trusting cmSeed, a staleness concern, and it sits inside onPageFinished's postDelayed(600)

⛔ CONVOY NEEDS NO RESET. The planner resets on entry and convoy resets at tap time. Do not re-litigate.

Also confirmed: convoy has two JS bridges — addJavascriptInterface at ~596 on existing (reused WebView; onViewportChanged ~633, no reseed, never tests lastMapProcessed) and ~831 on a fresh WebView (onViewportChanged ~860, has the reseed block). Persistent screen → re-entry takes the existing path. Verified identical on 07-24 (703625fb8) — this is how convoy has always been built, not a regression. Convoy's reseed gate was removed 07-01 and now fires on every pan and zoom.

⛔⛔ 00a-TRAP (08-01) — TWO SELF-INFLICTED BREAKS. Both killed convoy's entire artifact draw.

Convoy stopped drawing trails, tracks and waypoints twice during the session. Neither was a convoy bug. Both were instrumentation aborting a method before its real work — and both were silent: no error, no JS exception, no log.

⛔ TRAP 1 — ?: return inside an argument exits the whole function

android.widget.Toast.makeText(view?.context ?: return, ...)

Reads like a null-safe accessor. Is an early exit from the entire override. Placed in onPageFinished, it bailed before tile setup, setTileUrl, the persisted-frame restore and drawPersistedState. It also displaced the original val ctx = view?.context ?: return guard into a position where failing it is catastrophic.

Never put ?: return in an argument position.

⛔⛔ TRAP 2 — @JavascriptInterface methods run on a BACKGROUND thread

evaluateJavascript and Toast.makeText both require the main thread and throw when called off it — and the throw aborts the rest of the method, including the processViewport call that draws every artifact.

webViewRef.value?.evaluateJavascript("window.__routeMode = false;", null) // in onViewportChanged android.widget.Toast.makeText(context, "ROUTE MODE OFF <- CONVOY reseed entry", ...)

The existing code already knew this. It wraps its work in Thread { }, and SpatialDisplayManager uses main.post { } for every evaluateJavascript.

RULE: anything touching the UI or the WebView inside a @JavascriptInterface method must go through Handler(Looper.getMainLooper()).post { }.

⚠ PROCESS — three lessons that cost real time

  • Prefer targeted removal over blanket revert when a file holds a day's work. A git checkout <baseline> -- ConvoyScreen.kt was executed on the claim it "only costs toasts"; it also took the reseed block. Restoring from the day's commit and deleting the three offending lines was available the whole time and is what worked.
  • Cheap compile gate: ./gradlew compileGoogleReleaseKotlin — Kotlin front-end only, minutes rather than 30–50. A patch script proves the anchor matched; only the compiler proves the code is valid. A forward-reference error (a local fun at ~251 could not see a val declared at ~304) surfaced at 4m26s into a full release build.
  • Build times 08-01: 44m, 35m, 26m, 17m, 11m. Incremental builds are far cheaper than the standing ~40m assumption — a small fix does not cost a cold build.

Device is on Utah time, laptop on Eastern — logcat timestamps are DEVICE time. A two-hour misread sent one diagnosis down the wrong path. · Long cat > file << 'PYEOF' heredocs containing triple-quoted Python repeatedly mangle on paste in Git-Bash; python - << 'PYEOF' with string concatenation survives, sed -i survives best. · Use git --no-pager for diffs.

✅ 00-TRACK (08-01) — TRACK NAMING IS NOT A BUG. Investigation closed.

Reported: recorded tracks show as "Convoy Track" instead of the typed name; a field user could not find his track. Traced end to end — the name machinery works.

  • ConvoyGpsService.kt:386 writes the placeholder at record start (the name is not known yet)
  • ConvoyViewModel.kt:502 replaces it with the typed name, then f.writeText(text)
  • resolveTrackAdd(name.trim(), f) passes that name; insertTrackToDb(name, …) uses it — no re-derivation from the file

Two device GPX files both carried the correct typed name. The failing one (freds_track_b.gpx, 187 bytes) had an empty <trkseg> — zero GPS points captured → finalizeTrack: no geometry → no resolveTrackAdd, no hash rename, no DB row, no artifact. The app behaved correctly given an empty track. Stationary test, no movement. Fred: "my test was not adequate. i will drive about tomorrow."

Two real sub-items found while tracing:

  • OPENA recording that captures zero points writes an empty GPX and fails silently downstream. No user-facing message.
  • OPENresolveTrackAdd's DROP_NAME / DROP_ALIAS / ALIAS branches (hash already exists) do not update the name — they delete the source file and return, so a re-added track with a new name keeps the old row's name.

📜 00a — SPEC OF RECORD (written 07-31 EOD; EXECUTED 08-01, see 00a-DONE above)

EXECUTED 08-01This section is retained as the governing specification. It was written 07-31 EOD and carried out on 08-01 — see 00a-DONE above for what shipped, and 00a-TRAP for what went wrong on the way. The spec held; do not re-derive it.

Route planning is unusable today and has been since 43ab77e16 (07-03). The design was settled tonight in English before any code — do it in that order tomorrow.

🔴🔴 FIX SEQUENCE (Fred, 07-31 EOD) — everything else depends on step 1

1. SETTLE THE ROUTE STATE ONCE AND FOR ALL.

FOUR actions turn route mode OFF:

  1. Map entry, during the persistence setup process — ⚠ currently VIOLATED at ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt:1447, which reads persisted state and calls setRouteMode(true). Persistence must never arm a live session flag.
  2. Save — session ends
  3. Discard — session ends
  4. Select Artifact — ⭐ ends DRAW MODE but the session continues. This is the one that is not an exit, and the one most likely to be missed.

TWO actions arm it: opening Route+ · selecting Draw within the panel.

2. OBSERVABILITY ON THE ROUTE BUTTON IN THE ROUTE PANEL. With six transitions and no visible state, "did that discard actually turn it off?" is currently unanswerable. Fred: "I have had continue messages with no in-flight files" — the flag claimed a state nothing verified.

⭐ And one log line makes all six paths verifiable without reading any of them — at the top of setRouteMode: console.log('SETROUTEMODE -> ' + !!on). Any action that does not produce a line is the leak. It will also show true firing at map entry, confirming the :1447 arming we have inferred but never watched happen.

⚠ Do this BEFORE the deletions. The rest of the changes are worthless if the state is not reliably right first.

🔴🔴 HIDE MUST MEAN ABSENT, NOT INVISIBLE (Fred, 07-31)

Fred: "hide keeps them from being placed on the map, not just invisible" and "the behaviour we are looking for is focusing on artifacts we are displaying."

THE RULE: a hidden artifact type contributes NOTHING — not taps, not proximity, not snap, not selection. Visibility IS the selection. What is on screen is what you are working with.

⚠ Observed defect that proves it

Trails OFF, tap a track on the planner, get a list of TRAIL names.

Cause: hideTrails() does map.removeLayer(trailLayer) and sets trailsVisible = false — it does NOT null the variable. The proximity scan at grouptrack_map.html:831 calls checkLayer(trailLayer, …), which skips a layer only if it is null. So a detached-but-populated layer is still fully walkable, and hidden trails contribute to the proximity list.

⚠ It also explains "occasionally the detail panel instead" — the scan is a MAP-level click handler running alongside the track layer's own handler. Whichever resolves first wins the screen.

⚠ And the behaviour currently depends on HOW you turned it off: clearTrails() DOES null the variable, hideTrails() does not.

The fix — and why NOT to null on hide

⛔ Do not null the layer on hide. The retained variable is a CACHE — loadTrails rebuilds from a fresh GeoJSON payload, so nulling means a full query and rebuild on every toggle. At 10,000 trails that is a visible cost.

⭐ Fix checkLayer to test visibility instead of null:

function checkLayer(layer, visible, typeName, color) {
  if (!layer || !visible) return;

Four call sites, both maps. Correct regardless of which path turned the type off.

⚠ AND CHECK SNAP — likely the same bug in a different place

RouteManager.snap(lat, lon, trails, tracks, 30.0) is fed from queryTrailsByViewport / queryTracksByViewport — database queries, not layers. Those know nothing about display state, so a route vertex can probably snap to a HIDDEN trail. Under this rule it should not.

⚠ Not verified — ConvoyScreen.kt:596 builds the byId map from both queries and I saw no visibility filter, but only read part of it. Check before assuming.

⚠⚠ ROUTE DRAWING IS BLOCKED AS OF 07-31 EOD — and canvas is not the cause

Before today: a tap was SHARED — you got a popup AND a vertex. Messy but usable.

After canvas: the artifact consumes the tap. No vertex lands. Route drawing does not work.

⚠ Under SVG the artifact layer did not consume the event, so taps fell through to the map by accident. Canvas did not break the suppression — it removed the accident that was masking it. The mechanism has been broken since 43ab77e16 (07-03) either way.

⛔ Reverting canvas is the wrong trade (Fred: "more work to strip out today's changes than to fix the route state"). It would restore a broken mechanism to a merely tolerable failure mode while giving back the trail hit-rate fix — 3 hits in 40 taps at z18.

⛔ AND THERE IS NO WORKAROUND

Turning trails and tracks off lets the tap through — but then there is no snap and no guidance. Tracks were removed from snap on 07-30 (snapTrailsOnly, because a track is one geometry so snapping pulled the whole thing), and hidden trails are not in the query result. You get free vertices only.

⭐⭐ SO THE FIX MUST DELIVER VISIBLE + SNAPPABLE + NON-INTERACTIVE SIMULTANEOUSLY. During a route draw you need to SEE trails, SNAP to them, and NOT TAP them. Three concerns the code currently collapses into one flag.

⚠ Which rules out the SVG approach. sub._path.style.pointerEvents — the line 43ab77e16 deleted — is an SVG DOM property, and canvas paths are not DOM elements. Under canvas the mechanism is:

lyr.eachLayer(function(sub) { sub.options.interactive = !on; });

Stateless, survives layer rebuilds, works under canvas, and replaces the stash/restore block entirely. ⚠ VERIFY THIS FIRST — it is the one unknown between the spec and the patch.

⭐⭐ THE SPECIFICATION (Fred, 07-31, verbatim intent)

ConceptMeansRule
Persistencestate RECREATION — position, zoom, artifact display states, checked listsBoth maps write on every screen refresh. Puts the map back as you left it.
Route modeACTIVELY PLOTTING POINTS RIGHT NOW ⭐ FALSE ON EVERY MAP ENTRY, BOTH MAPS, UNCONDITIONALLY. Entered ONLY by choosing Add Route. Never restored from persistence.
Draft filea route was not finished Produces ONE MESSAGE on planner entry: "you have an unsaved route". That is all it does. No arming, no geometry restore, no drawBuildLine.
routeState.opena session ended without terminating An END-STATE write. Records only; never arms anything.

THE TAP RULE, entire:

  • Route mode OFF → tap opens artifacts (popup / detail panel)
  • Route mode ON → tap places a route point. Nothing else consumes it.

⭐⭐ THEREFORE POPUPS NEVER NEED SUPPRESSING. If the tap does not reach the artifact layer, no popup can open. Every unbind, stash, restore and bind-gate in the codebase exists to solve a problem that correct tap routing makes impossible.

THREE EXITS, one entry. Save · save in progress · discard. Each terminates route mode and writes the end state. To save a recovered draft you go through Add Route like any other route — no special-cased recovery path.

⚠ Route mode is an ACTIVITY, not restorable state. Fred: "a file on disk means you have not finished a route. The state is actively plotting points." Two different things, and conflating them is the whole mess.

⚠ Fred on why a boolean was not enough: "I have had continue messages with no in-flight files." The flag claimed a state nothing verified — and setting it false does not undo what setting it true DID (unbound popups, changed pointer events). Termination must REASSERT consequences, not just write a value.

⛔ THE REGRESSION — commit 43ab77e16, 07-03

Titled "suppress artifact popups during route-build on both maps (unbind/rebind + bind-gate + force-flag)". It replaced a working stateless mechanism with a broken stateful one:

- if (sub && sub._path) sub._path.style.pointerEvents = on ? 'none' : '';
+ if (on)  { sub._savedPopupContent = sub.getPopup().getContent(); sub.unbindPopup(); }
+ else     { sub.bindPopup(sub._savedPopupContent); sub._savedPopupContent = null; }

⭐ The deleted line was correct. Stateless, nothing stashed, and it suppressed the TAP rather than the popup — which is what let route-build taps fall through to the map. The function's own surviving comment still describes that behaviour: "route their pointer events out so the tap falls through to map.on('click') → Android.onMapTap (real coords)."

⚠ Why stash/restore cannot work: the viewport lazy-load rebuilds layers on every pan. _savedPopupContent lives on layer objects that get replaced, so the stash is lost and popups unbind PERMANENTLY. Observed 07-31: route mode off, popups still gone.

⚠⚠ AND THE GATES ARE INVERTED BETWEEN MAPS — Fred spotted this from behaviour alone:

  • Planner gates at BIND time (if (!window.__routeMode) layer.bindPopup(...)) — so it does nothing to layers already drawn. Arming on a populated map has no effect. Ineffective on the map that owns route mode.
  • Convoy guards at TAP time (if (window.__routeMode) return;) — always effective. On the map that cannot create routes at all.

🔴 TOMORROW'S WORK — mostly deletion

  1. ⛔ Delete the stash/restore block, both maps
  2. ⛔ Delete the bind-time gates (if (!window.__routeMode) layer.bindPopup(...))
  3. ⛔ Delete convoy's __routeMode tap guard
  4. ⭐ Restore pointer-events routing on route mode
  5. ⭐ setRouteMode(false) unconditionally on every map entry, both maps — the call, not just the flag, so consequences are reasserted
  6. ⛔ Remove the arming at ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt:1447 (window.__routeMode=true;setRouteMode(true); drawBuildLine(...)) — this is the named violation: persistence arming a live session flag
  7. ⭐ Convoy writes routeState.open = false explicitly on every save, so the handoff cannot carry a stale armed state. Absent ≠ false.
  8. ⭐ Route-mode state visible and manually terminable in the planner UI — with three exits, "did that discard actually terminate it?" is currently unanswerable

⚠⚠ CHECK FIRST, BEFORE WRITING ANY OF IT: the restored mechanism uses sub._path, which is an SVG DOM property. Both maps moved to L.canvas today and canvas paths are NOT DOM elements. _path may be undefined, in which case canvas needs its own hit-test bypass. This is the one unknown between here and a clean fix.

✅ SOLVED 08-01 — detail panel: the bridge was tested at BIND time

FIXEDThe planner's DB-track handler read:

if (tid && window.Android && window.Android.onTrackTap) { layer.on("click", …) } else { layer.bindPopup('<b>' + name + '</b>'); } // ← bound PERMANENTLY

If addJavascriptInterface had not landed when the layer was built, a popup was bound permanently and the bridge appearing a millisecond later could not undo it. Now tested at TAP time, matching what loadTrackFile already did. Marker TRACKTAP-BINDTIME-2026-08-01. Verified on device: full artifact detail panel opens.

⛔ This INVALIDATES the 07-30 diagnosis recorded as comments in BOTH map HTMLs — "the bridge fires with a good id and the composable never renders." For the planner's DB tracks the bridge never fired at all. Correct those comments during the §07b cleanup so they do not mislead again.

Convoy's equivalent handler was already correct — binds unconditionally, tests inside the handler. No change needed there.

⚠ Consequence: converting a popup into a click handler means setRouteMode's unbind/stash mechanism no longer covers it — that only handles bindPopup. That handler now needs its own if (window.__routeMode) return; guard. See 00-TAP below.

🔴 00-TAP (08-01) — TAP HANDLING: what is fixed, what is still open

Route mode was blocking every tap investigation. With it resolved, the remaining tap defects are now visible and separable for the first time.

🔴 OPEN — trail popups fire WHILE dropping route points (planner)

setRouteMode's unbind/stash block (grouptrack_map.html ~767) handles bindPopup only — it stashes content and unbinds on ON, rebinds on OFF.

Two candidates: (a) the trailLayer reference is stale — updateTrails likely creates a NEW layer object on each draw, so a layer created after arming was never unbound; (b) the mechanism does not reach canvas sublayers.

⭐ Argues for the HANDLER-GUARD approach over unbind/stash. if (window.__routeMode) return; read at TAP time cannot go stale; a layer reference captured at arm time can. This also matches the governing spec: correct tap routing makes popup suppression unnecessary.

⚠ OPEN — checkLayer: hidden types still produce popups (patch designed, NOT applied)

Deliberately held back on 08-01 so a regression would have an obvious cause. Design is settled; apply next session.

hideTrails() does map.removeLayer(trailLayer) and sets trailsVisible = false but does not null the variable; checkLayer skips only on null, so a detached-but-populated layer stays fully walkable by the proximity scan.

Fred's rule: "hide keeps them from being placed on the map, not just invisible." A hidden type contributes NOTHING — not taps, not proximity, not snap, not selection.

⚠⚠ TWO BLOCKERS BEFORE PATCHING:

  • Signature change required. The function is checkLayer(layer, typeName, color) — there is no visible parameter. Either add it at all call sites, or have the function test the visibility variable directly.
  • TWO COPIES, both in grouptrack_map.html — ~line 18 and ~line 854. That is the duplicate proximity scan; two popups race per tap. convoy_map.html has NEITHER, so convoy's proximity popups come from somewhere else entirely. Resolve the duplication before patching.

⛔ Do NOT null on hide — the retained variable is a cache; nulling forces a full query and rebuild per toggle at 10,000 trails.

⚠ OPEN — smaller items

  • In-progress recovery message fires with NO drafts on disk. Fred: "no open tracks in progress but continue to receive startup message." Suspect routeSeedOpen — it reads an open flag from the planning JSON rather than the draft directory, which is exactly the unreliable-status-flag pattern the STATUS-IS-DERIVED rule forbids. First checks: adb shell ls -la /sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/route_drafts/, and identify WHICH message is showing — the "route was left open" dialog or the inventory toast (an empty inventory legitimately says "No in-progress routes", which reads like a recovery message).
  • Logging issues around in-progress route adds — flagged, not characterised.
  • emulatedDrafts (planner ~188) is a val at composable scope with no remember — it re-reads the draft directory on every recomposition, on the main thread. Pre-existing.
  • ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt ~1058 sets routeMode = true when +ROUTE is pressed, before the New/In-Progress choice, and it is not an instrumented write site. Dismissing the entry dialog leaves route mode on with no toast. Bounded (the planner's entry reset clears it) but real.
  • Convoy GPS snapback on search — used to stay put after navigating away. May be correct for a riding map; needs a decision, not necessarily a fix.
  • Planner needed a force-close to pick up tracks after the convoy fix build.

Fred, 07-30 EOD and confirmed 07-31: "CONVOY FIXES FOR 2.6e, and places tags from spatial.db, polish for playstore." Three items. All three are display-layer work; none of them touch the import pipeline, which is done.

  1. smoothFactor — trails (built), then tracks and routes. Commit.
  2. Switchable spatial place labels. Commit.
  3. Convoy popup / waypoint tap. Commit.

A — Convoy map: popups and waypoints

⭐ Diagnosable with no build. Convoy already has a live console bridge — ConvoyJS at ConvoyScreen.kt:777, four ARTIFACTTAP lines and a ROUTEMODE trace. Read the log before writing anything.

⛔ Convoy does NOT have the planner's parse defect. That was fixed 07-30 (item 4, the dead head-of-file block). Convoy's failures are drawTrack / clearMarkers called against a page that is not ready — a different cause with a different remedy. The established one is postDelayed(600) in onPageFinished, which every other WebView JS init in this app already uses.

⚠ Dead route-mode scaffolding. routeMode is still declared at ConvoyScreen.kt:204, left from when route-add was removed from convoy. On a map that cannot build routes, those guards can only do harm — they can suppress a tap and there is nothing they can usefully protect. Delete them in the same commit rather than working around them.

⚠ Convoy map is FROZEN — but the freeze is a LAYER, not a FILE: the Kotlin↔JS↔Leaflet↔self-saving-setView↔MapStateStore tangle. Tap handling is not in that tangle, so this work is allowed. Rules stand: never add a moveend listener, never call setView / fitBounds / reportViewport.

B — Spatial label layer on BOTH maps (the SAT_LABELS_PLACES assessment)

NOT STARTED · THE 2.6e DECISION08-01: design settled, nothing built. This is the release's actual purpose and it is the item that did not get touched.

Three checkboxes, one line, CHECKED = HIDES (matches the existing removeTilesChecked idiom rather than introducing a second polarity in the same panel):

☐ Esri places ☐ Esri streets ☐ OSM places
  • All unchecked = today's map. OSM places ships CHECKED by default so the new layer stays hidden — one polarity on the line, and the default is a persistence value rather than a design compromise.
  • Location: planner upload/download panel, first section, Map Controls.
  • Persistence: new keys in the per-map JSON via savePlanningState(), safe-load-with-defaults, no schema bump. Must go in the capture-and-reseed block in both screens, inside the lastMapProcessed gate. Write through the existing helper only.
  • ⚠ The Esri toggles must act on RENDERING, not the download set. Hooking getDownloadSources() changes nothing visible — already-downloaded tiles keep drawing. Whether render-side suppression already exists is the open question.

⭐ RUN TEST STATE 2 FIRST (Esri places OFF, Esri transport OFF, OSM OFF). It needs no build — the Esri toggles are existing stores — and it empirically inventories what SAT_LABELS_PLACES actually contains. If street names vanish too, the split is not clean in the stored mbtiles and the answer is no. That single observation may settle the tile decision before a line of code is written.

Sampling: three fixed area types — town (Esri densest), trailhead/junction cluster (navigational value), open backcountry (noise test). Same zoom, same viewport, screenshot each state. Weight gaps by navigational value, not count. No automation is possible — Esri labels are pixels baked into raster tiles, so the comparison is necessarily eyeball.

OSM side: add a Places TypeBinding to SpatialDisplayManager — it is already map-agnostic, so one binding serves both maps; only jsUpdate/jsShow/jsHide need to exist in both HTMLs. minZoom per binding gives fclass thinning a home — city/town/village first, locality last (2,846 of Utah's 3,674, the noisy class). Make the divIcons visually distinct from Esri labels so agreement and disagreement read differently.

⚠ Cross-state boundary caveat: OSM points import whole-state; tiles are corridor-scoped. A rider who imports Utah and rides into Nevada gets no OSM place labels across the line, where the Esri store would have covered wherever tiles were downloaded. DECIDE: is the retirement condition COUNT(*) > 0 globally, or per-state-of-current-viewport?

reference_points holds Utah 7,723 / Arizona 9,324 and nothing renders them — confirmed on the 07-31 xref, where the table appears only at OsmImportWorker.kt:307, its index creation.

⭐⭐ NEW FINDING (07-31 xref) — and it corrects what I claimed on 07-28. I said labels would have to be hand-mirrored into both HTML files through the orphaned spatial_artifacts.js. They do not. SpatialDisplayManager is already map-agnostic:

  • drawPersistedState(mapKey, webView, context) at :116 — takes the map key and reads MapStateStore.readMap(mapKey)
  • TypeBinding registry at :31 carrying query / build / jsUpdate / jsShow / jsHide / minZoom
  • bindingFor(type) at :41 — adding a type is adding a registry entry
  • DS_OFF / DS_ON / DS_SELECTED — the ON/OFF persistence pattern already exists

So: add a Places TypeBinding and the Kotlin side serves both maps for free. Only the three JS receiver functions (jsUpdate/jsShow/jsHide) need to exist in both HTMLs. Materially smaller than the two-file hand-mirror I described.

⭐ minZoom is already a per-binding field, so the fclass zoom-thinning has a home without inventing anything: city/town/village first, locality last — it is 2,846 of Utah's 3,674 places and is the noisy class.

Rendering: places are POINTS, so L.divIcon renders them today — no MapLibre and no glyph bundling, which is the requirement most likely to be missed and the one that silently breaks offline labels.

Persistence: additive beside the Esri layers, default OFF, ON/OFF in the per-map JSON via savePlanningState() (ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt:265), and captured in BOTH screens' reseed block.

⚠ The three-state comparison is the point. Esri-only / OSM-only / both. Both-on will be hard to read — "Moab" drawn twice a few pixels apart, one baked into the tile and one as a divIcon over it. The OSM-only state is where the answer lives: does OSM alone carry enough to ride by?

⚠ The A/B has never been run. 07-26 was a browser prototype at mid-zoom; Esri's raster labels are densest at z16–18, which is the fair test and the one that decides retirement.

If OSM wins: retiring SAT_LABELS_PLACES takes bar 10 from ~107,000 to ~85,300 tiles (≈20% of total download — one of three near-identical stores, not the ⅔ that "eliminate the Esri label DBs" implies; road labels need MapLibre and are 2.7). ⚠ Config change first — remove it from getDownloadSources() — then deleteSource(). Reversed, the next download refills the file you just emptied. deleteSource() drops the whole .mbtiles: space back instantly, no VACUUM, and it sidesteps the unresolved "0 MB reclaimable" question entirely.

Behind window.__detailPanelEnabled = false today. The 07-30 work landed 3a — tracks fall back to a plain popup as a deliberate stopgap; this is the item that replaces the stopgap with the real detail panel, on both maps.

⚠⚠ TWO DEFECTS WEARING ONE SYMPTOM — do not assume one fix serves both. The planner's addPointMode guard was stuck true with route mode off. Convoy has no such guard and fails anyway. Same visible outcome, unrelated causes. Fixing the planner and testing only there would ship convoy still broken.

⚠ 00f — TWO FLAGS FOR ONE CONCEPT (the defect behind item 3)

Kotlin addPointMode and JS window.__routeMode mean the same thing and nothing keeps them in sync. Proven divergent:

TrackTap: PLANNER bridge id=8f8c39b1… addPointMode=true
TrackTap: PLANNER SUPPRESSED by addPointMode
— every ARTIFACTTAP in the same session: routeMode=false

⭐ Fred's framing is the fix: route mode is the wrong predicate — it means "Route+ is open", when what needs guarding is "the next tap places a vertex". One flag, one setter, both sides updated from the same call, and the narrow name.

⛔ item 3b's fall-through keys off __routeMode; if the predicate changes, that changes with it or it drifts again.

⚠ 00g — PROCESS LESSONS 07-30 (durable, not 2.6e detail)

  1. Build on verified work, not on an empty queue. Items 1–2 were validated an hour before the build and held to bundle item 3 — which was still diagnostic, introduced a defect, and forced a second build anyway. They could have been tested and certified in that window. Bundling delays verification AND entangles it.
  2. Never bundle diagnostics with fixes. Exploratory work can break settled work.
  3. Check source timestamps against the APK before testing. Twice today we tested a binary that predated the change, once losing a whole tap session.
  4. Connect the channel before theorising about what you cannot see. An unread log looks like evidence of absence.

🔴 04 — 2.6e RELEASE TASK: SNAP-2 APPLIES TO TRAILS ONLY

2.6e route planning only — NOT track recording

⭐⭐ Fred 07-29: "tracks can appear on map but snap2 should not be applied to tracks." Route planning snaps to trails only. Tracks keep rendering and stay tappable for details.

Two independent reasons it is right, not a workaround:

  1. Coverage. Track-snapping existed because UGRC's 49,125 trails left real ground unreferenced. At 89,527 OSM trails that gap is largely closed.
  2. lineId correctness. A snapped vertex stores lineId, and a track's lineId is per-rider — ten riders on one trail produce ten tracks with ten IDs. Snapping a route to a track never denoted shared geometry, so it was already wrong for the lead-cart contract.

⭐ SNAP-2 HAS TWO APPLICATIONS AND ONLY ONE CHANGES. Fred: "i am talking about route planning, not track recording. snap2 applies to each cart's track recording individually currently." The recording side snaps each cart's track ONTO trails — it consumes trails as targets, never tracks — so removing tracks from the target set cannot break it. ⚠ One track per cart per database currently.

Likely small: stop passing the track list to RouteManager.snap(lat, lon, trails, tracks, 30.0). Snap eligibility separates cleanly from display and from tap-for-details.

⚠⚠ VERIFY: this AVOIDS the whole-line bug, it does not fix it. A recorded track is ONE continuous LineString while curated trail networks are pre-segmented — that is why dropping a point ingested the whole track and trails behaved. But Geofabrik ships OSM ways, and a long way is a long single LineString. Test route-drawing on one deliberately long OSM trail before concluding the symptom is gone.

⛔ Do NOT physically split stored tracks or trails. Changing geometry changes geom_hash → breaks dedup and orphans every lineId. If a fix is needed it is linear referencing in Kotlin: record the snapped POINT and its position along the line, never the line's whole geometry.

🔴 06 — 2.6e OPEN: delete-by-source for TRAILS

08-01 EVIDENCEMeasured: a complete data-model rebuild takes UNDER ONE HOUR — OSM import, Utah state trails, tracks, waypoints. This materially lowers the stakes on this item. The argument for in-app delete-by-source rests on avoiding a costly rebuild; the rebuild is not costly. Worth re-weighing before it consumes 2.6e time.

exposure grew by 89,527 rows on 07-29

89,527 OSM trails are in the production spatial DB and there is no in-app way to remove them. deleteAllOsmPoints exists (unwired) for points; there is no trail equivalent.

⚠ Recovery today is a laptop operation: pull the DBs, DELETE ... WHERE source_id='osm', push back. Fine on Droid 2 (sacrificial), not fine for a tester.

⛔ It cannot simply be added: a route's snapped vertices carry lineId, so deleting a trail orphans them silently. That is why bulk delete-by-source was considered and rejected for the release. ⭐ This now needs a decision rather than another deferral.

⚠ Points differ — they carry no source_id; reference_points is OSM-only by construction, so that is a full-table delete. Any delete-by-source must also check for other sources aliasing a row first.

🔴 07 — 2.6e OPEN: C2 follow-ups (small, not blocking)

  • ⚠ bad_geometry: 0 on the 07-29 extract vs 89,394 on 07-28, with identical kept: 89536 and candidate_rows: 186652. Either the counting changed between runs, or the earlier figure was the droppedNoName collision. Parked, unexplained. There is a comment at OsmExtractWorker.kt:156 about what the field means. If the two are summed, a genuine decode failure is invisible.
  • ⚠ OSM place names contain characters outside cp1252 (U+02BC in Naatsisʼáán). SQLite is UTF-8 so storage is fine; any CSV export or Log.i on a name must cope. Crashed a PC-side script.
  • ⚠ trail_properties — unknown whether it has UNIQUE(source_id, source_unique_id). C3a uses a plain INSERT in try/catch so the first run announces it.

🧹 07b — 2.6e LAST: JS CLEANUP / DEAD-CODE REMOVAL (behind a fallback AAB)

Sequenced LAST by agreement (Fred 07-31), after smoothFactor, place labels and the convoy tap fixes are each committed — and with a controlled AAB built and kept so there is something to fall back to. It is the only step on the board with no user-visible upside if it goes wrong.

⚠⚠ THE FINDING THAT OUTRANKS THE REST — BOTH MAPS LOAD LEAFLET FROM THE INTERNET

Both map assets pull their core library from public CDNs:

  • convoy_map.html:7 and grouptrack_map.html:7 — https://unpkg.com/leaflet@1.9.4/dist/leaflet.js
  • convoy_map.html:10 and grouptrack_map.html:10 — https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/.../leaflet.draw.js

⭐ Line 8 on both is leaflet.polylineDecorator.js with no protocol — a local asset. So the vendoring pattern already exists in these very files; two libraries simply did not follow it.

⚠ Fred reports all maps render offline today, so this is NOT confirmed as a live defect. The likely benign explanation is shouldInterceptRequest — the app already intercepts convoy://tiles/ and may also serve these URLs from assets, in which case the CDN strings are addresses that are never fetched and there is nothing to fix.

Two commands settle it, no build:

  1. ls app/src/main/assets/ | grep -i "\.js$"
  2. grep -rn "unpkg\|cdnjs\|leaflet" app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ --include=*.kt | grep -v "\.bak"

How to read it: local copy and an interception rule → working as designed, withdraw the concern. Local copy but no interception → the local file is unused. No local copy → offline rendering is riding the WebView cache, which survives normal use but not Clear Storage, not cache eviction, and is not guaranteed across app updates. The equivalent field test is airplane mode after Clear Storage.

If it does need fixing: download both files into assets/ and change four src attributes. No logic change, no version drift, and it removes a runtime dependency on two third-party CDNs from an app whose purpose is working with no signal.

✅ CONFIRMED DEAD — spatial_artifacts.js

Grep-verified orphan: no <script src> loads it anywhere, and every reference in app/src/main/ is a comment. The live code is inlined in both HTMLs and has since diverged (convoy 9 trailLayer refs, planner 15).

Delete the file AND its six comments — convoy_map.html lines 26, 221, 226, 233, 405, 406. The comments are worse than the file: they sit directly above the real declarations at :411 and tell the next reader the code lives somewhere it does not.

⚠ Why it cannot simply wait for 2.7: the V2.7 plan names this file as its rewrite target. Leaving it means MapLibre starts from code that has not been live in months. Deleting it forces 2.7 to rewrite from the inlined copies, which are the truth.

⛔ Unifying the two inlined copies stays deferred — that work is deleted by the MapLibre swap and is already on the ruled-out list. Only the orphan and its comments go now.

⛔ NOT DEAD CODE — drawRoute and clearMap

field_crossref SECTION 4 lists both with zero callers, which reads as a clean weed-wacking target. It is not. Neither exists in either HTML at all — grep returns nothing.

They are stale entries in the xref generator's hardcoded bridge-method list. Nothing to delete; fix the list in recommit_docs_v14.sh or accept the noise. Recorded so the next pass does not re-chase them.

⚠⚠ DURABLE — WHAT THE XREFS CAN AND CANNOT CONFIRM (blast radius)

Established 07-31 while checking exactly this. The xrefs are the blast-radius tool, but their coverage is narrower than it looks:

  • where_used_raw.txt is 253 lines and indexes .kt ONLY. Zero HTML, zero JS. function_universe and navigation_xref likewise.
  • ⛔ So an empty result for a JS symbol means THE TOOL DID NOT LOOK — it is byte-identical to "safe to delete". This is a false-safety trap and it is how a live function gets removed.
  • ⭐ field_crossref is the strongest of the four: SECTION 4 bridge-method caller analysis, SECTION 5 state-field [R]/[W] map, SECTION 7 code issues and warnings. The [R]/[W] markers are what actually answer "who writes this, who reads it".
  • ⚠ It picks up JS fields it tracks (e.g. convoy_map.html:514) but not JS functions — coverage is field-shaped, not file-shaped.

Rule: for anything JS-side, grep the assets. The xref is necessary and not sufficient.

⚠ .bak_* files in assets/ pollute every grep — four convoy backups matched the spatial_artifacts search alone. Use --exclude='*.bak*'.

✅ ALSO GOES IN THIS PASS — dead __routeMode scaffolding on convoy

Proven inert 07-31 via field_crossref SECTION 5: every write to window.__routeMode comes from ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt (the planner) at :1155, :1232, :1257, :1447. Zero writes from ConvoyScreen.kt.

So on convoy the variable is permanently undefined → falsy → the guard at convoy_map.html:514 never fires.

⚠⚠ Which means it is NOT the cause of the convoy tap failures, despite being labelled // BUG A: suppress while route-building. That comment is an active false lead and has already cost time. Removing it is worth more than the two lines it deletes.

🔵🔵 08 — RELEASE TARGET 2.7 — REDEFINED 2026-08-22: MESH / GROUPTRACK SEPARATION. EOD-2026-08-22

⭐⭐⭐ 2.7's PURPOSE CHANGED on 08-22. It was the convoy tracking rewrite plus MapLibre. It is now the separation of GroupTrack from mesh messaging — the rewrite is what the separation requires, not the goal itself. The MapLibre work below is unchanged and still 2.7-tier; it is no longer what 2.7 is FOR.

SCOPE — deliberately narrow

  • Separate GroupTrack from mesh messaging. Mesh becomes a PLUGIN — the messaging component, a go-between.
  • A switch: mesh or IP-based GPS relay.
  • IP IS A STUB. Nothing implemented behind it.

⭐ The stub is what proves the boundary is real. An interface with one implementation is a wrapper; an interface with a second one declared has to actually be an interface.

⛔ Fred, 08-22, pulling back from a design that was mushrooming: "this thing is mushrooming beyond my ability to conceptualize it. we are writing network protocol not an application." That instinct was right — the discussion had reached relay rules and broadcast intervals, which is a different project. The narrow scope is the correction.

⛔ EXPLICITLY NOT 2.7 — do not let these creep back

All of the following were discussed on 08-22 and deliberately deferred. They belong to whoever builds the IP transport:

  • Every device relays broadcasts received over every channel
  • Every device broadcasts every 3 seconds
  • Hop limits and message-ID dedup (⚠ without these, relay + 3s is a broadcast storm — ten carts each rebroadcasting everything they hear will not fit a LoRa duty cycle)
  • Enrolment scoping of broadcasts
  • Group addressing over IP — Fred: "our real issue when you scale this up is targeting ip packets to a group whose members may change from ride to ride."

⭐⭐ THE THING THAT DISSOLVES THE HARD PART: a ride ALREADY defines the radio broadcast group and its encryption keys in 3.0. Fred, 08-22: "or the same registration of a user with both radio and ip config groups available." One registration, both channels — not two membership types. So IP group addressing is not a new problem; it is the existing ride model with a second transport. That is why it lands in 3.0 with the server and the paygate, and why 2.7 only needs the stub.

⭐ THE TRANSPORT CONTRACT — settled in principle, not built

Fred: "a messaging design layer separate for ip stack to long range radio. ending in the same result: updates of cart specific gps being handed off to a map layer."

So the contract is narrow: cart identity, position, timestamp. Same object whichever transport carried it. Everything above — the convoy display, lead-cart logic, the tracking rewrite — becomes transport-blind.

  • Transports can be simultaneous. Some carts on radio, some on cell, one convoy. A ride that starts in coverage and rides out of it is the normal case, not an edge one.
  • Duplicate positions are normal — the same cart heard twice is one update. Latest timestamp per cart wins. Far simpler than the same-ride track dedup in 3.0.
  • ⚠ Size the contract to the SMALLEST transport. Cart ID + lat/lon + timestamp fits LoRa. Anything richer is IP-only and must be an explicit second tier, not something that silently fails off-grid.

⚠⚠ THE LICENSING POSITION — why the separation matters beyond architecture

What the record has held since April (GroupTrack_V2.4_Handoff_Apr23.docx): GroupTrack is built on Meshtastic-Android (GPL v3), Leaflet (BSD 2-Clause), Esri tiles. And the protective claim, stated twice: "GPL v3 requires making modified source available on request. Note: convoy/ package files are original work, not modifications to Meshtastic code."

⭐⭐ THE STRONGER ARGUMENT, Fred 08-22: "if the plugin is a combined work it exposes the radio message sharing, not the route navigation planning." That is better than the folder claim because it is about what depends on what, and dependency is demonstrable. The route suggester built today reads SQLite and does graph search — no line of it could exist only because Meshtastic exists.

⚠ TWO THINGS THAT MAKE OR BREAK IT:

  • The boundary has to be real in the build. Narrow interface, messages in and out, and the argument survives inspection. Planning code calling into mesh types for convenience makes it rhetorical.
  • ⛔ IT IS STILL A QUESTION FOR A LAWYER. "How far does a combination reach" is exactly what is contested about GPL and plugins. The architecture makes the strongest available case; it does not settle it. This is the one place on this project where the cost of being wrong is disproportionate to the cost of asking. The April claim has never been checked either.

⚠ Attribution is the easy half and is still NOT DONE — open on the Play checklist since April (items 5.2, 5.6, P5). Fred 08-22: splash screen, or per map source in the selection flow. Neither is hard.

⚠ The iPad path is a fork of Meshtastic-Apple, also GPL v3 — the same question applies twice.

🤔 IS MESH A FEATURE OR A LIABILITY? (Fred's question, 08-22 — left open deliberately)

Against: the BLE issue is on record — budget Android devices cannot hold a connection to T1000-E radios, supervision timeout after 8–10 s, confirmed hardware not software. Everything built in the last month is solo-mode work. And the entire licensing exposure comes from mesh; nothing else in the app carries it.

For: convoy tracking is the differentiator nobody else has, and off-grid it only works because of the radios.

⭐ A framing worth keeping: mesh is the feature for a minority of rides and the liability for all of them. Which argues for exactly what 2.7 does — make it optional, make it separable, let the core product stand without it.

🔵 08b — 2.7 RENDERING TIER (unchanged) — CONVOY TRACKING REWRITE + MapLibre / VECTOR RENDERING

4-WEEK FRAME (FRED) scoped, nothing built

PURPOSE

Rewrite convoy tracking — the originating requirement — on a renderer that can carry it. Fred: "2.7 is a rewrite of convoy tracking functionality — may be the point we need to introduce these discussions."

SCOPE — 2.7 owns all of this

  • 1a Lead track replacement · 1b Lead cart lock ("In Progress (Not Yet Built)") · 1c Snap-2 on the lead cart track · 1d Vector rendering / MapLibre · 1e spatial_artifacts.js
  • 2 Roads / street names — ingest only · 3 Reprocess / refresh driver · 4 Removals and edits (open, not decided)

⚠ Why this section exists: the convoy tracking rewrite was assigned to 2.7 in session more than two weeks ago and never reached AllDocs — 07-29 was spent reconstructing it from GroupTrack_LeadTrackReplacement_Spec.docx, the Lead Cart Lock section and MAP-06 instead of reading it.

⭐⭐⭐ THE SEQUENCING ARGUMENT (strongest point; survives any storage number)

The dominant cost is not MapLibre — it is operating on FROZEN CODE. ConvoyScreen.kt is frozen to protect the Kotlin↔JS↔Leaflet↔self-saving-setView↔shared-MapStateStore tangle. A convoy tracking rewrite must open exactly that tangle anyway. → The choice is not "rewrite convoy now, renderer later"; it is rewrite that layer ONCE with MapLibre as the target, or TWICE.

⭐⭐ THE onX BENCHMARK (Fred 07-30) — the structural case

onX is vector-based, does three presentations, allows the map to be changed, at ~¼ our storage. Our own device data agrees: the 07-26 delete log showed one SAT job walking three near-identical stores at z18 (SAT 10,295 · SAT_LABELS_TRANSPORT 10,292 · SAT_LABELS_PLACES 10,294). Bar 10 post-corridor ≈ 107,000 raster tiles (SAT ~21,700 + transport ~21,700 + places ~21,700 + TOPO 21,000 + TOPO+ 21,000). Strip everything that is not photography → ~21,700, one fifth.

  • Vector is paid once and serves every presentation. One dataset restyled client-side gives satellite-with-labels, topo and road. Raster pays the full price per presentation — five stores today.
  • Switching maps becomes free — a consequence of the data being geometry rather than pictures. Not matchable with raster at any storage budget.
  • Vector stops at ~z14 and the client renders deeper. Since z15–18 is ~98% of raster tile count, vector does not shrink those levels — it removes them.
  • ⭐⭐ DOWNLOAD TIME IS A RELIABILITY METRIC, not a storage complaint (Fred). "8 hours to download an area that will not be ridden." A long job fails partway, needs retry logic we know is imperfect, demands mains power, and gates ride-readiness. The yardstick is time-to-ride-ready and probability of completion. Every open queue defect is amplified by long jobs and materially reduced by short ones.

⛔ CORRECTION TO MY OWN ANALYSIS: I talked the saving down to ≈20% and let that stand as if it settled the question. It answers a narrower one — retiring one label store, on one corridor, in one presentation. The "whole-state road vectors don't shrink with corridor" objection holds for a single corridor in a single presentation and stops being decisive once amortised across three presentations and repeat rides in a region. I was defending a narrow reading of a number instead of testing the case.

⭐ What stays true regardless: satellite imagery is raster, permanently — roughly the ~21,700 floor. The saving is everything that is not photography.

🔴 THE GATE — WebGL on the cheapest field device (NOT YET RUN, half a day)

MapLibre GL JS requires WebGL in the WebView. The fleet includes $60 tablets, and one documented class already cannot hold a BLE connection. onX ships native rendering on modern phones; we would ship GL JS in a WebView on budget hardware. If WebGL will not perform there, this model is unavailable to us at any storage saving — and learning that during the rewrite is the expensive way.

TEST: load vector_label_prototype.html in a plain WebView on Droid 2 and on the cheapest tablet. Check WebGL context creation, pan/zoom at z16–18, frame rate, memory. No frozen code touched. Do this before 2.7 commits to MapLibre.

🔴 TWO MEASUREMENTS OUTSTANDING (seconds each, gpkgs already on D:)

  1. How many gis_osm_roads_free rows carry a non-blank name (Arizona total 1,205,574; trails filter to 134,242 — named roads across all classes could be several hundred thousand, and road labels need LINE geometry for placement, so potentially 300 MB+ on a 108 MB skinny).
  2. How many carry a non-blank ref. If named roads come back ~50k, extract now and never revisit. If ~500k, ref-only is likely the answer — forest/county road numbers (FR 123, CR 45) are worth more than street names for OHV and are renderable in Leaflet today as a midpoint divIcon.

⚠ The "revisit the data" cost is worse than a re-extract: C4 deletes the zip, so adding a layer to the catalog later means users re-download ~600 MB per state.

THE FIVE WORK ITEMS (renderer)

  1. THE RENDERER. Leaflet cannot draw vector tiles → MapLibre GL JS in the same WebView. spatial_artifacts.js rewritten: L.polyline → GeoJSON sources + layer specs; per-feature click handlers → queryRenderedFeatures (MapLibre gives NO DOM per feature). The data path from SpatialDbManager is UNCHANGED.
  2. TILE INTERCEPTION — MapLibre's addProtocol replaces the WebView request intercept; still serving bytes from MBTiles.
  3. ⚠ GLYPHS — easiest requirement to miss. MapLibre renders text from font PBFs fetched by URL. Offline means bundling them in assets. No equivalent exists in the raster world, so nothing in the pipeline handles it. Miss it and labels silently do not render offline.
  4. MAP STATE — MapLibre's camera adds bearing and pitch; MapStateStore + the self-saving setView need rework. Nearest the frozen tangle.
  5. GETTING LABEL TILES ON DEVICE — planetiler --only-layers=place,poi,transportation_name,water_name,mountain_peak over a state bbox = a labels-only tileset. Then a product decision: bundle it, or a "download this region" flow.

⚠ ONLY THEN drop the two overlay layers from SAT's entry in getDownloadSources(). One-line config change, but it MUST come LAST or satellite ships with no labels.

⭐⭐ THE CROSS-PLATFORM MULTIPLIER

The presentation layer is already web (Leaflet/HTML/JS in a WebView) and is the ~90% shared surface for iPad and PC; SwiftUI was rejected for presentation precisely because it gives zero reuse of it. MapLibre GL JS is a web library — identical in an Android WebView, an iPad WKWebView and a PC browser. A renderer swap is a one-time change the layer all three surfaces inherit. ⚠⚠ The corollary is a real risk: doing the tracking rewrite on Leaflet BAKES LEAFLET into the layer iPad and PC are both waiting on — a long-tailed decision made by omission.

✅ Validated in a browser prototype 07-26 (no build, no device, no frozen code)

  • Esri raster imagery + OSM vector labels composite correctly — "the image overlays look perfect."
  • ⭐ OSM vector labels carry MORE data than Esri's downloaded roads/places layers — the opposite of the predicted outcome.
  • ✅ 3D terrain works; Leaflet cannot do it at all. Terrain-RGB encodes metres above sea level in RGB, so the client holds raw elevation → slope-angle shading computed on device, arguably worth more than satellite imagery for an OHV app and a fraction of the cost.
  • ⛔ OSM trail coverage is sparse at low zoom — OSM is a BASEMAP, not a trail source. ⚠ Minor ways only appear from ~z14, so any coverage comparison must be at z15+.

⛔ RULED OUT

Vector satellite imagery (cannot exist) · OSM as a trail authority · tiling user-authored artifacts (tiles are static; tracks are edited constantly) · doing this as a standalone 2.6 task.

Sources: SCOPING_v2.7_vector_rendering_2026-07-26.html · 07-26 Living Master V2.7 KICKOFF · GroupTrack_NewTick_Design_2026-07-01.md (tick/lead/identity rewrite) · GroupTrack_LeadTrackReplacement_Spec.docx · convoy_mapping_ipad_discussion.md (Mar 14) · Fred 07-30 (onX).

🆕 2.7 FEATURE — TRAIL GAP FILL FROM RIDDEN TRACKS (Fred 07-31)

THE PROBLEM, in Fred's words: "trying to draw a route using bits of a track you have already ridden but trails still do not show."

Route building composes from trails. A stretch you have ridden but which the trail collection does not cover exists only inside a track — and a track cannot be composed with, because it is stored as ONE geometry. So when planning, you cannot see all your real choices even though you have the ground truth on the device.

⭐ These are not a new class of artifact. Fred: "they are trails that simply do not appear in that collection. you need a trail to ride." Same table, same rendering — the collection is just missing a piece.

⭐⭐ DISPLAY FIRST, INSERT SECOND — the shape that makes it tractable

Fred: "may be simpler to have a function that shows the map with a colour segment that is missing and can be added."

Show uncovered stretches as an overlay; the rider decides which become trails.

  1. For tracks in view, compute which point runs have no trail within tolerance
  2. Render those runs as a distinct overlay
  3. Tap the track → artifact detail panel → Add Trail Segment
  4. Only then does anything enter the trails table

⭐ This removes three problems at once, which is why it is better than automatic insertion at import time:

  • Tolerance stops being critical. A false positive is visible noise the rider ignores, not a duplicate trail silently written to the DB.
  • Naming resolves itself — the user is looking at it.
  • Nothing accumulates against dedup, and GPS wobble never becomes authoritative geometry by accident.

⭐ And it is useful before the insert step is written at all. Simply SEEING where ridden ground is missing from the collection answers the original frustration; adding it becomes optional.

Design detail settled 07-31

Detection. Sample the track's points; for each, test distance to trail geometry returned by queryTrailsByViewport for a small bbox. Consecutive points with nothing within tolerance form an uncovered run. Discard runs below a minimum length.

⭐ RouteManager.snap(lat, lon, trails, tracks, 30.0) already performs this proximity test and returns null when nothing is near — "null" IS "uncovered", so detection is mostly free.

Tolerance 20–30 m, matching the existing snap radius. ⚠ Too tight and GPS wobble alongside a mapped trail reads as uncovered, producing a shadow trail parallel to a real one — that is the failure mode to design against, and it is the argument for showing rather than auto-inserting.

Naming — Fred's rule: inherit the name ONLY if the trail either side of the gap carries the same name. Both endpoints agreeing is strong evidence it is one trail; disagreeing means it connects two, and inventing a name there would be wrong. Otherwise leave it unnamed.

⚠ Unnamed is correct, not a fallback. Fred: "most of our trails are unnamed now and named would have a major trail connotation." A named trail SIGNALS significance to a rider, and today's ORDER BY sorts named first — so auto-naming gap fills would both inflate that signal and outrank real named trails at low zoom.

Colour. ⚠ Dashed is already taken — tracks are #39FF14 dashed, routes #FF00FF dot-dash, trails carry carto_code colours. A solid line in an unused colour will read more clearly than another dash pattern.

🔴 OPEN QUESTION — which gap, when a track has several

The detail panel opens for the WHOLE track, but uncovered stretches are specific sub-ranges. A track with three gaps makes "Add Trail Segment" ambiguous — and that is exactly the case that motivated this.

⭐ Fred's motivating case: several tracks named Broken Ridge in one area. From a route-planning standpoint that is unusable, because you can only select whole tracks. Segmenting the uncovered stretches makes it a set of distinct short pieces you can assemble — and "which Broken Ridge" stops mattering, because you are choosing a STRETCH, not a name.

Likely resolution: the overlay lines are separate L.polyline objects, so each can carry its own point-range identity and pass that through — rather than routing everything via track_id alone.

⚠ Worth measuring before building

How much would this actually find? Utah now carries 89,536 OSM trails. If ridden tracks are 95% covered, this produces stubs and the effort is not repaid; at 40% it is a substantial feature.

⭐ Measurable PC-side in minutes against the spatial DB — sample a track's points, test distance to trail geometry, report the uncovered fraction. No app code. And measure it on the Broken Ridge area specifically, not statewide — that is the case that motivates it.

⚠ Depends on today's detail-panel work. The flow ends at "tap the track → panel → Add Trail Segment", which only became possible once addPointMode stopped suppressing planner track taps and __detailPanelEnabled was re-enabled. Both landed 07-31.

📚 DOCUMENT SET — eighteen documents, none previously referenced from this checklist

GroupTrack_V3_Complete_Task_List.docx (6,187) · GroupTrack_V3_StubbedProcesses_FINAL_v2.docx (13,148 — the parked-code inventory) · GroupTrack_V3_Architecture_Plan.docx (17,074) · GroupTrack_V3_Walkaway_Activity_v1.docx (16,500) · GroupTrack_V3_Registration_Paywall_Spec_v2.docx (14,260) · GroupTrack_V3_CompleteImplementation_FINAL_v2.docx (8,940) · V30_Walkaway_Stub_Inventory_v1.docx (4,458) · GroupTrack_V3_PhaseB_Tasks_v2.docx (2,353) · GroupTrack_V3_PickupGuide_v1.docx (1,704) · plus V3_ProcessSpec_v1, V3_Online_Offline_Manual_v2, V3_MapEnhancements_v1, V3_TrackSharing_SpatialModel_2026-06-28, V3_CompleteImplementation_v1, V3_StubbedProcesses_v1, V3_Task_List_Apr1, GroupTrack_V30_StubInventory (hollowed — 242 chars), GroupTrack_V3_Implementation_Plan (stub).

NEXT: read Complete Task List + StubbedProcesses + PickupGuide end to end and reconstruct the actual completion state — what is built, what is stubbed, what remains — as this block's content. Fred to state 3.0's PURPOSE in his own words; it is not inferred here.

Server-side function that belongs under a hosting release (NOT a release title)

Same-ride track dedup — pruning duplicate recordings when tracks are served. ⚠ This is a HOSTING problem and only exists once tracks are served. Locally, ten riders each holding their own recording of one ride is CORRECT.

  • ⛔ geom_hash cannot do this job. Perfect for trails (89,536 rows / 89,536 hashes; the 313 UT/AZ border trails collapsing automatically prove it). Useless for tracks from one ride: ten devices drop GPS points at different moments → ten point sets, ten hashes, all legitimately the same ride.
  • ⭐ COLLAPSE FOR PRESENTATION, DO NOT DELETE. Canonical track per hash, hash + date → alias. Nobody loses their own ride and the collapse stays reversible. Server-side, not on the phone. Scope is bounded (same bbox + date window). Goal is "prune as best we can", not perfect matching.
  • Candidate metrics (none chosen): bbox overlap + Hausdorff/Fréchet · rounded/simplified-geometry hash · start/stop coordinates + time window (Fred 07-29, cheaper than either) ⚠ needs tolerance on both axes.
  • ⭐ CROSS-REFERENCE, not scope: 2.7's snap-2 makes recorded tracks converge on shared trail geometry, so part of this becomes hash-matchable outright. That work belongs to 2.7.
  • ⚠ Two riders on the same ground can store different lineIds. For DRAWING it does not matter; it matters only when two routes are COMPARED. Fix at compare time by geometry proximity, never by making snap pick differently.

🆕🆕 3.0 FEATURE — RIDER TRAIL NAMING, AND THE PENDING-CHANGE QUEUE (Fred 2026-08-22). TRAILNAMES-2026-08-22

Fred, 08-22: "unnamed is no name so first name replaces unnamed and becomes the single source of truth. we would move to server and back down users in v3." And: "when riding the user can name the section if it has a name that is not in our database (route markers)."

The ground has names the data does not. Route markers exist on the trail, on posts, and in no dataset we import. The only place that knowledge lives is with the rider who is standing next to the marker — which makes this the one mechanism by which the trail data can get better rather than merely bigger.

The measurement that motivates it

Bar 10 → Toroweap corridor, measured 08-22 against the live spatial DB (216,616 trails):

  • 4,311 trails in the corridor · 2,894 miles
  • 3,334 of them carry the literal string Not Named — 77%. Only 977 (23%) have a real name.
  • The named ones are good — Mt Dellenbaugh, Mt Trumbull, Whitmore Canyon, Temple Trail, Sullivan Draw — but they are the minority.

⚠ Not Named IS A VALUE, NOT A BLANK, and it already caused a false reading: the 08-22 inventory reported "100% named" because the column was populated. Anything testing does this trail have a name with an emptiness check is fooled the same way. Treat the placeholder set as data to be normalised, not as names.

⭐⭐ THE RULE — an absence loses to anything; a real name is never silently overwritten

  1. A placeholder is an ABSENCE. Not Named, Unnamed, blank, None — all mean the same thing. The first REAL name replaces it outright and becomes the trail's name.
  2. A real name is never overwritten by another source. Two genuine names for one geometry is the ALIAS case, which insertTrail already returns a decision for and which the alias machinery already handles.
  3. A rider name outranks a placeholder, never a source name. A rider standing at a marker beats OSM's silence; they do not silently overrule MassGIS or UGRC. Rider-vs-source on the same geometry is a conflict for the server to arbitrate, not for the device to decide.

⭐ This sits on machinery that already exists. Identity is geom_hash alone — the rule that made a UGRC trail and an OSM trail over the same ground ONE artifact with two names. A rider-supplied name is a third name against the same hash. And updateExistingFeature already refuses to touch geometry (NEVER geometry/bbox/geom_hash), which is exactly the guarantee naming needs.

⭐⭐⭐ THE PENDING-CHANGE QUEUE — what Fred asked for, and why it is the load-bearing part

Fred: "db updates must be saved for application for AWS server."

⛔ The naming happens where there is no signal. That is not an edge case, it is the normal case — the rider is at a route marker on the Arizona Strip. So a name cannot be an API call. It is a local write plus a durable record of intent, uploaded whenever the device next has a connection.

Two writes, not one:

  • Apply locally, immediately. The rider sees their name on their map before they have left the marker. No round trip, no spinner, no failure mode in the field.
  • Queue the contribution in a pending table that survives app restarts, reinstalls and months offline.

What the record must carry — enough to be applied by a server that has never seen this device:

FieldWhy
geom_hashTHE identity. Not trail_id — that is a per-device UUID and means nothing to the server or to another rider.
proposed nameWhat the rider read off the marker
previous valuePlaceholder or real name — the server needs to know which case this was, because the rule differs
source of the nameroute marker · local knowledge · signage. A marker photo is a later refinement, not v1
where and whenCoordinate and timestamp of the naming. Two riders naming the same segment from the same marker is strong agreement; from 40 miles apart it is not
contributorThe Google Sign-In identity already used as the cross-platform identifier
upload stateDerived where possible. ⚠ See the status-field warning below

⚠⚠ DO NOT GATE BEHAVIOUR ON A status FIELD IN THIS TABLE. That shape has burned this project twice — isSourceFullyImported() reading status=="processed" from a record written unconditionally marked three FAILED runs as permanently imported (07-27), and the same class produced the 08-21 manifest work. Prefer: a row exists = not yet uploaded; the row is deleted (or moved) on server acknowledgement. Presence is the state, and presence cannot drift.

⭐ The queue is the artifact. Same principle as the import manifest and the OSM ledger: a rider who names twenty sections on a three-day ride has twenty rows waiting when they reach signal. Nothing depends on them being online at the moment of naming.

Server side — arbitration and distribution

Stack is already standing: Google Sign-In · AWS RDS MySQL · PHP REST on EC2, with users, rides, enrollments, invites live through Phase B.

  • A names table keyed on geom_hash, holding every contribution rather than a single winner. The canonical name is DERIVED from the contributions, so it can be recomputed when a new one arrives and an arbitration mistake is reversible.
  • Agreement is the signal. Three riders independently naming the same hash Sullivan Draw is far stronger than one. Cheap first rule: most-agreed name wins; ties break to earliest.
  • Distribution back down is a list of geom_hash → name. That is small — names are bytes, not geometry — and it applies with the same "placeholder loses to a real name" rule the device already uses. ⭐ No geometry ever moves in either direction.
  • A rider's own name is never taken away from them. If the server's canonical name differs, theirs becomes an alias on their device rather than being replaced. Same principle as the same-ride track dedup above: collapse for presentation, do not delete.

⚠ Open questions — not settled

  • Which geometry does the rider actually name? Segments are short — the corridor's median is 0.31 mi — so a named road may be forty rows. Does naming apply to the tapped segment only, or propagate along connected same-name-absent segments? Propagation is a much bigger feature and probably not v1.
  • Moderation. Two riders, two different names, no agreement. Also: profanity, and names that are someone's private joke. A hosting release that publishes rider content back to other riders owns that problem.
  • Does a rider name reach OSM? Contributing upstream would help everyone and raises licensing questions that have deliberately been kept off this project — GroupTrack has never hosted or redistributed OSM data, which is what keeps ODbL share-alike clear of Fred entirely. Do not open this without deciding it explicitly.
  • Numbering. This is filed under 3.0 as Fred stated. ⚠ The unresolved 3.0-vs-4.0 split above applies — if names ride the same distribution path as tiles and rides, check which block owns it before building.

Related, not the same

2.7's TRAIL GAP FILL FROM RIDDEN TRACKS (section 08 above) promotes a recorded track to a trail where no trail exists. That creates geometry; this creates names on geometry that already exists. They meet at one point: a promoted track is unnamed by definition, so it is immediately a candidate for naming — and the rider who rode it is the one person who knows what it is called.

📱 12 — iPAD — POST-V2.6 TARGET (restored 07-30; was absent from the checklist entirely)

The evolution — four iterations, all in the corpus

  1. Native port via external dev — Roadmap V9, Mar 25 (iOS-01..08: fork Meshtastic-iOS, Apple Developer enrollment $99/yr, port the coordination layer, WKWebView + convoy_map.html, offline tiles in Swift, Apple IAP, TestFlight, com.grouptrack.ios).
  2. Staged Options A–D incl. WKWebView hybrid — Mar 14 discussion doc (short term: Meshtastic Apple as a display-only viewer, zero dev cost; medium: WKWebView hybrid, 2–3 weeks; long: full Swift fork).
  3. PWA-via-Bluefy-then-Capacitor — May notes.
  4. Repeatedly flagged "iPad V2.5 vs V3.0, not resolved."

⭐ THE COLLAPSE (07-04) — the whole PWA arc solved a non-problem

Convoy is a fork/extension of core Meshtastic's messaging. The native-vs-PWA-vs-WKWebView arc was all answering "how do we get BLE on iOS?" — a question that dissolves once convoy is understood as a fork of the mesh app. So iPad = a fork of Meshtastic-Apple (Swift/SwiftUI + Core Data + BLE + protobuf; already App-Store-approved; universal incl. iPad; ships an offline map + radio config). Mac + Xcode is the prerequisite. ⚠ FLAG: Meshtastic-Apple ships native TAK/CoT — reconcile against the roadmap's "TAK as design pattern only."

🔴 STILL OPEN — the A/B decision

(A) BRIDGE — convoy is its own native iOS app consuming mesh messages from the separate Meshtastic app over a local port. Less code; adds a runtime dependency + iOS-lifecycle fragility. (B) FORK — import Meshtastic's BLE/mesh and extend, as on Android. More work; no runtime dependency; same architecture as Android. Trade: effort favours A; field reliability + Android-sameness favour B. For an off-grid safety app, "bridge dies if iOS backgrounds the neighbour app" is a real mark against A — leans B, not decided.

Settled

  • Three surfaces split on connectivity: PC = planning-only web app (no mesh, no radio, online) · Android + iPad = off-grid native convoy execution. Convoy cannot be web.
  • SwiftUI REJECTED for presentation — does not port to a browser, zero reuse of the Android web layer. Swift's only job on iPad is the native shell (mesh source + WebView host + JS↔native bridge).
  • Config write is offloaded: the planning side creates a Meshtastic restore FILE; Meshtastic's own restore applies it. GroupTrack never does BLE for config.
  • KMP is the natural target — logic is already Kotlin; migration is incremental, expect/actual is literally "configured API resolves per platform." Does not let you fork Meshtastic-Apple (that is Swift); produces a shared logic module a Swift shell consumes.
  • Target end-state: a messaging/logic service layer behind an interface + logic-free presentation panels. Serving three platforms means logic written ONCE, not three copies.

🔴 NEXT ACTION (unchanged since 07-04, still not done)

Enumerate the bridge/IO surface from the current Android code — every JavascriptInterface method + every evaluateJavascript/postMessage crossing. That inventory is the true iPad build cost AND which functions can/cannot go to PC. It is the blast-radius question the xref docs answer.

Sources: GroupTrack_CrossPlatform_iPad_Strategy_2026-07-04.md · convoy_mapping_ipad_discussion.md (Mar 14) · Roadmap V9 §iPad Strategy + iOS-01..08.
