📜 13 — ORIGINAL (07-30): TILE SOURCE HYB IS DEAD (Fred 07-30) — live tester-facing defect

NEW predicted risk, materialized

Google Hybrid no longer functions — the URL returns no data. Roadmap V9 records MAP-01 | SAT=Esri, HYB=Google, TOPO=Esri Topo, RD=Carto Voyager | All 4 working | COMPLETE (March). One of four selectable sources now returns nothing.

  • A dead source can still be chosen and queued — jobs segment and enqueue normally, then every tile fails. Burns queue time; may leave an empty/partial .mbtiles that later looks like a corrupt download.
  • ⚠ Rule it in or out as a feeder for the open queue defects — markComplete refreshMode collision, refresh-never-completes, ETA starvation. A source where every fetch fails is exactly the input that would make completion accounting behave strangely.
  • The manual and any source-picker capture showing four working sources are now wrong.
ACTION: grep getDownloadSources() and the tile_sources table for HYB. Decide disable behind a marked constant (the pattern used for map-refresh) vs remove. Do not delete the entry silently — it is the record of why.

⛔ REJECTED — AND WHY (this section is new and belongs on every release block)

Google Hybrid / Google tiles: rejected on download reliability, field-observed — free Google download resources proved slow and unreliable at bulk scale — and now on dead endpoint. NOT rejected on quality or storage, and NOT reversible by a storage argument.

⚠ THE ARCHIVE CURRENTLY ARGUES THE OPPOSITE. GroupTrack_V25_MapTileSourceReference_v1.md (May 9) says Google tiles "work reliably and have for years" and recommends them as "the best free option for off-road", calling Google Hybrid "best overall hybrid." Fred's field evidence overturned that and the overturning was never written down. A future session scouring the corpus finds "one store instead of three, saves 3×" and re-proposes exactly what already failed. This entry exists to close that loop.

⭐⭐ THE METHOD LESSON (Fred 07-30) — why this happened

That May 9 document is 19,514 chars and eight sections: every URL template, {y}/{x} ordering gotchas, rate limits, API-key tiers, a tile_sources table design, Leaflet integration code. It is a thorough catalogue with no risk analysis, no strengths/weaknesses column, and no storage arithmetic — and the consequence sat in its own text: Google Hybrid = satellite + labels in ONE stream (1×); Esri World Imagery = no labels, therefore two extra raster label stores (3×). SAT_LABELS_TRANSPORT / SAT_LABELS_PLACES appear in the handoffs from May 10 — the very next day — and the multiplication was never performed. It surfaced on 07-26 from a delete log, and Fred spotted it: "it is 65,000 tiles because of the three layers."

The Esri choice was right on the axis that governs (bulk download reliability beats storage efficiency for an offline-first app). A risk table would not have changed the source — it would have moved the vector conversation to May, before Leaflet was wired in and before the tangle froze.

⚠ Also unweighed at the time and still live: Esri World Imagery is flagged in that same doc as legacy "mature support" — Esri pushing migration, max zoom 19 vs Google's 20. Both are risk-column entries about the dependency the whole map rests on.

⭐ STANDING STRUCTURE, all release blocks: options side by side · strengths · weaknesses · what it costs later · the risk that would reverse the choice · the cheap test that would settle it. Applied now: the WebGL-on-cheap-device test before MapLibre is committed to.

🔴🔴 14 — CARRIED FROM 07-27: MAP REFRESH ON SOURCE CHANGE (do first, stop-the-bleeding)

DO FIRST not a root-cause fix

The REPLACE/refresh function on the map-source select screen spawned 3287 downloads, lat/long spanning the continental US; downloads then stopped entirely.

  • ⚠ Cancel All does NOT persist. Cancel appeared to clear the queue; all 3287 jobs resurfaced on restart — the clear happens in memory and is never written to disk, so loadQueue re-reads the stale file every launch. This is the boot-loop mechanism, demonstrated — just without a crash.
  • ⛔ No recovery path on a release build. adb run-as = denied; X-plore cannot reach Android/data/<pkg>/ — Android 13+ removed third-party file-manager access entirely, MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE excludes it, the document-tree picker refuses it. Only root, adb-on-debuggable, or the app itself can read there. The only escape is Clear-App-Data.
  • Decision (Fred 07-23): turn off the JSON write / auto-refresh on source change. Refresh becomes manually requested as an Area download, riding the hardened submitDownload → enqueueArea → segmentCells path. DISABLE, don't delete — gate behind a marked constant; the bug is still undiagnosed and the path will be wanted.
  • ⚠ This does NOT fix Cancel All — separate defect, still open.
  • ⛔ DO NOT run replace-map-source again until this ships — recovery costs an hour and possibly all app-internal data.
  • Root cause still open: leading hypothesis is that the bbox handed to enqueueRefresh is not the viewport — a zero-initialised value, or swapped bounds arguments. Note 3287 ≈ ceil(enormous/50K), so segmentation worked correctly on garbage input → the bug is upstream of segmentCells. Compare ConvoyMapSourceScreen.kt:255-290 against ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt:218-250 for argument order; also read ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt:440-490 (:473 declares a quarantine file, and it is likely the same function where cancel's persistence fails).

🔴 15 — CARRIED FROM 07-27: RECOVERY SYSTEM FOR APP-PROTECTED DATA (the "Repair Area")

OPEN

⭐⭐ KEY CONSEQUENCE: filesDir holds radio configs, radio backups, users, rides and events — not just tiles. With no recovery path on a release build (see §14), a wedged file there is unreachable and Clear-App-Data destroys real user data.

  • The repair area is a HOLDING area — copy-back is GROUPED, not per-file.
  • Archive naming + retention defined; ⭐ the retention sweep runs ON APP OPEN, EVERY OPEN — not on repair-screen entry (a phone that never opens the screen must still be swept).
  • ⭐⭐ The manifest JSON is a work order + audit record.
  • ⭐⭐ Apply on RESTART, not on exit.
  • Applied-repair HISTORY retained.

Full design detail is in the 07-27 baseline text and must be carried forward verbatim into this block on the next pass — it was written once and has not been re-derived since.

🔴🔴 16 — CARRIED FROM 07-27: THREE DEVICE DEFECTS (07-24 PM) + completion-state family

gate the Play upload, not the build

  • ⚠ ETA poisoning (likely feeder of defect 1): completedAt gets stamped early, so throughput maths is computed against work that has not happened.
  • ⭐ Same family as the refresh bug — completion state and actual work drifting apart. Two symptoms, one root shape.
  • ⛔ The benign explanation is dead. First read was "hasTile skipped tiles already on device"; that no longer accounts for it.
  • ⚠ If SAT is broken, testers get corridor downloads with no satellite imagery — the primary layer. Highest-severity of the three.

🔴🔴 markComplete refreshMode COLLISION (found 07-26 — ONE LINE, high value)

ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt:641. ⚠ enqueueArea AND enqueueCorridor both set refreshMode = replaceExisting, so any area/corridor job collides. ⭐ Strong candidate for BOTH the refresh-never-completes report AND a large share of the ETA starvation. Displaced twice already — do it.

🔴 REFRESH DOES NOT LOG AS A COMPLETED JOB (new 07-24)

⚠ Bigger than a cosmetic status. ⚠⚠ Does it hold a concurrency slot? If the entry never leaves running/active, it consumes a slot forever. ⭐ Check ConvoyCorridorWorker in the SAME pass — the corridor worker deliberately duplicates completion calls per worker, so this class of miss can exist independently there. Confirmed still live: the corridor COMPLETED_TASK doc references it as an open Living Master entry bearing on the corridor ETA.

🔴 RESTORE PER-SOURCE Z-LEVEL DOWNLOAD LIMITS

⛔ ConvoyTileCalculator.maxZoomForSource(sourceName) (:72) is a STUB returning ConvoyConfig.DOWNLOAD_ZOOM and ignoring its argument — everything pulls to z18 including sources capped at 15. ⚠ corridorTiles inherits the same gap. ⚠ Cost is severe: tile counts quadruple per zoom. ⭐ It also affects MAP DISPLAY, not just downloads (Fred: "we should not zoom to levels the source cannot serve"). ⚠ SEQUENCING: capping z changes the corridor baseline numbers — the bar-10 measurement and every ≈80%/≈20% figure above are computed at the current cap.

Dead code to delete together

downloadMapsForTrackHash (SpatialDbManager:1945, zero callers) · gridCells (ConvoyTileDownloader:297, zero callers) · the unreachable if (cells.isEmpty()) branch in enqueueArea (~:168-186). ⚠ Also getTrackBbox (SpatialDbManager:1975) is a SECOND hardcoded FROM tracks (:1981).

It applied and built, but every value printed as literal text (sizeTiles=$sizeTiles) because I wrote ${'$'} escapes into the Kotlin. Fix: emit plain $slotName. The pre-existing SEGMENT logs are what actually carried the data. Backup at ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt.bak_20260723_134052.

🔵 17 — G5 ROUTE CORRIDORS (07-24 design, DESIGNED IN FULL 08-09 — NOT WRITTEN)

ready to write

⛔⛔ FIRST — A CONTRADICTION IN OUR OWN RECORD. RESOLVE BEFORE WRITING CODE.

This section (07-27) says the routes table ALREADY CARRIES geom_hash. The 08-06 notes say the opposite — that routes carry none and it must be added as a column plus a call at insert plus a backfill. Both cannot be true.

⚠ The whole 08-09 design below was reasoned out against the 08-06 version without this section being read first — the exact failure Fred named today: "another long lengthy discussion without awareness of prior discovery." If 07-27 is right, a chunk of the work below does not exist.

grep -n "geom_hash" app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/SpatialDbManager.kt | grep -i route
sqlite3 grouptrack_spatial.db ".schema routes"

THE GOAL (Fred 08-06, verbatim intent)

⭐⭐⭐ "I do not care about route. I am trying to create a method where a PRE-RIDE ROUTE CAN BE DISTRIBUTED AND IMPORTED WITH MAPS, allowing a rider to download accurate maps with efficient space utilisation BEFORE TRAILS ARE CAPTURED."

⭐ CORRIDOR, NOT AREA. Fred 08-09: "we know 90% of area are air." Confirmed by the 08-08 measurement — 30 tracks swept as areas cost 2M+ tiles on ONE source; 89 as corridors cost 1.5M across THREE. A route bbox is mostly air, so route downloads run as corridors from the WKT.

⚠ Half a mile either side is the padding (~1 mile band). Fred 08-09: "we should be fine with maps downloaded and trail being on it." ⚠ Holds for a SNAPPED route, whose geometry IS trail geometry via snap-2. A DRAWN route cuts across country — its corridor covers ground the rider will not travel and misses the trail where it bends. Open: does a drawn route download at all, or must it be snapped first?

⭐ WHAT FRED TESTED 08-09 — TWO THINGS DO NOT WORK TODAY

  • SAVE MAPS does not render for routes. ArtifactDetailPanel:111 gates on artifactType == "Tracks" && onDownloadCorridor != null. ⭐ The panel is otherwise ALREADY generic — line 27 documents "Routes" as valid, and rename/delete work for them (only Trails is excluded). SAVE MAPS is the one place someone narrowed it.
  • Import did not process maps for routes. Routes may have imported as artifacts — unconfirmed which half failed. ConvoyMapViewerScreen:230 calls ConvoyTrackOps.importGpxAllArtifacts.

⭐⭐ THE ARTIFACT-SPECIFIC SURFACE IS THREE LINES. EVERYTHING ELSE IS ALREADY GENERIC.

ComponentArtifact-specific?
enqueueCorridor:667 getTrackPoints(context, geomHash) YES — geometry resolve
enqueueCorridor:~702 allTrackGeomHashes() label lookup YES — a route would show a hex fragment as its queue label
ConvoyCorridorWorker:49 re-resolves from inputData YES — the job may run in a fresh process
corridorTiles(segments)✅ takes points, no opinion
slot/layer multiplier · bbox loop · QueueEntry✅ all derive from segments
worker dispatch · download · progress · tile write · ORPHAN block · markFailed✅ untouched

⭐ So there is NO second worker, NO parallel enqueue, NO duplicated job-building. Fred 08-09: "two new callable functions, return geometry for tracks and return geometry for artifacts, and all the work comes out of here and does not complicate this job."

⭐⭐ THE BUILD — TWO PASSES (Fred 08-09)

"Separate the existing working function into a new route/track solution but only implement the existing track in the new method. We will test and certify and then introduce the route bits."

⭐ WHY: this is the 08-08 lesson applied. Every component of the migration was already working; the REASSEMBLY introduced the defects and cost a device rebuild. Proving the refactor behaviour-neutral first means any failure in pass 2 is unambiguously the route code.

PASS 1 — structure only, ZERO behaviour change:

resolveGeometry(context, type, key)   -> wraps getTrackPoints; "Tracks" only
resolveLabel(context, type, key)      -> wraps allTrackGeomHashes; "Tracks" only
enqueueCorridor(..., artifactType)    -> explicit at EVERY call site
QueueEntry.artifactType               -> new field, persisted
inputData "artifact_type"             -> worker reads it, calls resolveGeometry

CERTIFY PASS 1: corridor downloads behave EXACTLY as before — same tile counts, same queue rows, same labels, same completions. Nothing new in the UI, so any difference is a regression.

PASS 2 — routes: the route branch in both resolvers · the routes SAVE MAPS button · import offers downloads at the END of the batch (Fred: "current import process creates db tables and submits imports at the end. I prefer that approach.") · route-create on final save.

⭐ Fred's entry-point order: prove it from Work-With-Artifacts FIRST — a stable bench where any number of SAVE MAPS requests can be submitted — then attach to import and route-create by artifact key.

⚠ DECISIONS AND TRAPS — carry these into the build

  • ⛔ NO DEFAULT ON artifactType. ~7 call sites edited explicitly. A default silently sends a route to the tracks table — CODE RULE 1.
  • ⚠ replaceExisting: Boolean = false is an existing default at a function we are touching — CODE RULE 2 says evaluate it at point of contact.
  • ⛔ THE SILENT SKIP AT :670. No geometry returns 0 with a log warning, and enqueueCorridorBatch counts it as SKIPPED, not failed. A route that cannot resolve would report as skipped and download nothing — worse than an error, because it looks like it worked. ⭐ Make it a real failure during PASS 1, before routes can hit it.
  • ⚠ QueueEntry.geomHash becomes a KEY whose meaning depends on type (routes would put a route id there). Name it honestly in pass 1 rather than leaving a field called geomHash holding a route id in pass 2.
  • ⚠ SERIALISATION MUST BE BACKWARD-COMPATIBLE — startedAt/tilesPerSec were added 08-08 with optLong/optDouble so old queue files still load. The new field needs the same, or Droid 2's persisted queue breaks on read.
  • ⚠ The worker error text at :55 ("Corridor job missing track or source") becomes wrong for routes.
  • ⭐ ADD THE ENTRY ID TO THE COMPLETION LOG LINE. The start line carries id= and hash=; Corridor complete carries NEITHER, so per-job durations cannot be matched from logcat — proven on 08-09 when the analysis had to fall back to completion sums. One field makes every future run exactly measurable.
  • ⚠ A BATCH SUBMIT IS WHERE 08-08 BIT. The migration queued 172 jobs and became unexitable. Route import producing several corridors must show the count BEFORE submitting and leave a way out after.
  • ⚠ Dedup: a route drawn along an existing track produces an IDENTICAL geom hash. resolveByGeom(type, name, geomHash) is type-aware so probably fine — verify, or planning a route over known trail could fail to save.
  • ⚠ insertRoute returns a FABRICATED id on duplicates — newId(), then INSERT OR IGNORE, then returns the fabricated id rather than the surviving row's. Fix while in there (carried from 07-27).
  • ⭐ A mixed route is the best test artifact (Fred 08-09): snap-2 segments and straight-line draws within ONE route exercise the whole range at once. Snapped portions should hug the trail; drawn portions produce a straight band across country. If they look identical, something is re-deriving geometry instead of using what was stored.

⭐ The blocker dissolved on inspection: the routes table already carries geom_hash. ⚠ Defects to fix while in there: insertRoute returns a fabricated id on duplicates — newId(), then INSERT OR IGNORE, then returns the fabricated id rather than the surviving row's.

🟠 19 — FIX 6 — PLANNER PADDING DRIFT (Fred priority)

FRED PRIORITY

"Annoying continually entering a shrinking panel." Trail shrinks / map grows on every reopen: the FIT function is reused for persistence, so restore re-pads an already-padded saved bbox → cumulative zoom-out.

Chosen fix: explicit padding argument — fitToBounds(bbox, padding:Int); restore passes 0, content-fit passes ~60. If the function is SHARED with convoy, default the argument to the content-fit value so convoy's call site is untouched (respects the freeze).

Scope first: grep -rn "fitBounds\|fitToBounds" --include='*.kt' app/src/main/java/ and grep -n "fitBounds" app/src/main/assets/*.html — the planner fit may go through the HTML.

THE PERSIST CHAIN (confirmed 07-29, three hops): the fit call → (…, 400) (MVS:284-285) → savePlanningState() (MVS:259) writes planning_panel.json. ⚠ savePlanningState() reads lastViewport* and ONLY onViewportChanged sets them, so a redraw racing a map move can persist a stale viewport.

⛔ CORRECTION: "convoy has cumulative zoom-out" is misleading as written — the convoy map is frozen and its call site is untouched; the drift is the planner's restore path.

THE PLANNER HAS TWO FIT IDIOMS AND THEY DISAGREE — site idiom vs content idiom. Reconcile before patching.

🔴 20 — THREE PLANNER ISSUES surfaced 07-29 by OSM coverage (NOT chased)

pre-existing fragilities, newly visible

⭐ All three appeared the moment coverage nearly doubled (49,125 UGRC → 89,527 OSM). Nothing in the 07-29 patches touched snap, route drawing or waypoint placement. Fred: "tracks and trails are virtually on top of each other."

  1. Snap should NOT resolve against trails when drawing on a track. ⭐ ELEVATED — now a 2.6e release task with its own section (§04). ⛔ Read RouteManager.snap's ranking before proposing anything — a preference order may already be expressible in how it is structured.
  2. Dropping a point selects the WHOLE TRACK — with tracks, not trails. Candidate cause: onProximityTap in MVS uses val radius = 0.002 (~200 m) and queries ALL artifact types. ⚠ Candidate, NOT a diagnosis.
  3. Waypoints cannot be dropped outside route mode — likely the same tap consumed by proximity selection. The most likely genuine bug of the three.
💡 THE DIAGNOSTIC NOT YET RUN: try all three where OSM added little — ground UGRC already covered. Normal there + chaotic in dense areas = tolerance/radius. Chaotic everywhere = something else changed. Five minutes, and it decides whether the fix is a number or a redesign.
